Summary

“I know you like to think the best of people, but you don't know them like I do,” Kal broke in, “I know you remember Lex and I used to be best friends, just like you and Lena used to be. For years Lex seemed to be nothing but an upstanding businessman and humanitarian, until one day he wasn't. It wasn't until it was too late that I realized he might never have been my friend at all. I know you have a hard time accepting the same might be true of Lena, but I don't want you making the same mistake I made.”

Kara took special care in returning her belongings to her bag, careful not to break anything, before taking a deep breath and responding, “I know how you feel about them, Kal, but Lena doesn't read that way to me. Not when I met her, not for the years we were friends, and not now.”

Or

Season 2 if Kara and Lena had met years previously and were friends for years before everything went down between Superman and Lex. Kal and Alex are sure that Lena will eventually follow the same path as Lex, but Kara is convinced that she knows better.

Notes

So, as I mentioned in the tags, there is a central trope that this fic is written around, but I decided not to tag it upfront. I will tag it once the story has been completed, so people who search for that tag can find it, but at least for the people who read the story while it hasn't been fully posted, they will get to experience the build-up and reveal (it isn't very far into the story at all really). So, if you are reading this before the tag is there, and you want to play, feel free to try to guess the trope as you read. If you are here after all chapters are up, just keep this in mind while you read since you already know the trope.

I am very glad to be sharing this fic with people finally (This is my first fic, and I've been working on it for over three years). However, keep in mind as you read that I wrote this for myself, and that takes precedence over anything from canon. Think of this more as my versions of the canon characters and events. I have incorporated many many headcanons that I've made over the years, including an entire rework of how Kryponian powers and Kryptonite work (nothing about this should be super hard to follow, it's not like I changed what powers or anything, just that some things work slightly differently now). I love all my headcanons and I will gladly gush about them to anyone who is curious.

For those who are curious or concerned, I have already finished the fic, editing and all, and it will be posted weekly (I might leave a cliffhanger for two weeks if I'm feeling mean). It is thirteen chapters and 105k words

At Long Last

by AdmDeltaFleet

https://archiveofourown.org/works/56580343

    Chapter 1

    Chapter Notes

    Kara bounced nervously from foot to foot as she looked at Kal out of the corner of her eye while they rode the elevator down to the lobby. They had just concluded their interview, and he had yet to voice his opinion on the matter. She had been glad to have his help slowly easing the Venture to the ground, but as the investigation had turned towards Luthor Corp, she found herself wishing that he had returned to Metropolis after the rescue. Against all reason, however, she was holding out hope that he might finally be getting over his prejudice-

    “I don't trust her.”

    -or not.

    “Just because her brother was a madman, it doesn't mean she is too. Why would she give us that drive if she had something to hide?” Kara shot back.

    “I've learned through hard experience that you can't believe anything a Luthor says, or does in this case.”

    “But-” Kara started retorting.

    “I know you like to think the best of people, but you don't know them like I do,” Kal broke in, “I know you remember Lex and I used to be best friends, just like you and Lena used to be. For years Lex seemed to be nothing but an upstanding businessman and humanitarian, until one day he wasn't. It wasn't until it was too late that I realized he might never have been my friend at all. I know you have a hard time accepting the same might be true of Lena, but I don't want you making the same mistake I made.”

    Kara took special care in returning her belongings to her bag, careful not to break anything, before taking a deep breath and responding, “I know how you feel about them, Kal, but Lena doesn't read that way to me. Not when I met her, not for the years we were friends, and not now.”

    Kal looked at her for a brief moment as Kara exited the lobby before him, sighing deeply as he followed. Blowing out a frustrated sigh of her own, Kara was glad to drop the topic, knowing nothing more would come of it now than had the previous times they'd discussed it following Lex's descent into villainy.

    It wasn't that Kara blamed Kal for feeling this way. She had seen the battles that embroiled them for years, as Kal did his best to stop Lex and Lex did his best to kill Kal. A brief glance at his face showed a dark expression as he too relived his past with Lex.

    “Look Kara,” Kal said, grabbing her arm gently and pulling her out of the flow of pedestrians on the sidewalk, “I know how you feel about this, and you know how I feel, but I turned a blind eye to Lex when people were warning me about him, and look what happened. I believed the same of Lex that you do of Lena, that he could never turn on me, and blinded as I was, I let things get out of hand, and in the end, it nearly got Lois killed. If there is even a slight chance that Lena might follow the same path, we must remain vigilant and act before it's too late.”

    From the fervor and conviction with which he spoke, Kara knew for him it wasn't a 'slight chance' but a certainty that Lena would be the same as Lex. In his mind, she was already guilty. He, like many others, judged Lena solely by her name, without ever bothering to listen to anything she had to say for herself, driven on in his belief by years of conflict between him and Lex and the near-death of his wife.

    Kara knew Kal had only phrased it that way in an attempt to placate her ire, but Kara leveled him with a look that told him she wasn't fooled, “Whatever happened to innocent until proven guilty, Kal? We are not judge and jury, acting as we see fit without proof. Lena gave us that drive to help us, and need I remind you that Lex kidnapped Lena because she was unwilling to join him?”

    “The information on that drive could easily be fake, or designed to mislead us, and for all we know she went willingly, or was even helping him the whole time,” Kal replied heatedly, brushing aside her arguments.

    Opening her mouth to let out a blistering retort, Kara heard something that made her head snap up, cutting her off.

    "Kal!” She called sharply, redirecting his attention from their argument to the drones closing on Lena's helicopter as she whipped off her glasses.

    Kara took off before he could respond, hoping Kal's figure had masked her transformation and knowing that despite his hatred for the Luthors, he would do the right thing and assist her in saving Lena.

    Kara rocketed upward, quickly unleashing a blast of red heat vision that destroyed the farther of the two drones before flying straight through the second, reducing it to dust as she turned to scan the skyline of National City for any threats.

    “Supergirl, what's happening?” Superman swooped in, looking slightly confused at Kara getting the jump on him in both noticing and reacting to the threat.

    “I took care of the two drones here, but there are more spaced throughout the city, likely to get us to divert our attention. I'll stay here and make sure Miss Luthor is safe, you take care of the rest of the drones. I'll join you as soon as I am able.”

    If Superman was perturbed about being ordered around, he didn't show it as he quickly turned to go save the day. As he departed, Kara turned to the helicopter, which had hovered in place since being confronted by the drones, and signaled the pilot to land.

    As soon as the helicopter touched down, Kara was at the door, the metal crunching slightly in her hand as she quickly pulled it open. She was met with a shaken-looking Lena, who she gently helped out of the helicopter as she let out a sigh of relief.

    “I've got you,” She murmured as she stabilized Lena, who had tried and failed to take a step away from the helicopter.

    She led Lena a few steps away, waiting until the other woman regained her footing before quickly checking the pilot was uninjured. As she returned to Lena, she scanned the building for any threats that may have been lingering within.

    Lena looked up at her as she approached, still looking shocked, but whether that was from the assassination attempt or the fact that not one, but two Kryptonians had come to save her, Kara wasn't entirely sure.

    “Come on, let's get you back to your office,” Kara said gently, unclenching her fist so she could lay her hand softly on Lena's back as she guided her toward the stairwell.

    Lena was silent as they headed back into the building, saying nothing as they entered, then descended the stairwell, still trying to process the unexpected turn her day had taken.

    When they were alone and safe in Lena's office, she finally turned to Kara and shakily asked, “What the hell was that?”

    “Someone is trying to kill you,” Kara replied firmly, a troubled look on her face.

    Before either woman could say anything more, the doors to Lena's office swung open. Kara immediately slid in front of Lena, putting herself between her and the newcomers until she realized it was only one of the security teams employed by Luthor Corp. Relaxing slightly, she turned back to meet Lena's still frightened gaze.

    “Promise me you'll stay safe,” Kara said, looking back at Lena's security detail as if to appraise their effectiveness, before her eyes slid once more to Lena's, hardening as she spoke, “Someone is still trying to kill you, but there are more drones out there targeting civilians. I need to go help Superman.”

    “It's okay,” Lena responded to the unvocalized apology, “I promise. Now go save the day. I'll see you next time, Supergirl.”

    With one last look back at the somewhat mischievous look attempting to cover Lena's disquiet, Kara flew off to track down the rest of the drones.

    “How did you notice the drones so quickly?” Kal asked as the pair landed on the DEO balcony, either still confused at having Kara get the drop on him, or just genuinely curious.

    “Well, Eliza did always say I was a better listener than you,” Kara replied, not really answering his question.

    “Seriously,” Kal pressed gently.

    Kara turned to him, keeping her face impassive, “This is my city. I know how it sounds. Those drones didn't sound like anything that I usually hear. They stuck out.”

    Kal seemed to accept this answer, turning to walk further into the DEO, but Kara frowned slightly as she followed him.

    “Hey, what happened?” Alex called to the pair as she looked up and put down the report she was reading.

    “Someone used weaponized drones to try and shoot Lena Luthor's helicopter out of the sky, while more drones targeted civilians throughout the city to distract us,” Kal answered for them, as Kara stood nearby, her troubled expression deepening as she got lost in her thoughts.

    “Did you guys handle it?” Alex asked, her attention now solely focused on the pair of Kryptonians.

    “Yes,” Kal responded, “I took care of most of the drones while Kara saw Miss Luthor to safety, then we cleaned up the remaining drones together.”

    “Good, I'll send out teams to collect the remains of the drones, so we can determine who sent them,” Alex said, gesturing at some nearby agents to ready themselves.

    As Alex finished directing the other agents, she looked up and noticed that her sister was standing, unaware, her brow crinkled, as the DEO bustled around her, “Kara, you okay? You're being awfully quiet about this, and your crinkle is back.”

    Pulled from her troubles, Kara looked up to find both Kal and Alex looking at her expectantly. Rubbing her hand over her wrist nervously, she met Alex's gaze. She could tell from the look in her eyes and the tone of her voice that her silence and body language had her sister worried. Kal was looking more closely at her too, slowly picking up on the hints of distress that Alex had read within moments of looking at her sister.

    Kara reached up and ran her hands over her face, smoothing out the all-revealing and traitorous crinkle as she did so.

    “Yeah, I- I just. I know it doesn't bother either of you, but she is- she was my friend,” Kara hedged, ”I know it's been almost four years, but seeing her nearly die still upset me.”

    Alex nodded, seeming to feel Kara's upset was justified, and turned to finish preparations for the DEO's investigation into the matter. Kal also nodded at her, his face shadowing once more with his past with the Luthors as he looked towards a screen showing footage of the attacks.

    With no one's attention on her, Kara left them to it, leaving the way she came in to go check in with Lena.

    Kara flew into the DEO earlier than normal the next morning to find everyone else already there.

    “Hey, what's up?” She called over.

    “We found him,” Alex responded, eyes breaking away from Brainy's monitor to look over her shoulder at her sister, “His name is John Corben.”

    “We managed to isolate several partial prints from the drones you destroyed, which I then cross-referenced against known assassins and their MOs to identify the culprit," Brainy stood and led them over to the table as he spoke, indicating the remains of the drones Kara had destroyed yesterday, “I would've identified him sooner if there had been more of the drones left intact.”

    “Thanks Brainy, I'll keep that in mind next time killer drones are targeting people,” Kara said sarcastically.

    “Thank you, Supergirl, but I assure you need not make such considerations. I am, after all, a twelfth-level intellect. I calculate that with as much as 89.9243 percent of the remaining evidence destroyed I would still have been able to identify the assailant.”

    Sharing a look of exasperation with her sister behind Brainy's back, Alex spoke up, “We still need to find him before he can make another attempt on Miss Luthor's life.”

    “I have already tasked-”

    “There is no need to find him,” Kara interjected, “I already know where he is going to be later today.”

    “What-Where-How?”

    The look of confusion on Brainy's face that Kara had beaten him to this information was almost enough to make her laugh, but the severity of the situation kept her from doing so.

    “I talked to Lena. I flew back to check on her last night after I left the DEO. She is holding a press conference this afternoon to officially announce the rebranding of her company to L-Corp.”

    “Well why didn't you try and talk her out of it? There is an assassin on her tail, and he has proven that he has no qualms hurting civilians to accomplish his goals,” Alex said with a concerned look on her face, although Kara felt it had much more to do with Kara going to talk to a Luthor alone without anyone knowing where she was, or even possibly concern for the civilians at the press conference, than anything to do with Lena's safety.

    Unclenching her jaw, Kara responded, “She is too stubborn to be convinced, and I am not stupid enough to try. This is our best chance to catch Corben. Between you, me, Kal, J'onn, and the rest of the DEO, we should be able to keep everyone perfectly safe and apprehend Corben at the same time.”

    Alex wasn't completely convinced, but at the determined look on Kara's face, she eventually agreed.

    As she nodded and turned away, Kara couldn't help but add, “And don't look so shocked that Lena didn't greet me with a Kryptonite bazooka when I talked to her last night.”

    Alex, turning back and looking slightly chastised, didn't bother to apologize, “I just worry about you.”

    “I know,” Kara said, watching her sister go back to work before whispering, “but you don't need to worry about this.”

    Kara stood next to Kal, waiting with no small amount of trepidation for the presentation to start as she re-reviewed the security preparations for the hundredth time in her head. She knew that Corben had to be somewhere nearby, waiting to make his move, but so far he had managed to evade them. She glanced at Kal, who was in turn looking off towards the screen behind the podium, where any minute Lena would ascend to give her speech.

    Kara picked out the plainclothes DEO agents spaced throughout the crowd of reporters that was waiting in anticipation for the announcement. Having double, then triple-checked their locations again, she looked at J'onn, then Alex, who occupied opposite sides of the crowd, waiting to either engage Corben or lead the crowd to safety, depending on how Corben had planned his attack.

    Kara knew Brainy was nearby in a DEO command van watching the venue and all angles approaching it, looking for anything out of the ordinary and ready to direct those in the field should the need arise. While Brainy was himself very capable in the field, Kara felt reassured knowing he was on overwatch today, knowing that no detail or inconsistency would escape his notice.

    “Brainy, are you seeing anything?” Kara asked for the dozenth time.

    “Negative Supergirl, facial recognition has yet to register Corben's presence, and the satellite is not picking up anything unusual. Either he isn't here, or he has so far managed to elude our detection methods. I will keep you informed.”

    “Understood.”

    Kara had no doubt that he was nearby, though. After failing to execute a remote strike, she knew he would make sure the job was done right this time. Personally. Whatever his scheme was, Kara was unable and unwilling to wait and react to it. She was determined to preempt the danger before it arrived. To sate her restless urge to do something, she once again scanned the surroundings for anything unusual, despite the fact that both she and Kal had already done so numerous times.

    Seeing something glint oddly underneath Luthor Corp, she quickly began to slip through the crowd as she spoke into her earpiece, “Guys, I see something. Hold positions while I check it out.”

    “Keep us apprised,” was all the response she got as Brainy maintained his vigil in the command van.

    She passed the screen separating the press from the back of the stage and quickly looked up, briefly catching Lena's eye as a look of understanding passed between them. Kara felt a pang of regret in her chest, but quickly pushed it aside to focus on the task at hand.

    As she approached the soon-to-be-rechristened Luthor Corp building, Kara focused on the sublevels, looking for what had drawn her attention earlier. Updating the group as she went, Kara quickly made her way to the subterranean parking garage beneath the building. Back in the distance, she could hear the crowd fall silent as Lena approached the podium.

    “It's a bomb,” she called out as she changed into her supersuit, “It's attached to one of the central pillars beneath Luthor Corp.”

    As she waited for Brainy to pull up the schematics to the building, she sent him several photos on her DEO-issued phone, before intently studying the bomb, as if she expected she might find a big button labeled ‘DEFUSE’ if only she looked a little harder.

    “Supergirl, if that bomb goes off, the entire building will likely collapse,” Brainy replied after a few moments of studying the bomb and schematics.

    “Well, any ideas how I can avoid that?” Kara asked, impatience coloring her voice.

    “Give me a moment. Superman, please begin scanning the surroundings for more bombs. Alex, J'onn, keep your eyes sharp, Corben will likely try and make his move while we are distracted.”

    As Brainy began addressing the other agents while simultaneously analyzing the explosive, Kara tuned into Lena's speech, listening as she spoke of getting out from under the shadow of her brother's crimes and making the newly rebranded L-Corp a force for good.

    Smiling faintly, Kara's attention returned to the bomb. Without Brainy's help, she had no hope of defusing it, but if worst came to worst she could absorb most of the blast with her body.

    As she had the thought, a sharp beep sounded from the bomb as it primed to explode. Without thinking, she blasted it with her freeze breath and ripped it off the support column, attempting to wrap it in her cape. Before she could fully enclose the device, it detonated, the force of the explosion funneling through the gaps in her cape, throwing Kara clear across the room and shaking the foundations of the building.

    Kara blinked rapidly, trying to clear the smoke from her eyes and the fog from her brain. In the back of her mind, she could hear the crowd panicking in the aftershocks of the explosion, but it was nearly impossible to focus with the deafening ringing in her ears.

    Taking a few dazed steps towards the door, Kara shook her head, trying to clear her mind and stop the explosions that were still echoing in her ears. As she stumbled into the wall closest to the door she had entered, she faintly heard everyone talking in her earpiece as they sprung into action.

    “Alex, the police officer at your nine o'clock is headed straight for Miss Luthor, that is most likely Corben.”

    “J'onn, I've cleared the left side of explosives, you and the other DEO agents direct the civilians that way while I check the rest of the buildings.”

    “I've got Corben, engaging.”

    Kara did her best to force her mind to process all that she was hearing. When she heard her sister's voice cleave through the chaos coming over the line as the DEO attempted to control the scene and lead the civilians to safety, she stopped and peered through the ground toward where her sister was violently exchanging blows with a man whose face she couldn't make out.

    Corben.

    Anger burned through her, searing the fog from her brain, clearing her head and propelling her from the building with a single-minded intent. Within moments, she was outside, but she was still too far away to stop what was unfolding before her eyes.

    Corben had managed to get the upper hand on Alex, and there was no way Kara would be able to catch a bullet from the gun now angled at her sister's head in time, not without causing catastrophic damage to everything between her and Corben, Alex included.

    Kara felt her breath leave her as she saw, as if in slow motion, Corben's finger begin to squeeze the trigger. Despite the fact that she didn't need oxygen, she still felt as if she were suffocating as fear sunk its talons deep into her chest. She forced herself to move, blitzing across the space between them, but she was too slow.

    She heard the gun fire.

    Once.

    Twice.

    She felt the scream clawing out of her throat, her eyes blazing with unholy fire as she prepared to smite Corben from the very surface of existence for what he had done, but at the last moment before she unleashed herself upon him, before the apocalyptic heat tore from her eyes, she saw that it wasn't Alex's body falling to the floor, but Corben's, and it wasn't he who was holding a smoking gun, but Lena. Lena.

    Before she could think, before she could even process, she had Lena wrapped in her arms, “You saved her, you saved her. Thank you.”

    “Of course,” Lena mumbled, clearly in shock, having just shot a man.

    Kara reached out and gently took the pistol from Lena just as Alex looked over her shoulder from where she was checking on Corben, “Both shots were through-and-through. He needs a hospital.”

    “There are no more explosives and the civilians are safe. Good work finding that bomb, Supergirl,” Kal said as he floated down from where he had been scanning the surroundings.

    And as much as Kara would normally preen at her cousin's praise, she couldn't help but feel slightly disappointed at the standoffish way he was eyeing Lena.

    After everything else had settled down at the scene, Kara had seen Lena back into the arms of her security detail before flying back to the DEO to debrief with the others. As Brainy worked to figure out who had hired Corben, the rest of them discussed the events of the day.

    “Good job out there everyone,” J'onn said with a look of fatherly pride for Alex and Kara, and a tense look that Kara didn't understand at Kal, “No civilians were injured and we caught the culprit. And an extra good job to you, Kara. If you hadn't seen that bomb, the whole building might've come down, and who knows how many people would’ve been hurt.”

    “I'm just glad we caught him,” Kara replied, letting out a relieved breath, “I feel much better that there isn't an assassin on the loose anymore.”

    “I have something,” Brainy announced, drawing the focus of the other four, “I was able to locate the account that Corben used to receive his payment. The origin of the payment is still unknown, but I was able to find a name associated with that account: Luthor.”

    “Can you tell which Luthor it was? Was it Lex?” Kal pressed, fury lighting his gaze.

    “Unknown, I am still working on it.”

    “Great,” Kara huffed, “Now Lena's mother is trying to kill her, and we don't know why.”

    “We still can't count Lena out, Kara,” Alex said.

    Kara shot her sister an incredulous look, “You think Lena paid someone to try and kill her? Twice?!”

    “We still don't know what Corben was trying to accomplish,” Alex answered, “He is a professional assassin, and you don't find it odd that Lena never received so much as a scratch in either attempt.”

    “She didn't get a scratch,” Kara spat disbelievingly at her sister, “because we were there both times to save her.”

    “Ladies-” J'onn tried cutting in, attempting to dispel the tension, but Kara bulldozed right over him.

    “Or don't you think that getting her helicopter shot out of the sky would've left her with worse than a scratch?”

    “I'm just saying it is too soon to rule her out as a suspect. She could've had an ulterior motive to hire Corben, and the assassination attempts were just a ploy to distract us.”

    “She saved your life, Alex!!” Kara shouted at her sister.

    “And now she looks completely free of any wrongdoing. Coming from a Luthor, I find that extremely suspicious.”

    “Look, Kara,” Kal interjected, “We know that your previous friendship with Lena might be clouding your judgment, but as I reminded you earlier, and as I will continue to remind you, I felt the same about Lex. I ignored every piece of evidence and every sign that he wasn't the person I thought he was, and it nearly cost me everything. Lena is a Luthor, and until she has proven beyond a shadow of a doubt that she isn't like her brother, she cannot be trusted.”

    Shooting Alex and Kal venomous looks, she rounded on J'onn and demanded with far more anger than he deserved, considering he had yet to say anything on the matter, “And you?”

    J'onn stared stoically back at her, letting her anger wash over him without comment as he gathered his thoughts.

    “Seeing as she has yet to do anything to indicate herself, we can treat her as a friendly for now, but we can't just ignore her family history,” Holding up his hand to forestall Kara's objection, he continued, “We won't act without evidence, but if she does anything to indicate she might follow in her brother's footsteps, we will have no choice but to bring her in before she can do significant damage.”

    Kara knew he was being fair, and that if the choice was up to Alex or Kal, they wouldn't have been so generous with their trust, but she was still angry. Rather than staying and yelling, she turned sharply on her heel and walked away.

    Alex watched as Kara spun and stormed away, perturbed by the way her hair blew away from her face from the wind generated by the speed of the movement, and the anger it belied in her sister. After Kara had marched back to the landing pad, Alex looked back towards Clark. As she met his eyes, she could tell they were thinking the same thing, but it was Clark who voiced it.

    “I don't understand why she is defending Lena so vehemently.”

    Initially, Alex had shared his confusion, but the more she thought about it, the more it made sense. Alex knew if there was one thing Kara could be described as, it was loyal. She would never hesitate to defend her friends, and defended her family even more fiercely. The difference, she thought, is that Kal and herself were usually on the other side of the equation, being defended by Kara rather than having Kara defend someone from them.

    “I know you know this, but maybe sometimes we forget,” Alex responded, “Kara is the most loyal friend you could ever hope to have. She will never abandon someone because of the actions of another.”

    “But Lex-” Kal started, his anger overtaking his reason.

    “I know, I know,” Alex agreed, “but that is the difference between her and us; She can see beyond what Lex did in Metropolis, but we will never forget. You will never forget that Lois was nearly collateral damage of Lex's plan, and I will never forget that Kara could've very easily gotten killed if Lex had known she was there. We will never look past Lena's family unless we know she is nothing like them, whereas Kara never truly considered it a factor.”

    Kal nodded, although he still didn't look very happy about Kara's defense of a Luthor. Alex didn't blame him, knowing that Lena could very well pose as great a threat, if not an even greater one, to Kara's life than Lex had to Kal's, but without some form of evidence, Kara would never abandon someone, even someone that she hadn't considered a friend in years.

    She pondered the various methods she could pursue to gather evidence, legal or illegal, when she suddenly had an idea.

    “J'onn-” she started.

    “No Alex, I won't do it.”

    “I didn't even say-”

    “I don't have to be a psychic to know what you were about to suggest and I won't do it,” He said firmly, “It is a great invasion of privacy, and unless I have a very good reason to suspect Miss Luthor, I am not willing to cross that line. Until you come up with some proof, we will have to stick to our usual methods.”

    “Well then,” she turned to address Clark and Brainy, “I guess we will just have to keep our eyes out for evidence then.”

    Chapter End Notes

    So, there it is: Chapter 1. If you have thoughts, or any ideas as to what the trope is, feel free to let me know in the comments. I won't be confirming anything, but I think it will become rather clear if you were right or not over the next two chapters

    Chapter 2

    Chapter Notes

    Surprise, I'm early! This is in part because I'm impatient, and also because I plan to post on Saturday/Sunday from now on to make things easier on my schedule, and as I cover later, these next two chapters are long. Also, I should probably notice that I tend to be pretty loose with the POV, so it will transition from one character to another in the middle of scenes, so keep that in mind.

    I don't think I fooled as many of you as I would have liked to last chapter, but alas I don't think it's because I (or Kara) wasn't subtle enough, but because you all know too much about the characters. If I did fool you, then that is a great success for me, but the jig is most certainly up, if not in this chapter then in the next.

    So, Guess what! Flashback time. Over the next two chapters, we will see all the backstory that everyone was talking about in the first chapter. In the beginning, I debated spreading these about the chapters, but then they probably wouldn't have been seen chronologically, so I decided to put them all into these two chapters. I had assumed that these flashbacks weren't going to take up much length, but boy was I wrong. These next two chapters are the two longest in the whole fic (13k for this one and 17.5k for the next one). I could have split them up into smaller pieces, and it might very well have made the pacing of certain events a bit better, but I didn't want to spend more than two chapters in the past back-to-back, and I'm sure many of you also are looking forward to how this story will turn out and don't want to wait either. Anyway, time to get into it.

    To stave off any potential confusion, the dates of these flashbacks are calculated in comparison to the end of the last chapter, not to the beginning chapter 4 (Which will take place some time a bit after the end of chapter 1), and were rounded to the nearest week/month for convenience sake.

    7 years, 4 months ago

    Kara was excited to spend the holidays with her cousin. In the five years she had been on earth, the time they had managed to spend together was far too infrequent for her liking. Between Kara's school, Kal's work and superhero duties, and life, they were kept apart for the most part.

    Being near Kal was soothing for Kara, not only because he was a part of her home that had also survived, but also because he was the only one who could understand what growing up a Kryptonian on Earth could entail. He understood how easy it was to get overwhelmed by the massive amount of stimulus she was always receiving, the constant rein she had to keep on her strength and emotions to avoid breaking anything or anyone around her, and having to awkwardly explain away any indications that she wasn't human.

    On the other hand, having grown up a human, Kal had no idea the cultural strain that Kara faced each and every day. He didn't have to cope with unfamiliar terms, phrases, and gestures; he didn't have to pretend to understand historical and pop culture references that Kara had no basis to understand; and most importantly, he wasn't forced to face constant judgment for failing to adhere to cultural behaviors, practices, and mores that were often completely different than those that had been ingrained in her since childhood.

    Being an alien in an unfamiliar world was hard. Being an alien in high school on a new world was significantly harder.

    However, she still loved her cousin dearly, and couldn't blame him for things outside of his control. When she had first arrived on earth, there had been some distance between them as they both had to overcome their expectations for the other, but they had grown closer as they bonded over their lost world. Kal had never known Krypton, but he still wanted to learn about his people and culture, and Kara had gladly taught him all she knew.

    Now, being with Kal was a respite, both culturally and physically, where she didn't have to pretend to be someone she wasn't, where she had an outlet for her powers that she never could find anywhere else, and most notably, someone with whom she didn't have to constantly control herself.

    For the few days leading up to Christmas Eve, it had mostly been Kara, Kal, and occasionally Lois, who for the most part had been leaving the cousins to their own devices, only stepping in when Kal had to leave for his hero duties. However, following the Christmas Eve lunch the trio had just finished, Kal approached Kara with a regretful expression.

    “Kara, I know I promised you we'd spend the whole week together, just you and I, but I was hoping I could spend some time with my friend today.”

    Kara looked up at him from where she was sitting on the table, feeling the corners of her mouth pull down slightly in the beginnings of a frown. This week was supposed to be a celebration of her upcoming graduation the following summer, and Kara felt that old sense of abandonment well up in her chest once more, tears threatening to spring up in her eyes.

    She knew it was a reaction from the first few months she had spent on earth, when Kal dumped her with the Danvers, promising they would take care of her, when she had wanted to stay with him in Metropolis. If she couldn’t do that, she would have liked to remain in the fortress, with what little remained of her culture, that she had lived in for more than two months when she had arrived, but he had insisted going to the Danvers was for the best. Over the following six months, things had been tense, with Kal barely visiting, but eventually, they had come to understand one another a bit better and had been growing closer ever since.

    At the growing look of upset on Kara's face, Kal quickly continued speaking, “Normally, I would put him off, but he just lost his father. You can come too if you wish.”

    Kara felt her hurt fade. She couldn't be mad at him for wanting to comfort his friend after he had just lost a parent, even if he hadn't been there as she grieved the loss of her family, of her planet. It didn't hurt that Kal had waited until she was well-fed to ask, nor that he had asked in Kryptonese. Hearing her native tongue always soothed Kara, even as it caused a pang of loss in her chest.

    “Of course I don't mind. Let me get ready then we can head over,” Kara agreed, the Kryptonese flowing off her tongue much more smoothly and fluidly than it had Kal's.

    A little over an hour later, Kara watched with fascination as the cast iron gate swung open, allowing their taxi through. As they drove up to the mansion, Kara took in the property with avid interest. The land surrounding it seemed to sprawl on forever, beds of gorgeous flowers and perfectly manicured lawns flowed throughout the elegantly landscaped expanse until they met pale stone walls far in the distance.

    As they neared the top of the driveway, the palatial mansion became the focus of Kara's attention. She had seen such buildings while watching television and movies, desperately trying to acclimate to human culture, but the true scale and grandeur of such things had never been adequately portrayed.

    As they exited the taxi, the front doors were thrown open, pulling Kara from her stupor. A man was quickly approaching them, clad in an immaculate business suit and completely bald. Before Kara could ask if this was the friend they had come to see, the man had pulled Kal into a hug that likely would've been enough to hurt a human.

    “Thank you for coming, Clark,” The man said, releasing Kal from the hug.

    Kara fidgeted slightly as the man turned an inquisitive look on her, before turning back to Kal with a raised eyebrow.

    “This is Kara,” Kal offered, “Our families are close. She is visiting from Midvale for the week and I thought I'd bring her along. Kara, this is my friend, Lex.”

    “That's all I get, friend,” Lex said the word as if it was a grave insult, but winked at Kara.

    “Best friend,” Clark amended, sighing playfully at Lex's antics.

    Kara followed Lex and Clark inside, silently marveling at the décor that Lex was breezing past as if it were nothing more than ordinary. Kara took in as much as she could as they moved through the spaces quickly, drinking in paintings and doing her best not to touch any of the richly colored woods and fabrics.

    The Luthor Mansion harshly contrasted with the home that she had grown used to in Midvale. The Danvers's house, while spacious, didn't feel empty, and radiated a sense of warmth and family. The mansion, however, gave off a sense of opulence, distance, and emptiness, as if no amount of love and happiness could possibly fill the space, and everyone who resided there had long since given up trying. With the Danvers, life was something to be lived and enjoyed. Here, it seemed like something to be survived.

    Along the way, they encountered a severe-looking woman who seemed to embody the aloof atmosphere of the entire mansion, whom Lex introduced as his mother, Lillian. She offered them both a sparse greeting before carrying on with her business.

    Finally, after several more minutes of walking, they came to a sitting room, inside which there was a girl who looked to be about Kara's age.

    “Lena, this is Kara. She is a family friend of Clark's. Is it okay if she stays with you while Clark and I talk?”

    Piercing green eyes pulled away from the page they had been reading and met Kara's. Lena didn't say anything, just nodded her head in acquiescence.

    After Kal and Lex left, the former giving Kara a reassuring look before departing, Kara was left alone with Lena. She sat gingerly in one of the chairs in the room, distinctly under the impression that it was worth more than anything the Danvers owned. She looked over at Lena, too nervous to say anything, and not wanting to disrupt the content silence the other girl had fallen back into.

    With Lena's attention returned to her book, Kara tried looking around the room, taking in nothing before her attention was quickly drawn back to Lena. Kara could feel a loss weighing on Lena, something that resonated deep within Kara, where she still tightly held onto her people and her planet.

    Suddenly unable to bear the quiet anymore, Kara broke the silence, “I'm sorry to hear about your dad.”

    Lena looked up at her, a look of surprise flittering over her face, as if she had forgotten she was no longer alone, her eyes briefly meeting Kara's eyes before her gaze flicked back down to her book as she fiddled with its pages.

    “He wasn't really my dad,” Lena said, still intently focused on the pages of her book, ”I was adopted when I was four.”

    Kara found herself looking sadly at the other girl. Clearly, the loss of her father wasn't the one that weighed so heavily on her, but Kara was determined to help alleviate it nonetheless. Kara knew she might never be able to heal from her own trauma, but she would help Lena with hers in any way she could, even if she had to start small.

    “I was adopted too,” Kara said, trying to at least let Lena know that she wasn't alone.

    Lena gave her a small smile, her attention once again quickly returning to her book, a signal that not even Kara could misinterpret.

    “What're you reading?” Kara asked, doing her best to respect Lena's unwillingness to continue the topic, but not wanting to lapse back into silence.

    “Just reviewing one of my old Organic Chemistry textbooks, I was bored.”

    Kara considered Lena more closely. She had always struggled with the pacing of Earth's science classes, having learned most of what would be considered Earth's most advanced sciences before she even turned thirteen. She was pretty sure, however, that Organic chemistry wasn't usually taught until after high school. What she wasn't sure of was why Lena seemed embarrassed, or maybe even ashamed of it.

    “Reviewing? Isn't that a college-level textbook?” She asked carefully.

    “Didn't Clark tell you?” Lena said with a bit of a smirk, growing a bit more confident at this change in topics, “Us Luthors are geniuses. I just graduated college and I'm barely seventeen. I would've done it sooner, but I did want to have a somewhat normal life. And yes, before you ask, I do read science textbooks for fun.”

    Kara wasn't sure if it was the soft lilt in Lena's voice that she was only convinced she could hear because of her super hearing, or the defensive tone Lena had at the end, or even the fact that Lena seemed to share the love of learning that Kara had gotten from her father, but Kara was convinced that the statement wasn't meant to be boastful.

    “That's awesome, Lena,” Kara reassured her, reaching out without thinking and squeezing Lena's hand, hoping she chose the correct gesture to comfort the other girl.

    Lena looked down to where their hands met, her brows furrowing slightly as if she wasn't used to such affections before looking back up and smiling hesitantly at Kara. Kara returned the smile brightly, proud of herself for getting it right, but also truly happy to have brought a smile to Lena's face.

    “Most people I went to school with thought I was weird,” Lena confessed, pulling her hand back and fiddling self-consciously with her fingers.

    “Well I think you're amazing. I haven't even graduated high school yet, and I'm already eighteen. You should never be ashamed of wanting to learn or for being yourself.”

    Kara reached out again, and this time their hands met in the middle. Lena was quite surprised to find herself smiling as she looked into Kara's eyes. Lena didn't smile, and she definitely didn't have friends, but nonetheless, she found herself wanting to be Kara's friend.

    She had never met anyone like Kara. Someone who didn't want anything from her but for her to be herself and to be happy. With just a few earnest sentences, Kara had already bypassed the walls that she had built after years of bad experiences that had allowed her to survive growing up in the Luthor Mansion.

    Hours later, once Lex and Clark had returned, and after Lena had pointedly ignored Lex's comment about this being the happiest he had seen her in years, Lena found herself facing the displeasing prospect that Kara had to leave.

    Never in such a short period of time, had Lena ever warmed up this much to anyone, not even Lex, whom she hadn't admitted to liking for over a year. So, when it came time for Kara and Clark to depart, Lena surprised even herself by pulling Kara aside.

    “I know we just met, and that we are going to college on opposite sides of the nation, but I would like it if we could be friends,” Lena said, ignoring the frantic beating of her heart.

    “I would love that,” Kara said, beaming back at Lena.

    Pulling her phone out, Lena handed it to Kara for her to enter her phone number. In the brief moment before Kara took it, Lena felt her stomach twist uncomfortably, as if she was still afraid Kara would say no.

    As Kara handed Lena her phone back, Clark called out to tell her their taxi had arrived. Lena turned her head away, not wanting to have to watch Kara leave, when suddenly, she found herself wrapped in Kara's arms. She immediately stiffened up before she realized what was happening, but when she did, she relaxed, tension she hadn't even realized she'd been carrying flowing out of her.

    “Merry Christmas Eve,” Kara said, pulling back slightly to look at Lena.

    Before Lena, still shocked by this recent turn of events, could respond, Kara released her and turned toward the taxi, leaving a flustered Lena behind, who secretly thought to herself that she could get used to having Kara as a friend.

    When they got home, Kara had bid Kal and Lois goodnight and headed up to the guest bedroom she was sleeping in while staying with them. She flopped down in her bed, ignoring the frame groaning in protest, too busy thinking about her new friend.

    Lena had initially been aloof, but once Kara had gotten through the stiffness of her upbringing, and her disbelief that anyone might want to be her friend purely for the pleasure of her company, she found that she really liked Lena. She was even considering asking Kal if they could go back to see the Luthors again before they left, knowing it would be unlikely they would see each other again until the next time she was in Metropolis.

    Kara was pulled out of her reverie by her phone buzzing in her pocket, a sound she definitely had not been waiting for since she got into the taxi earlier that evening. Looking at the screen, she saw that it was indeed a text from Lena.

    11:37 PM

    [Lena]: Hey, It's Lena.

    [Kara]: Lena!

    [Kara]: Hi!

    [Lena]: Hi Kara. How was the trip home?

    [Kara]: It wasn't bad, but I wish we could have stayed longer. I had a lot of fun today

    [Lena]: I wish you could've stayed too, but I actually need to pack. Lex is going on a business trip tomorrow to London for a week, and I am going with him

    [Kara]: Oh, that's too bad :( I was hoping to see you again before I go back to Midvale

    [Lena]: I would've liked that, but this is Lex's first business trip as CEO, and I wanted to support him

    [Kara]: Of course. I understand. We will have to get together the next time we are both in Metropolis

    [Lena]: Definitely

    [Kara]: Well, I'll let you get back to packing. Goodnight Lena

    [Lena]: Goodnight Kara, and Merry Christmas

    6 years, 3 months ago

    Kara was, without a doubt, Lena's best friend. Her only real friend, if Lena was being honest with herself. Despite only having seen each other once in person, and going to school thousands of miles apart, they still talked for hours every day.

    They would tell each other about their days, Kara complaining about her professors while Lena complained about her fellow doctoral candidates. They would compare the crappy pickup lines that guys threw at them, and Lena was even pretty sure that Kara was pretending not to understand any of her science and math classes just to have an excuse to convince Lena to tutor her, insisting that she was far better than any of the tutors on campus.

    In short, Lena had never been closer to another person, besides Lex, in her life. That's why it hurt so much.

    Her brother's anti-alien policies had begun catching the attention of the media, and it was clearly destroying the friendship between Clark and Lex. The further apart the two former best friends grew, the more certain Lena became she and Kara would share the same fate. After all, she knew Kara would take Clark's side, and Kara would expect Lena to take Lex's.

    So, slowly over the weeks, she started distancing herself from Kara, hoping that if they weren't as close when their friendship snapped, it wouldn't hurt as much. It didn't matter that every time Lena ignored one of Kara's calls or texts, or made an excuse to get out of talking to her her heart ached more than she thought possible. This was for the best. It had to be.

    Kara certainly wasn't making it any easier. The more Lena tried pulling away, the harder Kara fought to hold her close. Finally, the day after Lex publicly called Superman out as a threat, and Clark released a scathing article in Superman's defense, Lena knew the bridge between the two former best friends was too damaged to ever be repaired, and that it wouldn't be long before she and Kara found themselves in the same position.

    Deciding it would be easier to end things now, and knowing she wouldn't survive if this dragged on any longer, she opened up her phone to text Kara after nearly a week of unanswered messages.

    12:18 PM

    [Lena]: Kara, I need to talk to you

    [Kara]: Lena!! I've been worried about you

    [Kara]: You haven't been answering me, what's wrong? What do you need to talk about with me?

    [Lena]: Kara...

    But Lena couldn't bring herself to end their doomed friendship just yet, she wasn't strong enough. Instead, she cried and ignored Kara for a little bit longer, desperately clinging to the illusion that she and Kara could be okay, that she didn't have to give up something, the only thing that got her through most days, but she could only pretend for so long. Finally, Lena gathered herself to say what she needed to say.

    12:55 PM

    [Lena]: Kara, we shouldn't be friends anymore

    [Kara]: WHAT?!

    [Kara]: Lena, what are you talking about!?!

    [Incoming Call Kara Danvers]

    [Kara]: Lena, answer me!

    [Incoming Call Kara Danvers]

    [Incoming Call Kara Danvers]

    [Kara]: Lena!

    [Kara]: Lena, answer the call or I am going to come find you to talk some sense into you!!

    Lena, worn down from weeks of ignoring her best friend's call and having no doubt that Kara would somehow track her down, finally gave in and answered Kara's next call.

    "Kara-"

    “What the hell are you talking about Lena!”

    The outburst momentarily stunned Lena, because not only was that the most profane sentence she had ever heard from Kara, but also because this was by far the angriest she had ever heard her friend. Sure, from time to time she got frustrated at her professors or guys who wouldn't stop hitting on her, but it had never once pierced her bubbly demeanor. Now, however, she sounded pissed.

    “Lex-”

    “What does he have to do with us?” Kara snapped.

    Lena took a deep breath, gathering herself, her frustration at getting interrupted twice momentarily pushing aside her grief and allowing her to speak.

    “Everyone is treating me differently since he started on his new anti-alien path. They are treating me like I am the xenophobe. I know that you'll take Clark's side and everyone expects me to be like Lex. Everyone else already hates me. I couldn't stand it if you hated me too,” Lena let out a brief gasping sob, quickly losing control of her usually tightly reined-in emotions, "Please Kara, please. It's easier this way.”

    “You think I hate you?” Kara said, her wounded tone causing Lena to feel even worse.

    “Why shouldn't you? If you don't now, you will soon. I know how you feel about alien haters. Please let me go, Kara, I can't stand the thought of you hating me too,” Lena was fully sobbing now, overwhelmed by her surety that Kara would agree and tell her that she did, in fact, hate her.

    “I don't hate you, Lena. I could never hate you. I-” Kara cut off suddenly before continuing, “You are my best friend.”

    Lena felt a slight warmth in her chest, slowly growing and beating away the empty hollowness that had enveloped her life for the past weeks. A dimly flickering hope that Kara did believe in her, that the most important thing in her life wasn't going anywhere.

    “You- You don't?” Lena asked tremulously.

    “Of course I don't Lena. I know you. I know you are nothing like Lex, that you are far too smart, far too kind, and far too good to ever think what he does about aliens,” Kara said with unwavering conviction, “I will always be your friend, Lena, and I would never judge you for someone else's actions.”

    “Thank you, Kara. I-” Lena paused for a brief moment, taking a deep breath before continuing, “I'm sorry.”

    “It's okay, Lena,” Kara reassured her, “But please don't do that to me again.”

    “I won't. I promise.”

    Lena hadn't felt this light in weeks. With Kara's belief in her, she could face down the maelstrom of condemnation that she received on a daily basis without succumbing to her tendency for self-blame.

    “Alright, now that we've got that settled, I have to get back to my chemistry class,” Kara said, blowing out a sigh of relief.

    “Kara!” Lena squawked.

    “It's okay, you'll just have to tutor me to make up for pulling me out of class.”

    Lena could practically hear the teasing smile in Kara's voice, but if Kara wanted to continue to pretend to be bad at her classes as an excuse to spend more time with her, who was Lena to complain?

    “You're on.”

    3 years, 10 months ago

    Kara had decided she was mad at Kal. Not only had she wasted over five hours flying on an airplane when she could've flown much faster by herself, but what had truly sealed Kal's fate was the food. Kara's early departure time had prevented her from eating a full breakfast, and the pitifully abysmal bags of peanuts and pretzels she had been given on the flight had done nothing to satiate her appetite.

    As she approached the baggage carousel she spotted Kal already there waiting for her. A smile split her face briefly before she remembered that she was definitely very upset with him, and she forced her face into a pout. At the same moment, Kal turned to look at her, and noticing her pout, hastily summoned a look of contrition to cover his smirk. All this did was let Kara know that he wasn't fooled by her pout, and he was happy to see her too.

    “Lois has a feast waiting for us at home,” He promised.

    At the mention of food, Kara's composure cracked, and she smiled widely at her cousin, earning an equally wide smile in return. As much as Kal's lectures about not using her powers and the resultant irritations that it caused, Kara's ire was easily rectified by the promise of food. It didn't hurt that, if Kara should choose to follow him into herodom, she knew Kal would support her completely.

    Kara pulled Kal into a tight hug, one that doubtlessly would've killed a human, and sighed as Kal returned it. They had no one but each other that they could hug at full strength, and both enjoyed not having to rein themselves in for once. Clark slowly started squeezing Kara harder, eliciting a small laugh from her as she returned the gesture.

    It was an old game between them, one that had started as a way for Kara to have a safe place to unleash the incredible strength she possessed without fear of injuring someone. As she had grown older, it had become a competition between the two strongest beings on the planet to find out who truly was the strongest.

    So Kara squeezed. She squeezed with enough force to crush a mountain, enough to crack a planet. She unleashed her full strength while Kal did the same. A few minutes passed, ordinary people walking by, not realizing that they were witnessing a monumental struggle between two titans. Finally, with a small grimace, Kal released Kara, and she pulled back with a victorious smirk. She had never won before, and she couldn't wait to tell Lois.

    “Geez, cuz. Have you been sunbathing or something?” Kal asked, rubbing his ribcage.

    Kara didn't answer, just kept smirking gleefully. He definitely wasn't hearing the end of this anytime soon.

    Kara and Kal turned their attention to the carousel that had at last started to be loaded with the luggage from Kara's flight. As they waited for Kara's bags to appear, they caught each other up on their day-to-day lives. They talked at least once a week, so there wasn't much to cover, but it was nice nonetheless. After grabbing one of Kara's suitcases each, making sure to pretend they felt at least somewhat heavy, they made their way out to Kal's car in the parking garage.

    On the drive home, they discussed Kal's exploits as Superman, a topic they only ever referenced over the phone in the case Kal was injured due to their concerns for secrecy. They discussed the topic for most of the ride home, talking about the new threats that seemed to pop up almost weekly, even covering some of the more mundane aspects, all while dancing around the topic of the Luthors.

    They both knew that Lex couldn't be trusted as far as they could throw him, which was quite far, all things considered. Where they disagreed was Lena. Kara was convinced that she had no ill will towards the world, aliens, or the two Kryptonians. Kal, on the other hand, was much less willing to trust the sister of the man who had once been his best friend. He wisely kept his distrust to himself though, or he risked getting an earful from a very, very irate Kara.

    Kara, however, didn't have the luxury of avoiding the inevitable argument this time around, as she was planning on taking Lena out to dinner while in Metropolis. In the three and a half years they had been friends, they had never once seen each other in person outside of their initial meeting. By some unfortunate twist of fate, every time Kara had been in Metropolis, Lena had been pulled away by Lex or Lillian to the other side of the world. Kara found the fact that she had only seen her best friend in the flesh once distasteful, and since they were finally both back in the same city, she was determined to rectify it.

    After Kal had finished telling Kara about Reactron, a new villain that was plaguing Metropolis, the conversation lulled into a pleasant silence that Kara reluctantly broke, “Lena and I are going out to dinner tomorrow night.”

    “Are you sure that's wise?” He asked in a deceptively neutral tone as the steering wheel groaned under his grip.

    “She doesn't know I'm an alien, not that it would matter to her,” Kara retorted, already getting testy, “She's my best friend, whom I haven't seen in three and a half years. I'm going whether you like it or not.”

    “You know I worry about you, Kara. With everything going on with Lex-”

    “I know you worry, but Lena isn't like Lex. And need I remind you that Lena couldn't murder me if she tried. I'm going, unless you have some proof that Lena is in on Lex's schemes?” Kara couldn't help but end with the sardonic question, knowing full well Kal had none.

    “I do not,” Kal replied stiffly.

    At once, Kara regretted her tone. Besides Alex and Lena, Kal was probably the person she was closest to on Earth, and she hated to argue with him. If only he would let up on Lena, she thought her life might be perfect, or at least as close to perfect as anything could ever come with the gaping hole the loss of her planet had left within her.

    After a few moments of tense silence, Kara spoke up again, “I'm sorry, Kal. Can we just forget about it for now? I want to enjoy my time here with you.”

    “Of course,” he assented, much more jovial than mere moments before.

    They spent the rest of the night with smiles on their faces, bantering back and forth over dinner, challenging each other to see who could eat the largest portion. After Kara had once again vanquished Kal, she had gone up to bed with excitement simmering in her chest. After far too long apart, she would finally be seeing Lena again tomorrow.

    Kara rung her hands nervously as she surveyed the clothes strewn all over her room. It was ridiculous. There was nothing to be nervous about, she was just going to dinner with her best friend. A completely normal thing to do with your best friend, but the fact remained that there seemed to be nothing adequate for her to wear to this completely, definitely normal event.

    Finally tiring of the sound of frantic whooshing, Lois had entered Kara's room and carefully navigated the quagmire of clothes on the floor to reach where Kara was looking at herself in the mirror, debating whether the blue or red blouse looked better for the third time.

    Lightly setting a hand on Kara's arm, slightly startling the frenetic Kryptonian, Lois smiled kindly at Kara in the mirror, “Go with the blue, it brings out your eyes.”

    Ten minutes later, they had finally settled on the blouse Lois had picked, a deep blue that made Kara's eyes look like the sky at twilight, paired with a black sweater vest and slacks. Lois helped her apply a light layer of makeup, before pulling her hair back into a loose and intentionally messy bun.

    Giving Kara a once over, she quickly unbuttoned her top two buttons so that her collar bones were ever so slightly visible, before smiling at her work, “There, Kara, you look great.”

    “Thank you so much, Lois. I have no idea what I would've done without you,” Kara said, looking at herself in the mirror and finally feeling ready.

    “Of course Kiddo, let's get you on your way.”

    Kara followed Lois out of the room, before heading down the stairs towards the front door. Kara and Lena had agreed that it was best they meet at the restaurant, lest they run the risk of running into the other's family. There were still thirty minutes till they were supposed to meet, but Kara figured she would rather be early and wait than stay around and risk Kal lecturing her again.

    As she turned the doorknob, she heard Kal call after her, “Kara, wait.”

    With a slight grimace, she turned back to face him.

    “I know you and I have a difference of opinion about Lena,” He started, an earnest look on his face, “But I just want you to be safe.”

    “I am still invincible you know, and believe it or not, it seems like I'm stronger than Superman these days.”

    “I know,” he said with a small smile, “But I can still worry about you same as Lois worries about me when I am out being Superman.”

    “It means a lot that you worry,” Kara said truthfully, "If anything happens, I'll call you. I promise.”

    “Thank you.”

    As she turned to leave, Kara distinctly heard Lois scold him, telling him that he had better not eavesdrop.

    Despite arriving 10 minutes early, Kara found Lena already waiting for her at the entrance to the restaurant.

    “Lena!” Kara called out.

    “Kara!” Lena managed just before Kara tackled her into a tight hug.

    “I missed you. It feels like forever since I've seen you.”

    “We FaceTimed two days ago,” Lena said with a teasing smirk, not that Kara could see it with her head buried in Lena's neck.

    “You know what I meant,” Kara pouted as she inhaled deeply, pulling the scent of Lena deep into her lungs.

    “I missed you too,” Lena relented, smiling warmly as she pulled Kara a little more snuggly into her.

    They lingered in the hug for a few minutes, neither of them in any particular rush to break it. Finally, Lena pulled away and grabbed Kara's hand, leading her into the restaurant. As they waited, Kara took in Lena's outfit. She was wearing a dark green cashmere turtleneck sweater with dark jeans. It lent her a soft look that Kara knew most people never got to see.
