    Rather than actually tell Lena, Kara looked down at her feet as she started fiddling with her glasses, suddenly finding herself petrified of hurting Lena.

    “Hey,” Lena soothed, reaching out and taking Kara's hand, “It's okay. Whatever you need to tell me it will be okay.”

    “Well, there are actually two things,” Kara said, remembering how she managed to escape and longing to postpone telling Lena her secret as long as she could, “Lois knows.”

    “Are you sure?” Lena asked, her face carefully blank.

    “She has to know,” Kara replied, “Every time I tried to get away, someone would either stop me, or come with me, and every time, Lois would give me this look. Then, today, she pulled me aside and told me to go to the store for food we definitely didn't need, then looked me dead in the eye and mouthed 'You have one hour, enjoy it.'”

    “Do you think she'll tell Clark?”

    “No,” Kare responded after a moment, “I don't think I fooled her in the first place. If she was going to tell Clark, she would've done so by now, and she wouldn't've helped me get away.”

    “Then there is nothing to worry about,” Lena said, causing Kara to look even more worried, “What was the other thing you needed to tell me?”

    Kara, it seemed, couldn't contain her nerves anymore and shot to her feet, quickly walking to the other side of the room. Lena waited patiently for Kara to gather herself, watching as her girlfriend's shoulders rose and fell with every breath. Finally, when Kara did turn back to face Lena, she could see that Kara's nerves had given way to fear.

    “Please don't be mad,” Kara said, looking close to tears, “I've wanted to tell you for so long, but I've never been able to find the right way to say it.”

    Lena felt her heart squeeze in her chest as she began to feel panicked. Whatever Kara had to tell her must be serious, but Lena couldn't imagine what it might be. Kara stood still, seeming to have lost her will to speak, trembling as she refused to meet Lena's gaze.

    “Kara, darling, what is it?” Lena asked, trying to keep her tone as reassuring as possible as she began dreading what might've caused her girlfriend to have such a visceral reaction.

    Kara walked slowly back to Lena, drawn in inexorably by her. When she reached the couch, she sat down heavily before gazing up at Lena.

    Lena wiped Kara's tears away, pulling her into a gentle hug, “It's okay, darling. It's okay. Whatever it is, we can deal with it.”

    “I'm-” Kara started, pulling back from the hug before taking a deep, shuddering breath and meeting Lena's eyes, “I'm an alien.”

    Lena was momentarily too stunned to speak. Whatever she had been expecting, it certainly hadn't been that.

    Taking Lena's shocked silence as confirmation of her fears, Kara quickly began pleading with Lena as more tears streamed down her face, “Please, Lena. Please don't be mad. I'm sorry, it has nothing to do with you, I-”

    Lena, finally regaining her composure, broke Kara off, “Hey, no. It's okay. I'm not mad.”

    “You- you're not?” Kara asked through a small hiccupped sob.

    “No, darling. I was just surprised,” Lena said truthfully, cupping Kara's face and wiping the tears away with her thumbs, “It doesn't matter to me that you are an alien.”

    “I knew it wouldn't,” Kara said, shaking her head at her overly emotional reaction, “I just- I couldn't help but feel that you would be mad that I hadn't told you sooner, or think it had to do with your family.”

    “You don't owe anyone your secrets, Kara, not even me.”

    “I wanted to tell you, though. I've always wanted to tell you. I want you to know.”

    “Can I ask why you never told me then?” Lena prodded gently.

    “This isn't a conversation that I wanted to have over the phone, Kara replied, “This is important, and I wanted to tell you in person. When we went out to dinner, I wanted to tell you, but I didn't want to spring it on you first thing. I could've revealed it when the mugger tried to rob us, but I didn't want to show you, I wanted to tell you. I could've told you the last few times we were together, but we have such little time together, that I didn't want to ruin it.”

    “Why tell me this time then?”

    “I know you said I don't owe you my secrets, but I owed you this one before we took this next step. It wouldn't be right if I hadn't told you. That, and there are certain things I need to warn you about before we do this.”

    “Oh?” Lena asked, genuinely curious.

    “Mainly, I am a lot stronger than humans, and when I get angry or excited, I can lose control over my strength. I've never done this, and I don't know how well I'll be able to control myself. It's hard enough sometimes when I am kissing you. I'm afraid I'll hurt you if I lose my focus.”

    “I trust you, Kara,” Lena said, “But if you don't want to do this, we don't have to do it today. We can wait until you are ready.”

    “I am ready,” Kara replied, “I just needed you to know.”

    “Well, consider me warned,” Lena said with a sultry smile, “Now take me to bed.”

    It would've been nice to be able to say they had an amazing first time, but neither of them had any experience, so for the most part, it was a fumbling mess. The important thing, however, was that they had enjoyed themselves, awkwardness and all, and they felt closer to one another than they ever had.

    They were lying in Lena's bed, still a few minutes remaining before Kara had to leave, when Lena's mind returned to their earlier conversation. In retrospect, it made sense that Kara was an alien. Kara had never shied from her defense of aliens and never hesitated to denounce those who discriminated against them. Additionally, now there was finally an answer as to how Kara could eat as much as she did and still remain in the dazzlingly good shape Lena had just seen up close and personal.

    “So,” Lena said, her sudden speech causing Kara's arms to tense slightly where they were still wrapped around Lena, “I have questions.”

    “Yes?” Kara replied openly.

    Turning so they were nose-to-nose, Lena momentarily got distracted looking into Kara's eyes, which she had never seen without her glasses, before she pressed on, “Do you even need to wear glasses?”

    “No,” Kara answered, “My vision is actually much much better than a human's. The glasses help tune some of it out so I can focus more easily. I don't really need to wear them anymore, if I don't want to.”

    “Is Kara Danvers your real name?”

    “No, I was born Kara Zor-El of the Great House of El.”

    That sparked something in her brain. She knew she had heard that before, and she had a creeping feeling she knew the answer to her next question before she even asked it.

    “What planet are you from?”

    Lena wasn't entirely sure she wanted to hear Kara's answer. If she was right, this had far greater significance to their relationship than Kara being an alien.

    “Krypton,” Kara replied, looking like she also understood the seriousness of what she was saying on the situation, and the impact that it could have on their lives.

    “Krypton,” Lena repeated, needing a moment to process the magnitude of this revelation, “You're Kryptonian? Like Superman?”

    “Yes. Superman is actually my cousin.”

    It made sense then, Lena thought, why Kara had suggested they keep it a secret. She imagined Superman or Kara's other family would handle the news about as well as Lex if they knew the truth. Still, she couldn't suppress the deep-rooted fear that maybe Kara had never told her because of her family, even though Kara had explicitly stated that was not the case.

    “No, Lena,” Kara said, catching onto her train of thought and doing her best to derail it, “It has nothing to do with your name. You are not your family, and you certainly are not your brother. I trust you. Completely.”

    “But you lied to me that day in the alley. You pretended that you didn't know Superman.”

    “I know, and I'm sorry. I didn't want to, please believe me,“ Kara said earnestly, “It had nothing to do with you being a Luthor. I didn't want to have this conversation then, and I certainly didn't want to bring anything up in front of him.”

    Try as she might, though, Kara couldn't fully ease the doubt that one day she wouldn't be able to look past their families and that someday she would lose that trust. Lena could feel the kernel of doubt take root somewhere behind her heart, but she did her best to ignore it.

    Putting on her best smile, she met Kara's eyes, “I knew you didn't need help with your STEM classes.”

    “No,” Kara agreed, seemingly relieved at the change in topics, “I learned science that hasn't yet been discovered on Earth before I turned 13. Needless to say, the science classes I took here were basic by comparison. You can't blame me for using it as an excuse to talk to you more.”

    “You're the worst,” Lena said, pulling Kara in to kiss the smirk off her face.

    “I am, but you love me anyway.”

    “I do.”

    With that, Kara got up to leave, having already stretched the hour Lois gave her much further than she should have. The hurried nature of her departure brought the precariousness of their situation crashing back into focus, and Lena couldn't help but wonder where this revelation would land them.

    “Well, we definitely can't tell anyone now,” Lena said, trying to gauge Kara's reaction.

    Something in her tone caused Kara to turn back. Crossing the space to where Lena was still lying in bed, she crouched down so she could look into Lena's eyes, her hands coming up to cup Lena's cheeks.

    “Lena, as long as I have you, none of that matters. I love you, and that is what's important.”

    Despite Kara's reassuring words, Lena felt another trickle of doubt that Kara would ever choose Lena over her family if it came to that, but she did her best to push it out of her mind.

    Chapter End Notes

    So, there you have it. Kara and Lena are dating, and they've decided not to tell their families. I do so wonder what they will get up to in the next three years before chapter one takes place. Anything could happen, I suppose, but I image you all have a good idea where this is going. Feel free to leave your thoughts about this turn of events in the comments and any predictions for the next chapter, or what you think might be coming beyond, and I'll see you all this upcoming weekend with the next chapter.

    Anyway, I hope you all liked this chapter as much as the previous ones. A few thoughts about this one, that internal monologue Kara was having while at dinner with Kara is sort of my go-to headcanon for what Kryptonian culture was like, and this will come up later and is actually a pretty important part of Kara's thought process throughout the whole fic. I developed this idea from the original stance that Kara tends to take in this fic, but it this addition took that original idea and made it so much better.

    Secondly, I think this may be something that only I really appreciate, but it's been said that people in fics/movies/TV, or whichever media format tend to sort of skip the beginning of relationships, especially when there is previous tension. I could be wrong about this, but I think that getting into a relationship, then immediately starting to make out (or going straight to the bedroom) isn't quite the norm (not that there is anything wrong with it, I just think that media portrays it as overly common). Now, reading this, you might be confused then why I had Kara and Lena do exactly this, and therein lies the secret joke I find so funny. I had Kara do this because she learned most of her cultural norms not from gradual exposure to real life over time, but almost exclusively by consuming massive amounts of media while she quickly acclimitized to earth, so she thinks this is the norm when it probably isn't (again, maybe I'm just imaging things, who knows).

    I've talked about this with some of my friends before, the reasonability of Lena having multiple doctorates by such a young age, but I've decided that I like it. The justification I settled on for explaining why Lena might choose to do such a thing is so that she can stay away from Luthor Corp, and as such regularly interacting with Lex and Lillian for just a little while longer.

    Chapter 3

    Chapter Notes

    So, this post is coming later than I'd like (I wanted to get it up yesterday but life lifed) and sorry if anyone got an erronious notifications last night, I realized I had made an error with the chapter notes and I had to fix it (I'm not sure how the notifications work for fixes without new chapters, so if you did get one or two notifications sorry). Anyway, We are back with chapter 3! This is the second round of flashbacks and covers the last parts of what happened pre-canon and the setup, after this we will be into the story moving forward from chapter 1. Just a reminder, the times are calculated from the end of Chapter 1, and Chapter 4 will be taking place two or so months after that.

    As I've mentioned, this is actually the longest chapter in the whole fic, at around 17.5k, so strap in and enjoy. There is a bit of exposition, especially in the first part here, but I promise most of it will be relevant later (some of it is just headcanons that are not super duper relevant, but what good is fanfiction if you can't just throw your headcanons into everywhere even remotely relevant). If you want something to look out for this chapter, I am at least 90% sure I invented a word at some point, so keep an eye out for that. There is also an ATLA reference

    I don't know that I mentioned this before, though it might have been clear from last chapter, but when someone speaks Kryptonese, their speech is in italics. When it comes to single words or short phrases, most of those are just emphasis, but a few might be Kryptonese, though interpreting it one way or the other shouldn't change anything (it hopefully will be clear from the context)

    2 years, 4 months ago

    It had been a year, to the day, since Kara told her that she was an alien, and it was one of the best and worst things that had ever happened to Lena. On one hand, now that her secret was out, Kara could come to see Lena much more often. She didn't have to worry about justifying how she got there to Lena or explaining to Clark where she was constantly running off to. She could just fly straight from National City into Lena's arms, and as a result, they got to see each other more frequently and had grown ever closer.

    On the other hand, her brother was an alien-hating madman. It had taken her longer to see than it should have, and it hurt more than she would ever likely admit, but now she was constantly worried about what would happen if Lex found out Kara was an alien, and that they were together. After all, he hated Superman, and all Kryptonians by extension, even more so than normal aliens. She could only imagine how he would react if he knew his sister was dating one.

    But, as much as she still loved Lex, she loved Kara more. So while it hurt that the brother she knew was gone, it was the truth. Maybe if it weren't for Kara, she would have tried harder to reach her brother, doubtful as it was that the man she knew still existed, but she couldn't risk Lex catching on.

    Between her family and Kara, she knew who she would choose, even if someday when faced with the same choice, Kara might not do the same for her.

    The only upside to Lex's obsession was, so long as Lena didn't pique his interest, disagree too vehemently with him, and pretended his xenophobia didn't make her sick, he more or less left her alone. As long as she stayed in Metropolis and Kara was discreet, they had nothing to worry about. It wasn't perfect, but as long as she had Kara in her life, she would take it. She would take Kara any way she could have her.

    As if her thoughts had summoned the Kryptonian, Kara dropped silently down on her balcony. Lena immediately felt her tension and disquiet dissipate, as it always did when Kara was near. She hadn't been expecting Kara for a few days more, until after Christmas. Her girlfriend had been rather busy lately, due to her new job as Cat Grant's assistant, but Lena wasn't going to question it, and she certainly wasn't going to complain about seeing Kara sooner.

    “Kara,” Lena said with a smile, “I wasn't-”

    Lena broke off as Kara was suddenly standing before her, a gust of wind blowing across her face and leaving her slightly shocked. She knew that Kara had all the same powers as Superman, but besides her flight, Lena had never seen her use any of them.

    Without any fanfare, Kara dropped to one knee as she pulled something out of her pocket.

    “Marry me,” she said, almost as if in greeting.

    If Lena had been shocked by Kara's abrupt appearance and superspeed, it was nothing compared to how she felt now.

    Kara waited a moment to see if Lena was going to respond before continuing, “I know that we are still young, and that this is sudden, but I was going to ask you this weekend anyway. I was trying so hard to think of some romantic way to propose, but then I realized I didn't want to wait any longer. I love you, Lena. I love you so much, and I don't want to spend a moment longer not being your wife than I have to, so I stopped waiting. I came straight here to ask. Lena Luthor, will you marry me?”

    Before Kara could say anything else, Lena had thrown herself into her arms.

    “Yes,” Lena said before pressing their lips together.

    Eventually, Lena pulled back, awkwardly getting her feet back under her from where Kara had caught her. Wiping gently at her eyes, she looked at the velvet box Kara was still holding, but instead of a ring, there was a bracelet nestled into the soft material.

    “It's a Kryptonian Wedding Bracelet,” Kara explained, “They function on Krypton as engagement rings do on Earth.”

    Rather than respond, Lena held out her left wrist to Kara.

    “You have to understand what you are agreeing to,” Kara said gently as she took out the bracelet from the box, “For Kryptonians, for me, marriage is forever. If you agree, there can never be anyone else for me. Are you ready to make that commitment?”

    Without lowering her wrist, she met Kara's eyes and said with unwavering conviction, “Forever sounds perfect.”

    The smile Kara gave her as she slid the bracelet on her wrist warmed Lena more than the sun ever had.

    As it turned out, Kara had also brought a second bracelet for herself, and Lena had barely finished slipping it on her wrist before dragging her to the bedroom.

    After pushing Kara down onto the bed, Lena had flicked off the lights before hitting the hidden switch she had just installed. Instantly, the room had been bathed in red light. Behind her, Kara inhaled sharply.

    “I can turn them off,” Lena quickly offered.

    As she spoke, Lena turned back to face Kara, unsure of what she would find, but as Kara looked up from where she had been staring at her own hands in awe, she saw a fire she had never seen in Kara's eyes before.

    “No,” Kara replied, her voice thick with emotion as she beckoned for Lena to come closer.

    When Lena was close enough, Kara reached out for her. She ran her hands gently, reverently, over Lena's face, tracing every angle and line with her fingers, then her eyes, as she took in her beloved in the light of Rao for the first time.

    “You would have looked so beautiful on Krypton,” Kara whispered, tucking a strand of Lena's hair behind her ears as tears streamed down her face.

    As Lena took Kara in, she found herself with a similar sentiment. Kara had always looked beautiful on earth, but under the red light, she looked luminous, her beauty ethereal, unlike anything Lena had ever seen before. On Krypton, Kara truly would've been a sight to behold.

    Tentatively, Kara wrapped her arms around Lena, squeezing gently, barely hard enough to register. She looked into Lena's eyes, scanning for any signs of discomfort, before she slowly started squeezing harder. Tighter and tighter Kara hugged her, still constantly scanning Lena for any signs of discomfort, until she finally seemed satisfied that she wouldn't hurt her.

    Burying her head in Lena's neck, she let out a sob of relief as she continued to hold Lena tightly. Even now, Lena knew, she wasn't using her full strength, which would still be greater than that of a human, but Kara didn't have to constantly hold herself back for fear of hurting Lena, spending every moment balanced on a knife's edge of control.

    That was, after all, why Lena had developed the red sun lamps. She knew that, even after years and years of practice, while Kara was easily able to subconsciously regulate her powers to avoid hurting or breaking any and everything around her, Kara would never fully trust herself with Lena, always holding back more than necessary, despite Lena's insistence that she trusted Kara to not lose control and hurt her.

    “Thank you,” Kara whispered hoarsely into Lena's neck.

    “Of course, Darling,” Lena said, gently stroking her hand through Kara's hair, “You can let yourself go now.”

    As if she had only been waiting for permission, the moment the words had left Lena's lips, Kara was on top of her, pinning her hands above her head, and pushing her into the bed with a forceful kiss.

    Later, after they had finished thoroughly enjoying the sun lamps, Kara and Lena were lying in bed, Lena still tightly wrapped in Kara's arms. In her haste to get her and Kara to the bedroom, Lena hadn't taken more than a moment to admire the bracelet Kara had placed on her wrist, but now that her thirst for Kara had been momentarily satiated, she took the time to examine it.

    It looked almost like silver, and although the coloring wasn't quite right to convince Lena of its purity, it was stunning to look at nonetheless. More notably, however, the surface had remained completely unscuffed by their previous activity. Curious, Lena reached out a sticky finger and touched the surface, only to find that her touch had left no residue behind.

    The unusual property of the bracelet quickly brought her scientific curiosity to the surface, and she began fiddling with the bracelet. To her great surprise, the bracelet seemed to fit just as snuggly anywhere she moved it on her arm, seamlessly reshaping itself to maintain the perfect fit.

    Distracted as she was, she hadn't noticed that Kara had pulled back slightly to watch her play with the bracelet. Finally, Lena finished her fiddling, before turning to Kara to request an explanation, only to be met with a look of such pure love and adoration that she lost her train of thought.

    “Do you like it?” Kara asked tentatively.

    “It's beautiful, Kara,“ Lena said earnestly, “I love it.”

    “I'm glad you think so, but that‘s only the shell,” Kara replied.

    “What do you mean?” Lena asked, her brows furrowing as her curiosity returned tenfold.

    “Well,” Kara explained, sitting up and taking Lena's hand into her lap as she did so, “If anyone who knew anything about Kryptonian customs, like Alex, saw the real bracelet, they would've immediately known what it meant. I decided that it would be a good idea to make it look like a regular bracelet, so it could go unquestioned.”

    As she spoke, Kara gently ran her thumb over the bracelet. To Lena's awe and amazement, the silver surface of the bracelet transformed before her eyes, melting back into the bracelet and revealing its true form. The metal it was made of was unlike anything Lena had ever seen. It was iridescent and seemed to shimmer with light, illuminating the relatively dim bedroom. It was as if Kara had taken a drop of the sun and forged it into the metal.

    Lena knew at once, even as she continued to marvel at the bracelet, that Kara had been right. The metal it was made of was unlike anything on Earth, or even anything Lena had seen that other aliens had brought to Earth. Even more obvious, however, was the engravings across the surface, proudly displaying Kara's house crest interspaced with other writing in what Lena could only assume was Kryptonese.

    “What does it say?” Lena asked breathlessly.

    “Well, this is a Kryptonian Glyph, the symbol of my house,” Kara said, indicating the familiar symbol before pointing at the engraving next to it, “These are the words of my house, what the glyph stands for; El mayarah. It means stronger together. The other two words are something I chose, the Kryptonese words for love and eternity.”

    “Read them to me,” Lena requested in a whisper, having only heard Kara say two words in Kryptonese and already in love with the way it sounded coming off her tongue.

    As Kara read the words, Lena could see that speaking her own language again, even briefly, had made Kara glow with happiness, and Lena was overwhelmed with the urge to immerse herself in Kara's culture, if only to make her smile like that again.

    “Does yours do that?” Lena asked, desperately searching for an excuse to hear Kara speak Kryptonese again.

    “Of course,” Kara said with a smile, taking Lena's thumb and running it over her own bracelet, “I made them to match.”

    Sure enough, just like Lena's, at the swipe of her thumb, Kara's bracelet had melted away to be replaced by the near-glowing otherworldly metal. Her disappointment that there was no engraving for Kara to read her distracted her momentarily from what Kara had said, before her mind finally caught up.

    “You made these?” She asked, her awe that Kara had made them herself increasing Lena's appreciation of them even further.

    “I did, I had to sneak off to the Fortress of Solitude to do it, but it was worth it.”

    “How do they work?”

    “They use Kryptonian nanometallurgy. Krypton, as you know, was very technologically and scientifically advanced. They found a way to introduce nanotechnology into the crystalline lattice of the metals so they could manipulate the structure at near-atomic scales. In this case, I used it to allow the metal shell and true form to interchange. It also prevents the metal atoms from engaging in any physical or chemical reactions, so the bracelets will stay unmarred and untarnished forever.”

    “What metals did you use?”

    For a genius, Lena was having a rather hard time formulating her thoughts to ask an intelligent-sounding question. Normally, her brain would be spinning with the possibilities of this new technology, but instead, it was much too fixated on her fiancée to dedicate any thought to anything else.

    “The coating is an Nth metal alloy that I specifically formulated to look like silver, but unlike silver, it is nearly indestructible. Superman and I are the only two beings on the planet that could hope to destroy it. The core metal is an alloy that holds a lot of significance on Krypton.

    “Much of Kryptonian ritual and religion had to do with the sun god, Rao. So, in our most important religious and cultural symbols, we always sought to honor him. We captured his light and infused it into these symbols using a gold-dwarf star alloy. That way, these objects could glow with Rao's light to honor him, just as we do ... did,” she amended sadly, “There are no words for its name in English.”

    Lena to a moment to take in her fiancée. As she had talked, Lena had seen it again, the spark that was in Kara's eyes as she talked about her people. But underneath, there was a sadness in Kara knowing that she would never see them again, that they were truly gone and she was the last true child of Krypton.

    “Tell me more about the nanotechnology,” Lena requested, attempting to pull Kara away from the thoughts of her people.

    Kara, well aware of their shared love of science, gladly indulged the change in topics, “The nanotech can systematically break bonds in the lattice, allowing the two solids to pass by each other in microscopic pieces before being reassembled on the other side.

    “Additionally, it allows for the engravings to show. Both alloys are far too resilient to be engraved by anything on earth, but the nanotech can resize the metal and cause it to take any shape necessary, assuming there is enough metal. That‘s why you could move it up and down your arm and have it still fit perfectly.”

    “How does it know what shape to take?” Lena asked, not wanting to assume anything about this new and unfamiliar technology.

    “The nanotech is evenly spaced throughout the lattice in discrete pieces, but they function as one system, similarly to how individual cells make up a single organism. Any functions or changes you want the bracelet to undergo has to be programmed into the nanotech,” Kara explained with an indulgent smile.

    “What keeps someone from accidentally triggering the transformation?”

    “The bracelets are programmed to recognize only our genetic signatures. No one but us can reveal the true form, and if you do have to take it off for some reason,” Kara said, looking as if the idea of Lena taking off the bracelet pained her greatly, “It will automatically revert to the hidden form.”

    “I never want to take it off,” She reassured Kara, earning a smile before looking down at Kara's unengraved bracelet, “Why doesn't yours say anything?”

    “I chose what yours said,” Kara replied, “It seems only fair that you get to choose what mine says.”

    “I'll let you know, darling,” Lena said, smiling at the thoughtful gesture from Kara.

    The conversation lulled into a pleasant silence, the newly engaged couple content to bask in each other’s presence. Lena's mind, however, soon decided that there was no time to rest and there were pressing issues to be addressed. Rather than guess, however, she thought it best to start figuring out what both she and Kara wanted their wedding to look like.

    “So,” Lena started, shifting onto an elbow from where she had been lying on Kara's chest.

    “Hmmm?” Kara responded, somewhat distracted by Lena's bare chest.

    “I was wondering about the wedding.”

    “What about it?” Kara asked, focusing back on Lena's face with some difficulty.

    “What type of ceremony are we going to have? Obviously we can't invite anyone, but we need someone to officiate it. Do you want to exchange rings? Or another set of bracelets?” Lena rattled off the questions, her excitement to marry Kara overcoming her normally reserved way of speaking.

    “Well, we can't get officially married without leaving a trail, not that the legality matters to me,” Kara said, a smile splitting her face, “so I thought we could do it in the Ancient Kryptonian style. It's really not that different, but it will bind us by Kryptonian tradition. The Kryptonian marriages of old were more of a bonding of two souls than a legal thing, which fits our situation pretty well, I thought.”

    Kara paused to wait for confirmation from Lena, but Lena couldn't find any words to express how much she wanted that with Kara, so she resorted to nodding enthusiastically.

    “Now, for the ceremony itself, you are right, we will need to figure out who could officiate it, but we can worry about that another day,” Kara continued, the mirth Lena felt reflected equally on her face, “As for the rings, I wasn't sure if it was a good idea to get you an engagement ring. Even if you wore it on a different finger, I thought it might attract too much attention.

    “Traditionally, on Krypton, the bracelets act as the engagement ring, then at the wedding, something else was exchanged. Something special, whether a reminder of a cherished memory, or a heartfelt gift. It could be anything, really, so long as it had meaning. Think of it almost like a zeroth anniversary present.

    “My father gave my mother this necklace,” Kara explained, indicating the necklace on her bare chest that Lena had noticed before, nothing more than a blue teardrop-shaped stone set in silvery metal, “On Krypton, this was a symbol of home. It was a promise that they would always find each other and be home, no matter how far apart they were. My mother never took it off until the day Krypton died, when she gave it to me. I've worn it ever since.”

    Lena pulled Kara into a hug, knowing she would need the comfort, waiting for a few heartbeats before giving Kara her answer, “I want all of it. Rings, bracelets, gifts, I want all of it with you. Even if I can't wear it, I want everything. It can be a little bit of human tradition and a little bit of Kryptonian tradition. The more ways I am bound to you, the better.”

    Kara pulled back slightly, but didn't say anything. Instead, she just looked deep into Lena's eyes for a few moments before pulling her into a deep kiss. There wasn't much room for conversation the rest of the night.

    Graciously, Kara's boss Cat had allowed her to have the holidays off. Though Lena suspected that it had more to do with not thinking Kara would be very useful in celebrating Christmas or the New Year with her children than any form of generosity, but she wasn't complaining. Minus a quick detour back to Midvale to spend Christmas with her family, Kara had been in Metropolis ever since she proposed, and it had been the best week of Lena's life.

    Unfortunately, Lex, without an ounce of grace in his body, nor anyone he loved or loved him to distract him through the holidays, hadn't given Lena a choice but to come into work on New Year's Day, lest she arouse his suspicion.

    So, regretfully, Lena found herself stepping into one of Lex's new R&D labs. This lab, however, unbeknownst to Lena, and unlike any of the other labs at Luthor Corp, was completely soundproofed. No sooner had the door swung shut behind her, than her phone began ringing.

    Frowning as she looked at the caller ID, she quickly scanned the lab for Cameras before answering the call, “Darling, are you all right?”

    “Lena! Are you okay? Do you need me to come down there?”

    “What?! No, Kara. I'm fine,” Lena said, completely bewildered by Kara's obvious distress.

    “Oh... Okay. I'll see you when you get home then?”

    The abrupt shift in Kara's tone from worried to embarrassed spiked Lena's curiosity, but she didn't feel comfortable prolonging the conversation any longer than needed while in the same building as her brother, “I've got to go, Kara, but will you tell me about it tonight?”

    “Of course. I love you.”

    “I love you too.”

    After that unusual interruption, Lena did her best to get back to work, but she couldn't focus, wondering what could have prompted Kara to think she was in such danger. Try as she might though, she couldn't make much progress, so once she had done enough to placate Lex for the day, she quickly packed up and headed home.

    Despite her arriving home nearly an hour earlier than she normally did, she found that Kara didn't seem to be remotely surprised to see her.

    “Lena, hi,” Kara greeted awkwardly.

    Lena paused at the tone and gave her fiancée a once over. Kara refused to look at her, instead staring straight at the floor in an attempt to avoid making eye contact and tugging nervously at her bracelet.

    Seeing no need for such behavior, Lena approached her gently, grabbing one of Kara's fidgeting hands and lightly leading her over to the couch so they could both sit down.

    “Darling,” She said soothingly, gathering both of Kara's hands in hers, “Why don't you tell me about it.”

    “Well,” Kara started, her eyes flicking briefly up to meet Lena's before falling to rest on their joined hands as a blush dusted her cheeks, “I- I couldn't hear you.”

    You couldn't hear me?” Lena repeated, her brow crinkling slightly.

    “Your heartbeat,” She clarified.

    Lena knew that Superman had super hearing, so it made sense that Kara did too, but she had no idea that Kara could've heard her heartbeat a city's length away.

    “You listen to my heartbeat?”

    “Always. I don't mean to invade your privacy or anything. I don't even really mean to do it, it just happened one day. I can always hear everything around me within a certain range, but my brain automatically filters the vast majority of it out, otherwise I would never be able to function. If I focus, I can pick out a familiar sound, like Alex's heartbeat, or Eliza's, or yours, but one day it changed. One of the times I visited you, I stopped having to listen for it, it just came to me without any effort. I honestly don't think I could tune it out even if I tried.

    “Then, as I was flying back to National City, I expected it to fade away, but it never did. Usually I would have to really really focus to hear something that far away, but it's like a part of me is completely and wholly dedicated to you now, to making sure you are safe.

    “It doesn't matter where you are, or where I am, I can always hear it, and I've come to rely on it. It keeps me calm when I am angry or nervous. It reassures me that you are okay. After a year of hearing it constantly, I can tell what all the little variations mean. I can tell when you are angry or sad, when you are happy or scared, when you are feeling well rested, or when you are so tired you can barely stand.

    “Most of the time, it is just in the background, like the baseline to the melody of my life, but when it varies, I cannot ignore it. Then today, it suddenly cut out, and I was lost. I didn't know what happened to you. You could've been shot, or abducted, or something, and I just lost it. I'm sorry for scaring you, I was just so worried. I nearly stormed down to Luthor Corp to find you, secret be damned.”

    This time, it was Lena's turn to blush. She didn't quite know how to feel that her fiancée had an emotional barometer that she had never mentioned before, but Lena couldn't possibly be upset at Kara for it, not when it so clearly came from a place of love. It did, however, explain Kara's preternatural ability to know when she was upset and needed someone to talk to, once the time it took the sound to reach her was accounted for.

    “Hey, you have nothing to be sorry for,” She said, taking one of Kara's hands and placing it on her chest, “My heart is yours, and you can listen to it any time.”

    At that, Kara scooped Lena up, pressing their lips together as she carried her to the bedroom.

    The next night, Kara's final night before she had to return to her life in National City, after they were done utilizing the red sun lamps once again, Lena was just enjoying being near Kara, soaking up as much of her fiancée as she could before they were parted again. She was lying so her head was pillowed on Kara's shoulder, her arm snug across her chest, with one leg kicked up over Kara's waist, content to lie there forever.

    Nearly drifting off to sleep, Lena was awoken suddenly when the doorbell rang. Cursing herself, she reluctantly rolled off Kara to get dressed. Anticipating Kara being hungry after the night's activities, she had preemptively ordered food for when she knew they would be done. What she hadn't anticipated, however, was that the arrival of food meant she would have to get out of bed when she was that comfortable.

    “Who's that?” Kara said, tensing as Lena crawled off of her and peering at the wall as if she was looking straight through it.

    She probably was, Lena realized. She hadn't told Kara she had ordered anything, wanting to surprise her fiancée, but the unannounced late-night visitor had undoubtedly spooked her.

    “I ordered food. I'm sorry I didn't tell you. I wanted it to be a surprise.”

    “Oh. I thought...” Kara trailed off as her body relaxed and her brow unfurrowed, “Never mind.”

    Hopping down off the bed, Lena quickly finished redressing in the scattered clothes Kara had nearly torn off her earlier and went to greet the delivery person. As she walked back into the living room, food in hand, she saw that Kara had also redressed and was looking happy at the prospect of eating. Much as getting out of bed had displeased her, the look on Kara's face was well worth it.

    “You ordered Pizza!!” Kara said, as if this was the greatest thing that ever happened to her, “Rao, I love you.”

    As Lena finished her first slice and started on her second, she turned away from the one-woman pizza demolition machine that was her fiancée and flicked on the TV. As the news appeared on the screen, she put down her second slice of pizza. She wasn't hungry anymore.

    Kara, detecting something was wrong, put her slice down as well, first looking at Lena, then tracking Lena's attention to the screen. Neither of them said anything, and neither of them needed to. Kara just pulled Lena close and held her as she cried.

    There had been an incident in California. Someone had set off a device designed to create an earthquake deep inside a fault line. Tens of thousands had been injured, hundreds were still missing, and a few dozen had already been confirmed dead. There was no evidence as to who had done it, but Kara knew, and so did Lena. Her brother had just murdered dozens, likely hundreds of people. The only consolation, slim as it seemed, is that Superman had managed to stop the device before it tore the state in half and killed tens of millions of people.

    Lena had come to peace with the fact that her loving brother was gone, that he had descended into his xenophobia and would never come back, but she would never have believed him capable of this. Now she knew better. Her brother was a monster, just like Lionel had been in the end.

    So, for over an hour, Kara held Lena as she sobbed, cooing soft reassurances, but Lena felt like she had never deserved anything less.

    When Lena finally felt strong enough to pull away, she found an oddly guilty look on Kara's face.

    “What is it, darling?” Lena asked, her voice croaky.

    “We don't have to talk about it right now, Lena. We can just sleep and cuddle if you want.”

    Lena might have acquiesced, if not for the fact that she could feel Kara playing with her bracelet, much the same way she had been doing with her own the previous day.

    Taking a moment to master her emotions, she looked Kara in the eyes and said, “It's okay, we can talk about it.”

    “I just feel so upset, and not for the reason I should be. I mean, a lot of people died, and that sucks, but the only thing I can think about is the fact that this is just another thing that my family is going to blame you for, when you had nothing to do with it. How messed up is that,” She said dejectedly.

    After a few moments of commiseratory silence, she continued, “Why can't they just understand that you have nothing to do with it? Besides this one thing, my life is better than it ever has been since I landed on Earth. I have an amazing fiancée, and I just want to be able to tell my sister about it.”

    As Kara ranted, she stood up and turned away from Lena to look over the city. Of course, while Lena had known that Kara was like Superman, this was the first time she had felt it. Ever since Kara had told her she was Kryptonian, she hadn't bothered to put a dampener power when they were together. Without it, Kara radiated a soft and reassuring presence that brushed lightly against her senses.

    Lena had never seen Kara this angry before, however, and the feeling she was giving off had changed, now much more similar to the way Superman felt when he was presenting his power, but from Kara, it felt much more ethereal and otherworldly, and in no way human. If Lena hadn't known that Kara would never hurt her, she might've listened to the primal part of her brain begging her to run.

    As it was, Lena knew this problem was unlikely to go away, so there was no point in getting upset about it. She had let herself grieve the brother she had lost, and his actions, and now she wanted to move on. She had accepted that Kara's family might never accept her, but as long as she had Kara, she couldn't care less.

    Lena got up from where she was still sitting, and crossed over to Kara, wrapping her arms around Kara's middle and leaning her head against Kara's back, feeling her fiancée relax immediately underneath her.

    “I love you,” She said.

    “I love you too,” Kara replied.

    They stood like that for several minutes, letting their emotions flow and their breathing settle before Kara turned to face Lena, “I just wish they saw that your brother's actions aren't yours, that you can be trusted.”

    “I know darling, I wish that were true too, but as long as I have you, I can bear anything.”

    “You're right, as long as I have you, anything is worth it.”

    For the first time since Lena had turned on the TV over an hour ago, a small smile split Lena's face. Apparently, however, tonight was not meant to be a happy one, for as soon as Kara had put a smile on her face, she took it right back off again.

    “I wish I didn't have to take my bracelet off, though,” She said regretfully.

    “What!?” Lena asked a little sharply, taking a displeased step away at the notion of Kara removing her bracelet, “I thought the shell meant that you didn't have to take it off?”

    “That was so you don't have to take it off. It doesn't matter what mine looks like, Clark and Alex both know I would never wear a bracelet unless I found the person I wanted to spend the rest of my life with. If it weren't for that, I would never take it off, never.”

    Kara truly looked as if the thought deeply pained her, but Lena was having trouble focusing on her facial expression when she was still trying to digest what she had said. Why on earth would Clark Kent possibly know anything about Kryptonian wedding traditions? There was only really one explanation Lena could think of.

    “What?” Lena said, not wanting to jump to conclusions.

    “I don't want to take it off, I swear-” Kara started repeating before Lena cut her off.

    “Why would Clark Kent know anything about Kryptonian wedding bracelets?”

    Lena was pretty sure that she knew the answer, but the dumbstruck look on Kara's face was more than enough to confirm her suspicions.

    “What? I- I don't know what you are talking about.” Kara spluttered, brows furrowing.

    While she did a pretty good job of pretending, Lena knew her fiancée, and she knew she was lying. Now she was angry, her fists clenching at her sides. She could care less about Clark being Superman, in fact, it made a lot of sense, but Kara lying to her face was making her see red.

    “You're lying, Kara,” She accused in a dangerous tone.

    “I'm sorry, Lena,” Kara repented, not even trying to deny it, “Can we please not talk about this? I shouldn't have told you that.”

    Of course.

    Of course that was what this was about. Lena should have known better than to ever assume that Kara would have chosen her over her family. She should have known that eventually, Kara wouldn't have been able to see past everything that her brother had done and would stop trusting her. She should have known that Kara wouldn't have been able to see past her name forever.

    All at once, her every insecurity and every negative thought came crashing down around her. Every fear she had ever had since she learned that Kara was a Kryptonian had come to pass. She couldn't breathe. She couldn't think past the anger and grief warring in her head.

    “Lena-” Kara tried, clearly picking up on her distress, but Lena cut her off.

    “Don't,” She spat, her harsh tone causing tears to prick in Kara's eyes.

    She couldn't deal with the grief that was building in her chest at the inevitable end of the only good thing in her life, so she let her anger take over, lashing out and pushing people away the way she always did when she was upset.

    “Does this even mean anything to you?” Lena demanded, raising the arm with her bracelet on it, “Because on earth, you aren't supposed to lie to the people you claim to love.”

    A look of devastation flashed across Kara's face, but Lena was too far gone to care. Kara took a small step toward Lena and tentatively reached for her, but Lena turned and stalked away from her. When she turned back to face Kara, she found the distance between them halved, but Kara seemed unable to bridge the rest of the gap.

    Kara stood there, looking desolate as tears streamed silently down her face. If Lena's pain and anger hadn't been clouding her thoughts, she might have realized that the only other times she saw Kara like this was whenever Kara had told Lena even the barest details of her life before earth, but Lena was too consumed by her own emotions to care.

    “You lied to me,” Lena hurled the words across the space between them like a stone, throwing the pain Kara had caused back at her, striking her like a physical blow, before slowly closing the distance between them so she could give the blade in her heart the final twist so Kara would see what she had done, face-to-face, “Because you don't trust me.”

    “Lena, that's not true,” Kara managed through her silent sobs.

    The cruelty of the denial was too much for Lena to handle. Though she knew it was pointless, she planted both of her hands on Kara's chest and pushed with as much anger-fueled strength as she could muster.

    To her surprise, however, the push had sent Kara stumbling back until she fell over. Lena knew she hadn't taken her by surprise, but instead, Kara had let herself be pushed, and in fact hadn't even used an ounce of her immense strength to stop Lena.

    The realization snapped her out of her stupor, and she felt her anger drain away as she looked down at Kara, looking tearfully back up at her. Neither of them spoke for a few moments, and Kara made no effort to get up off the floor.

    Finally, Kara spoke, her voice still constricted with tears, “Please, Lena. It has nothing to do with me not trusting you. It wasn't my secret to tell.”

    “It has nothing to do with my family?” Lena pressed, needing the reassurance.

    “No, Lena. I have never judged you for your family, and I never will. I'm sorry that I tried to lie to you, but I didn't realize what I had said, and I panicked trying to cover it up.”

    Lena walked over to where Kara had fallen and sat down beside her, “I'm sorry I got so angry. I am just always so scared that you will treat me like everyone else one day, and that you'll stop trusting me.”

    “That will never happen, Lena. Never,” Kara emphasized, before taking Lena's braceleted wrist in her hand, “And this means everything to me. Please don't doubt that.”

    “I'm so sorry, Kara. I didn't doubt it. I was lashing out. I was hurting so badly that I wanted to make you hurt too. I'm so, so sorry,” Lena said, ashamed at her behavior, at the venom she had unleashed on Kara, tears running down her cheeks at what she had said and done.

    “Hey, it's okay,” Kara said, pulling Lena into her arms and pressing a gentle kiss to Lena's forehead.

    She had done what she always did, just what the Luthors had trained her to do. Lillian would have been proud, and for the second time tonight, Lena felt completely unworthy of Kara's affection.

    “Don't think that,” Kara chastised, eliciting a look of surprise on Lena's face.

    “Don't give me that look, Lena. I know you, and I know exactly what you are thinking, and it isn't true. You deserve the world.”

    Kara leaned down and connected their lips, resulting in a rather tear-filled kiss on both their parts.

    When they finally pulled apart, Lena looked up into Kara's eyes, “I won't tell anyone,” She swore.

    “I know you won't,” Kara said, meaningfully meeting Lena's gaze, “because I trust you.”

    2 years ago

    In the end, they decided to go to Midvale for the wedding. The list of people who knew about Kara and could officiate a Kryptonian wedding was pretty slim. Obviously, both Alex and Kal could've done it, but they were out for equally obvious reasons, which left only two choices: Lois and Eliza.

    Between the two of them, while Lois already knew, and likely would have agreed, the pair had chosen Eliza to do it instead. For one, it didn't feel right to put Lois between them and Clark and risk straining their relationship. Additionally, Lena thought it would be nice for Kara to have at least one member of her immediate family that she could share their relationship with.

    So, a week before they planned to be married, Kara flew them both to Midvale to give them enough time to get everything set up. Kara set them down behind the house, near the beach and out of view of the street.

    As she set Lena down, her fiancée removed the bracelet from her right wrist, where she had worn it ever since Kara had placed it there the morning she had left Metropolis after their argument.

    Taking the bracelet from Lena, she put it back on her own wrist, and immediately felt better. Though she had barely had more than a week to get used to wearing it, no amount of time could make its absence feel normal again. When she didn't have it, it felt almost as if she was missing a part of herself, and she could only feel complete as the metal once again wrapped around her wrist.

    After taking a moment to enjoy the sensation, she opened her eyes and met Lena's loving gaze, “Stay here. I want to go talk to her before you come in. I need to make sure she won't tell anyone before I introduce you.”

    “Okay, darling. I love you,” Lena said, more than content to let Kara take the lead when it came to her own family.

    “I love you too,” Kara said, noticing that Lena seemed a bit cold from the flight over and slipping off her jacket so she could wrap it around Lena's shoulders before she went inside, “I'll call for you when we are ready.”

    With a parting kiss to Lena's forehead, Kara turned and walked up to the house. Taking a brief moment to make sure her bracelet was tucked into her sleeve, she made her way into the house, the back door creaking slightly as she opened it, startling Eliza in one of the other rooms.

    “Eliza, it's me,” she called out.

    “Oh, Kara, you scared me,” Eliza said, appearing in the doorway to the living room, “I didn't know you were coming.”

    “I'm sorry. I would have called ahead, but this was kind of last minute.”

    “Oh, well I'm happy you are here. I love when you visit,” Eliza said, walking forward and wrapping Kara in a hug she sank into.

    “How did you get here, anyway?” Eliza asked, and at Kara's silence, she squinted her eyes before continuing, “You flew, didn't you.”

    Kara didn't want to see Eliza's disapproving look. She knew that Eliza was only concerned for her safety, but she really didn't want to get into it at the moment.

    Leading them to one of the couches, she spoke as they sat down, “I know you don't like it, but there is something important I needed to discuss with you, it couldn't wait.”

    “I understand, sweetie,” Eliza said a bit more gently, knowing that Kara could be impatient when things were important to her.

    “I need to tell you something, but you have to promise me you won't tell anyone.”

    “Of course,” Eliza responded with the ease of someone who spent years dealing with the secrets of teenage girls, not quite grasping the seriousness with which Kara spoke.

    “I'm serious, Eliza,” Kara said, trying not to get frustrated at Eliza misinterpreting the weight with which she spoke, “This is not some silly secret to me. This is important.”

    Eliza was a bit taken aback by Kara's tone. Never in all the years since she had adopted her had Kara once spoken so fiercely to her.

    Before she could overcome her surprise to respond, Kara went on, “If you agree, it will be hard, but when I say you can't tell anyone, I mean anyone. I don't care who it is. You can't tell Alex, if the president herself walked in, you couldn't tell her. If Jeremiah somehow came back to life, you couldn't tell him either.”

    As Kara finished, Eliza's surprise compounded. She couldn't imagine what Kara might take so seriously that she would invoke Jeremiah's name.

    “Kara, I-” She started uncertainly, before deciding to address the only likely of the three options, “I don't know if I feel comfortable having to lie to Alex.”

    “I understand that, and if this isn't something that you think you can handle, I won't be upset, but if you don't promise, I can't tell you.”

    What Eliza had said was true, she didn't want to make a promise that meant she might have to lie to Alex, but Alex wasn't her only daughter, and it was clear from the intensity with which she spoke that whatever this was it was extremely important to Kara.

    “Okay,” Eliza eventually agreed, “I won't tell anyone.”

    Apparently, this still wasn't quite good enough for Kara, because she pinned her with an intense look and said, “Swear it.”

    “I swear it,” Eliza replied, meeting Kara's eyes firmly.

    Finally, Kara relaxed, “Good, that's good.”

    “So, what is this important secret?” Eliza asked after a moment, curious what could elicit such intensity from Kara.

    “Right,” Kara said, coming too from the impassioned state that she had been in before turning to look over her shoulder, back towards the door she had come through and calling out, “Lena!”

    Eliza's brow furrowed, because surely Kara couldn't be calling for who Eliza thought she was, but today, it seemed, was a day of surprises, for none other than Lena Luthor was now walking into her house, shooting her a nervous look before smiling at Kara.

    “What-” Eliza started, trying to figure out why Kara had brought the sister of her cousin's greatest enemy to meet her, but before she could manage more than a single word, she saw something that made her stop dead.

    As Lena approached, Kara reached out a hand for her, causing her sleeve to slip down her arm, revealing a bracelet. Kara was wearing a bracelet. Eliza had thought that she couldn't have been any more surprised today. She had been wrong. Very wrong.

    After staring at the bracelet on Kara's arm dumbly for nearly a minute, she looked up to meet Kara's expectant eyes.

    “We're getting married,” Kara needlessly informed her.

    Eliza still wasn't capable of speech, but her eyes moved from Kara to Lena in question.

    “She isn't like her family,” Kara immediately flamed up, “She has nothing to do with what is between Kal and Lex.”

    Kara said it with the same impassioned and heated voice with which she had made Eliza swear to keep the secret, and finally, the day's events all clicked into place. She knew what everyone was saying about Lena, how she must've been in league with her brother, and also knew how fiercely Kara defended the people she loved, and if the way Kara had lit up when Lena walked in was any indication, Kara loved her very deeply.

    “Okay,” Eliza said simply.

    “Okay?” Lena questioned, finally breaking into the conversation, and looking as if she found Eliza's simple acceptance hard to believe.

    “Yes,” Eliza said, “If Kara trusts you, then I trust you too.”

    “It's that simple?” Lena asked in disbelief.

    “Yes,” Eliza said as Kara beamed at her from her position beside Lena.

    The whole interaction had made Eliza rather sad. She could tell that Lena clearly believed that Kara's family could never accept her, and really she couldn't blame her. If there was one trait that Kara and Alex shared, it was the defense of those they loved with a fierceness that could never be contained. If Alex had found out about this, she would never have accepted it, for she could never accept Lena.

    Eliza, however, could see even in the brief moments with which she had known Lena, how heavily her brother's actions had burdened her, and how she had been conditioned to expect to be judged for them at every turn. All in all, it made perfect sense that Kara would swear her so strictly to secrecy. If Alex, or Kal for that matter, had ever caught wind of their relationship, it would be nothing short of catastrophic.

    “So, how can I help you ladies?” Eliza eventually inquired after she had finished assimilating this new information.

    “Well,” Kara started, sharing a loving look with Lena, “As I said, we are getting married, and we decided it was best if we do a more Kryptonian-style ceremony. We were hoping you would officiate it for us.”

    Eliza felt tears spring up in her eyes. She was honored that Kara and Lena wanted her to officiate their wedding.

    “It would be my pleasure.”

    In the week leading up to the ceremony, Lena had spent a lot of time planning with Eliza, and it gave her something she hadn't even noticed she was missing. Something that she hadn't had since she was four, and something that Lillian had certainly never once provided her; a mother.

    Eliza had been the mother that she had never let herself admit that she wanted. Spending the week going over plans with Kara and Eliza, curling up on the couch and watching Jeopardy as she sipped tea, going for long walks on the beach with her soon-to-be wife, all gave Lena such a feeling of family that it made her content in a way she hadn't thought possible.

    For the ceremony, they had decided on the secluded stretch of beach behind Eliza's house where they were unlikely to be interrupted.

    Since they weren't having a traditional human wedding, Kara had argued that they needn't get ready separately, but Lena had insisted. Eliza had flitted between the two women all afternoon helping them get ready. Kara had finished first, and so she had headed out to the beach by herself, much to her own chagrin.

    Just as Lena was finishing up the last touches of her makeup, she heard the door open again behind her. Looking up, she saw it was Eliza coming to check on her again.

    “You almost ready?” The older woman asked.

    “I’m ready, I'm just having a hard time believing this is real,” Lena replied honestly, “Some days, I just don't understand why Kara chose this path, with the secrets and lying, and with someone that most of her family can't wait to condemn.”

    "I can't tell you why Kara chose you, Lena,” Eliza replied, examining the face of the young woman before her as if her daughter's motivations could be gleaned within its depths, “But I can tell you that she did, and that there is nothing she would not do for you. To be loved by Kara is to have someone who is always on your side and who will always protect you, no matter the cost.

    “And, I can tell you that Kara loves you more than anything I have ever known her to love. Never once, not in all the years I have known her, has Kara ever spoken as fiercely to me as she did when she made me swear never to tell anyone about you. Make no mistake, Kara is an excellent judge of character. She believes you are worthy of her love, and she is right, it has only ever been you that doubted it.”

    Normally, someone being able to see through her as Eliza just had would have terrified her, but this time, in this moment, it had the opposite effect. Meeting Eliza's eyes, she smiled faintly and nodded, trying her best not to let any tears fall and ruin her makeup, before standing up and following her out of the room.

    When they reached the back door, Eliza went out ahead to the beach where Kara was waiting while Lena stayed behind, waiting for the sun to begin setting, when they had agreed the ceremony would begin. It was one of the few things that Kara had insisted upon, not that Lena was apt to deny her anything, because it was the time of day that reminded her the most of Krypton.

    As Lena waited, she looked at her dress one last time. It was sleek, fitting over the top half of her body like a second skin, flowing off her shoulders down her arms and torso, before falling loosely from her hips. It was elegant, but simply so.

    It wasn't the dress that she might have picked for herself when she was a kid dreaming of her wedding, but she didn't care. As long as she married Kara today, she could wear a burlap sack as a dress and still be the happiest person on the planet.

    As the first oranges and reds of the sunset swept across the sky, Lena descended the steps to the beach behind the house, heading to where Kara and Eliza were waiting. With nothing pressing at home, and her powers still far from fully recovered from her solar flare, she had decided to visit Kal in Metropolis, where his feud with Lex was winding down to its final hours.

    Although both Kal and Alex had expressly forbidden her from engaging with Lex in any way, they hadn't refused her the ability to just be there to lend her support. However, when the sun had turned red and with both Kal and Lois out, Kara had decided to walk to Lena's apartment, hoping to at least be able to offer Lena some comfort.

    What she found when she arrived, however, made her feel sick. There had clearly been a struggle. The door was hanging off a single hinge, some of the furniture had been overturned, and there was glass all over the floor. It must have happened after the sun turned red, otherwise, she would have heard it, but the fact remained that her wife had been taken while she was in the same city, and she hadn't been able to do a single thing about it.

    If she hadn't so recently overexerted herself, or if she had more time to grow her powers, she might've had enough residual energy to be able to do something. Even if she had arrived earlier, even powered only by a red sun, she would've had more than enough strength to take out a few human thugs, but as it was, she was neither powerful enough to prevent Lena from being taken, nor to rescue her.

    She still had a slim fraction of her power and speed left, and her senses still far surpassed those of a human, but most of her more advanced powers were beyond her. All in all, she was a shell of her normal self, and no matter how hard she strained, she couldn't even pick out a single beat of Lena's heart over the chaos of the city, and without it, she felt lost. She didn't know if she would rather collapse into tears, or find Lex and punch him into space.

    Useless, that's what she was. There was nothing she could do but sit back and wait, pacing back and forth endlessly, minute upon minute, hour upon hour, crying until she had no tears left, screaming until only a sliver of her voice remained, wondering if she would ever be the same after the day was done.

    Forever, that's as long as it took. People lived their entire lives, children grew old and died in those long hours. Civilizations rose and fell, stars were born and burned out, endless universes came into being and winked out of existence as Kara waited.

    Without the constant that was Lena's heartbeat, Kara's life didn't flow. Time warped around her, losing its cadence and passing her by entirely, a solid rock standing alone against eternity.

    When the first pounding beat reached her ears, relief flooded through her, and she fell to her knees as forever ended and time restarted. A mere minute later, as Lena entered her apartment, it was to see a much-slowed blur moving toward her. Finally in each other's arms again, both women collapsed to the floor, sobs of relief echoing between them, clinging to each other with no intention of letting go any time soon.

    “How?” Lena asked anyway, “How can you trust me after this?”

    The way Lena said it nearly broke her, because Lena herself sounded so broken. If Lena being here hadn't meant that Lex had already been taken care of, Kara was tempted to track him down herself for the pain he was causing Lena in this moment.

    Lena held the knife there, where even the slightest twitch might have disastrous repercussions, while Kara waited patiently, looking up at her wife with unfiltered love and adoration.

    Slowly, Lena relaxed, and the fear of losing Kara left her eyes. It was ironic, Kara supposed, that on one side of the city, a super and a Luthor had battled fiercely, each doing their best to foil the other, while on the opposite side of the city was the other super and Luthor, one willingly offering the other their life as a sign of trust.

Alex, Kara knew, always sought to defend and protect her, so when there was a perceived threat to her emotional or physical well-being, Alex didn't react well. It didn't matter how many times Kara told her she was fine, and that no harm had come to her and she was just rattled, Alex loathed to leave her alone for more than a few minutes at a time.

No matter what she told Alex, she could not get more than half an hour before her sister started wondering after her, making it entirely impossible to visit Lena in Metropolis.

It wasn't known to the general public, and the government had quickly confiscated all the evidence, but Lena knew, and so Kara knew.

She was purposeful in it, slowly and methodically building Lena up until she was trembling around her, holding her there suspended in her near bliss for as long as she could before finally pushing her over the edge.

    As she worked, Kara looked up at her wife, smiling softly to herself as she saw that Lena had completely forgotten about the events of the week, at least temporarily, as she lost herself in her pleasure.

    Later, after Lena had finally tapped out, Kara was lying on Lena's chest, letting the steady thrum of her wife's heart under her cheek lull her to sleep as Lena absently threaded her fingers through her hair.

    Just as Kara was about to fall asleep, Lena finally spoke, “I think I need to leave Metropolis.”

    “Hmmmm?” Kara groaned sleepily in response.

    “Well, Lillian is stepping down from Luthor Corp, which means the position of CEO will fall to me. I think it would be best for me and the company to put some distance between us and Metropolis. The name Luthor in this city will forever be eponymous with Lex and his rampage. They will never trust me.

    “I want to turn Luthor Corp into a force for good, and in order to do that, it needs a fresh start, so I am going to move the headquarters and rebrand to L-Corp. This can also be a fresh start for me. I can get away from the stain of Lex's shadow. I can finally make a name for myself outside of my family.”

    “That's great, Lena,” Kara said sincerely, still trying to fight sleep so she could pay attention, “Were where you thinking of moving?”

    “National City,” Lena responded.

    Kara hummed in acknowledgment, not processing what Lena had said before her eyes snapped open, her smile putting the sun to shame, “Really!! You mean it?! When can you move?”

    “Of course I mean it. There is a large Luthor Corp regional headquarters there, so it should be easy to convert to the full headquarters. Hopefully I can make it in time for our second anniversary, but convenience isn't why I am doing it. Really, I just want to wake up in your arms every day. Plus,” she added with a bit of a smirk, “I've heard that Supergirl lives in National City. It would be good if I could establish a good working relationship with her.”

    “Well,” Kara said, returning her smirk, “I happen to know Supergirl personally. I could introduce you if you wanted. I'm sure you'll get along fabulously.”

    “Thank you, darling,” Lena said against Kara's lips before pressing them together and kissing Kara senseless.

    Chapter End Notes

    So, a few thoughts about this chapter. Firstly, about their little fight they had. I truly hate when Lena calls Kara out as Supergirl, and Kara stumbles and lies directly to her face about it. It may or may not be in character, but I really dislike it. The only reason that Kara did so was because it was Clark's identity, not hers, and it wasn't her secret to tell. As for Lena pushing her, while in real life that would probably be a red flag, I particularly think that Lena only did it as a way to vent her anger and she was under the distinct impression that nothing would happen. I wrote the idea years ago and found it funny (from a certain perspective it is, but it could also be viewed as problematic). Ultimately, I left it in because Kara's reaction to it is part of one of my favorite head canons that I will in fact beat over your heads at every possibility (There is only one time that I am aware of that the head canon is broken, and it was actually in this chapter).

    Anyhow, I've googled quite a bit about what the sky on Krypton might look like, and it is a rather complex thing. I don't think it is unrealistic that it might be red, but I couldn't really find any specific evidence that supports that outside of speculation. However, I've decided to just stick with it as I like it thematically, and I am at least sure that it isn't impossible.

    Yes, you read that right. The day Kara came out as Supergirl was in fact Lena's Birthday, and absolutely nobody can convince me otherwise. In that same scene you may have read about something called The Railometer™. Forgive me for writing that, I think I was delirious when it happened. It's not that it doesn't portray my head canon about Kara having sex when she is excited, because it does (got that from Stay the Night I'm pretty sure), it's just that every time I read it is seems more and more unhinged, but I can't get myself to take it out and replace it with something perhaps a bit more sensible. Anyway, that was the first real smut, and that is how I tend to write it. I don't much care for any of the terminology involved, so I tend to ignore it and focus more on why it is happening and what the characters are feeling more than anything else. Also, I 100% subscribe to the unrealistic fanfiction sex side of smut. If they aren't doing it for hours and having enough orgasms to cause nerve damage they aren't doing it right.

    About Lex, for some reason, I know not why, I was under the impression that he was dead the whole time I was watching the show, up until the time when he was shown to be alive. I decided to stick with this take for this fic because in doesn't really matter (he wouldn't have shown up anyway) and it actually does make the fic better. A lot of what was in these flashbacks might not make sense up front, or at least not seem very relevant, but I assure you that they will come back up later. That being said, there is definitely somethings that I wrote here just because I wanted them, and really no one could stop me.

    Chapter 4

    Chapter Notes

    All righty, here we go. I meant to have this chapter up yesterday, but I was busy all day. I'm not super happy that AO3 is going down the day after I post this, but oh well I know maintenance is important.

    Anywho, on to the chapter itself. This takes place about two months after the end of chapter 1 (which would make it six months after the end of chapter 3). The rest of the story is going to take place throughout season 2, but there are some things that I have just changed completely/removed, there are some things that still happen but are just going to get skipped over, and some things that will happen in slightly different orders/ways than in the show. For example, I just skipped over the intro and integrations of Maggie and Mon-El, because spending time in those scenes wasn't really needed, and I accelerated Maggie and Alex so that it happens in the space between chapter 1 and 4, rather than part way through the fic. At this point, I am assuming that anyone reading this fic has either seen season 2, and or read enough season 2 fics to know the basic ideas of what is coming. If you have any questions about anything that you read in comparison to canon, I will be glad to answer them to the best of my ability.

    Anyway, back to it. Kara and Lena are officially married, and now Lena has finished moving to National City. This chapter is a shorter one, more or less just easing back into the main timeline with a healthy helping of domesticorp. Plot will resume in chapter 5

    It had been an action-packed two months since the two attempts on Lena's life, during which the president had visited and nearly been assassinated and new threats had emerged with CADMUS and Metallo, but Kara would be lying if she said that even after all the distractions, she wasn't still more than a little frustrated that her friends and family where still suspicious of Lena. Especially in light of the close calls on her life.

    That, however, was a problem for a different day. The main objective of tonight was to utterly annihilate everyone at game night.

    The dynamic was a little bit different tonight, seeing as everyone was adjusting to having Alex's new friend Maggie, join the group. Although, if Kara was any judge, the sly glances they kept sharing all night, and the guilty look Alex threw her every once and again said they might've been a little bit more than friends.

    As everyone was leaving, a drunk Mon-El and James supporting an even drunker Winn, Brainy looking lost in thought, his hands steepled together as they often were, and finally Maggie, sharing a significant look with Alex as the latter announced she would stay behind and help her sister clean up.

    Alex and Kara, both knowing that Kara could clean up in no more than a few seconds, looked at each other expectantly.

    “Alex, are you gonna tell me-”

    “How did you know?” Alex asked, looking shocked that her normally clueless sister had managed to pick up on it.

Kara let her, and everyone else, believe that she was as oblivious and bad at lying as she pretended to be as Kara Danvers. Truth was, her mind had been honed on Krypton to be observant by her scientist father. As for the lying, she had indeed once been bad at it, but when she had needed to learn, Lena had more than sufficiently taught her.

I'm glad you have someone besides me, and you don't need to feel bad about enjoying it, even if we spend a little less time together. We will still have game nights, and sister nights, and work. It's not like we will never see each other again.”

She thinks she can accept whoever I choose, but I know she will never accept this.

    "Hello, Kryptonian."

It had been a quiet month since Lena had come back to National City, with only one or two small incidents with an alien fighting ring and a gang of criminals with alien weapons, but both had been easily resolved with Lena's assistance. It seemed, however, the quiet times were coming to an end. At least this woman hadn't just blasted her voice across the whole city at an extremely high pitch like most villains. That was just irritating.

    "CADMUS,” She responded.

    “Got it in one,” The woman responded, a fake congratulation on her voice, “and do you know why we call ourselves that? Cadmus was the very first Greek hero.”

    “You are not a Hero,” Kara replied, scanning the surroundings to see if she could hear when the voice originated.

    “Now, now, Supergirl, no need to be rude. I'm inviting you over. We already have a guest waiting for you,” The voice said in a mocking impression of hospitality, ”your Daxamite friend, but if you tell anyone where you're headed, he dies. If you refuse to come, he dies. Come to me, Supergirl. But, I should warn you. In the myths, Cadmus was really only famous for one thing; he killed monsters.”
