    Hank lumbered towards her, and Kara, still recovering from flaring, didn't even have the opportunity to shield herself as the first hammering strike rocked her body. The blow knocked her to her hands and knees, and before she could even draw in a breath, a kick connected to her ribs, knocking whatever air had been left in her lungs out of her.

    He was merciless, raining kick after kick into her stomach and chest. The pain spread through her as she futilely tried to protect herself, but it was in vain. In this condition, and already worn down as she was, she had no hope of stopping him.

    Eventually, the blows stopped, and Kara sucked in a desperate breath, but her relief was short-lived. She felt someone kneel over her as her body was roughly lifted by the front of her suit. She barely had any time to prepare herself before Hank's fist connected violently with her face.

    On the second hit, she felt her lip split. By the fourth, she could feel her nose starting to give. By the tenth, everything had started to go dark as black crept into the edges of her vision, and she thought that Hank might beat her straight into unconsciousness, but right before the pain became too much to bear and she slipped under, Lillian spoke from the corner of the cell.

    “Enough.”

    Finally, Hank let her go, and she fell to the floor in a heap.

    A sharp tug forced her chin up, and she couldn't help the hiss of pain that escaped her. Lillian surveyed the damage, looking with something akin to glee at the bruising that was already beginning to show and the blood trickling from her lip and nose. The fingers gripping her face, tightened, digging even deeper into the already tender flesh.

    “So, you do bleed after all,” She said with satisfaction before turning to Hank, “Take her to my lab.”

    As she lay limply in Hank's arms, barely clinging to her consciousness, she fuzzily saw Lillian turn to watch as she was hauled away, “And Supergirl, that was just a taste of my hospitality. If last time is anything to go by, you have three more days of fun to look forward to with us.”

    Kara's last thought before she lost consciousness was of her wife. She would be worried.

    Lena paced back and forth restlessly. It had been twelve hours since she had last heard from Kara, and she had no doubt that something had gone poorly. However, there wasn't much she could do. She knew where Kara had gone, but that undoubtedly wasn't where she still was. More importantly, however, there was no explainable reason she would have this information that wouldn't end up either exposing her and Kara's relationship or landing her in a cell.

    As worried as she was, if that singular word could even cover how she felt, she would not expose their relationship. For one, she had faith in her wife, that she would come back to her. Second, She firmly believed that it was Kara's decision to tell her family the truth, and unless Kara was proven to be in mortal danger, Lena would not cross that line.

    However, the longer Kara was missing, the harder Lena found it to contain herself. After her first meeting, she had canceled the rest of her day and locked herself in her office, lest the media catch wind something was amiss.

    Eventually, she couldn't help herself. She needed to know. Pulling out her phone, she pulled up the contact and hit call.

    The line rang for a few moments before the call went through, “Danvers, who is this?”

    “Alex, it's Lena,” She responded.

    “Luthor,” Alex replied suspiciously, “How did you get this number.”

    “Kara gave it to me, in case I ever needed to get ahold of you,” Lena said, pushing down the pang she felt at Alex's tone.

    “Well, what do you want?”

    “I was supposed to have lunch with Kara today, and she didn't show up, and she didn't call or text. I was wondering if something happened to her.”

    If Lena was being honest with herself, she wasn't entirely sure why she was making this phone call. She already knew that something was wrong regarding Kara's rescue. Maybe she needed to be sure that someone was looking, or maybe she needed someone to commiserate with, even if Alex didn't know that was what was happening.

    “Have you considered that she just changed her mind about you?” Alex asked, making no attempt to mask her dislike of Lena and displeasure at the continuation of this conversation.

    “She wouldn't do that,” Lena said, doing her best to hide the sting of Alex's remark hitting her deepest fear.

    “You're probably right,” Alex agreed, clearly frustrated at her sister for what she considered a shocking lack of judgment, “She probably had something come up at her job last minute and didn't have time to tell you. She's very busy, you know. I'm sure she would be glad to tell you about it all later. I, however, have better things to do with my time.”

    Lena blinked as she heard the dial tone that indicated that Alex had, in fact, hung up on her. Rather than make her feel better, the call had done the opposite. Clearly, Alex had no idea that her sister was missing and probably believed that Kara had indeed had something come up and would be in contact later.

    Lena quickly made her way to her desk. If the DEO wasn't looking, then Lena would do it herself.

    Kara, in a way that was becoming uncomfortably familiar recently, slowly and painfully rose to consciousness. Her eyes fluttered around the cell and landed on Mon-El, unconscious on the opposite side of the cell. Thankfully, it seemed Lillian was a woman of her word, because the bullet had been removed from his leg.

    She tried to think back to what had happened after Hank had taken her away, but it was foggy. She remembered a needle, pricking into her normally invulnerable skin. There was definitely a memory of the unmistakable acidic burn of Kryptonite, but that was all she could recall.

    She could feel the tingling feeling of her body recovering from not only the solar flare, but also the beating she had received, only one of several if the soreness lingering in her body was to be believed. Lillian, it seemed, assured that Kara would be out of commission and likely overconfident at her plan's success, hadn't even bothered to move Kara to a room without a window.

    Making her way over to the singular patch of sunlight in the cell, she sat down, feeling slightly better as the light washed over her and hastened the reinvigoration process. Having solar flared, the process was slower than it would have been otherwise, but nonetheless, she could feel immediately as her body began to stitch itself back together.

    She leaned her head back against one of the bars as she waited, pondering her situation. She had no doubt that Lillian never intended to let her leave alive, if not to remove one obstacle to her plans, then at least in some twisted attempt to make Kal feel the same pain that she had felt at the death of her son.

    Mon-El, though, might have a chance. If Lillian did come to finish her off before she could escape, she might turn Mon-El lose, just so word of her victory would spread and strike dread into the hearts of aliens everywhere.

    Invariably, her thoughts turned to Lena. If she did die, Lena would be left with no one. Any brief piece of concern that Alex or Kal may have ever felt for her undoubtedly had nothing to do with her and everything to do with avoiding Kara's ire. At least, she could hope, that Eliza would tell Alex off and take care of Lena, but she knew that it was more likely that Lena would retreat into herself and cut everyone and everything off.

    A few hours later, Mon-El groaned, slowly sitting up. Kara looked over at him, her hand falling away from where it had been rubbing at her other wrist.

    “What happened?” He asked groggily, “I remember you waking up, something about a solar flare, then it's all foggy.”

    “They shot you,” Kara replied, maintaining her position in the sunlight, “Turns out, you are allergic to lead. It must've caused a systemic reaction.”

    “Are you okay? Your powers?”

    “I'm doing better, the light helps,” Kara said, waving a hand vaguely at the windows.

    “Why would they leave us where you can get the light, it seems foolish.”

    “Their understanding is flawed,” Kara responded, “Most people think my powers have one dimension. I get hit with sunlight, I get powers. When I don't get enough sunlight, or I solar flare, no powers, simple as that, but it doesn't work that way. Yes, I get my powers from the sun, but it isn't just about generation, it's also about storage.

    “When I absorb more energy than I use, the excess gets stored, to a point, until I have need of it. When I solar flare, it is actually me using up the entirety of my surplus of energy, which in turn burns out my ability to build the excess back up. During that time, I feel exhausted, like I haven't slept in years, and I'm a lot more vulnerable than normal, but I'm not actually powerless.

    “Last time this happened, Alex had forbade me from going back out as Supergirl for three days, mostly because she worries, but also partly because everything with Lex happened literally the next day. I guess CADMUS took Supergirl not appearing for three days to mean I will be completely powerless for the next three days. I might have to thank Alex for fussing over me if we get out of here.”

    “We'll get out of this Kara,” he said confidently.

    “Probably,” She said, her hand thoughtlessly sliding to her wrist as she continued, “But if I don't make it, I need you to do something for me.”

    “Kara, you can't-”

    “I need you to promise me, Mon-El,” Kara said, more forcefully this time, cutting across Mon-El's reassurance.

    “Okay, I promise.”

    “If I don't make it, I need you to tell Alex to forgive Lena.”

    Mon-El stared blankly at her for a few moments before opening his mouth to ask incredulously, “What?!”

    “I don't want-” Kara started, before suddenly stopping.

    A warm, intense tingling sensation flowed across her body. It was familiar from after she had fought Red Tornado. Tentatively, she flexed her powers, and sure enough, the solar flare had ended. At some point, beyond her notice, Kara's injuries had finished mending and her body had gotten to work restoring her other systems. She couldn't be sure, since she had been unconscious part of the time, but it felt like it had happened faster this time around.

    Quickly, she cast out her hearing and felt an enormous wave of relief when Lena's heartbeat once again filled her ears. It sounded muffled, so they were most likely underground, which explained why Kara hadn't been able to hear it previously. Unsurprisingly, it wasn't beating at its normal slow and reassuring pace. It fluttered fretfully in Kara's ears at Lena's apparent distress.

    “You're powers?” Mon-El guessed, having watched the varied expressions flash over Kara's face.

    “Yeah,” Kara said, standing and walking to the bars of the cage, “Let's get out of here.”

    “Slow down, Kara, you shouldn't rush. At least absorb a little more light before you try and bust us out of here. That's Nth metal, it's nearly indestructible.”

    Kara knew he was right. She was definitely far from recovered, and pushing herself when she was in this state was not wise. But Lena was clearly worried for her, and Kara wouldn't leave her waiting a moment longer than she could help. She just had to hope that pure adrenaline could do the job.

    Kara put one hand on the door, using the other to brace herself against the cell, and push with everything she had. She pushed, and pushed, and pushed, she pushed even as her still replenishing energy reserve protested as it was once again squeezed for every drop of power she could muster, driven by her desperate desire to get to Lena and relieve the anguish she had inadvertently placed upon her.

    Finally, with a loud screech, the cell door gave way and bent far enough to give them space to make their way through. Kara slipped out first, Mon-El following with an awed look on his face. They didn't encounter anyone on their way out of the base, Lillian evidently not thinking it worthy of many guards while her prisoners were incapacitated.

    Kara supported the limping Mon-El through the halls, until finally they came to the surface. Once they were free, Kara flew them the relatively short distance back to National City.

    Setting down a few blocks from the DEO, Kara turned to Mon-El, “Go tell Alex what happened. I have something I need to take care of quickly, I'll be there in a bit.”

    Nodding, Mon-El hobbled off down the street in the direction of the DEO. Once he was out of sight, Kara took off, stopping at her old apartment, as it was nearer, showering and grabbing a fresh set of clothes in a matter of seconds before heading to L-Corp. Landing just outside, she changed before heading in, nodding to the security on duty before getting in the elevator.

    She spent the whole long ride to Lena's floor tapping her foot impatiently. She exited as the doors opened, nearly clipping one in her haste to get to Lena. Jess looked up from her desk as Kara passed, not bothering to even buzz Lena's intercom as Kara approached Lena's office doors.

    As Kara walked through the doors, causing Lena to look up. There was a slew of emotions on her wife's face: irritation at being interrupted, a frantic, manic energy as she undoubtedly attempted to track her mother's movements, and unmistakably underneath it all, fear, but it all melted away as her eyes landed on her wife.

    “Kara!” She exclaimed, flying out of her seat with what Kara might've considered superspeed had she not known better.

    Lena crashed into her arms at full speed, and rather than trust her exhausted, nearly twice solar-flared body to put the right amount of resistance to catch Lena without hurting her, Kara simply went with her momentum, allowing Lena to fall on top of her as she fell to the floor.

    Before Kara could say anything, Lena kissed her, and kissed her hard, pushing her down into the floor as she crushed their lips together. Lena didn't let up until they were both breathless, trading the kiss for pressing her forehead to Kara's, making no move to get up.

    “I'm sorry,” Kara whispered against her lips.

    “Don't apologize,” Lena whispered back, “I'm just glad you're here.”

    Kara pulled her close, one hand reaching up to cradle the back of her neck as the other kept her secured firmly above her. After holding her as long as she dared, Kara floated them both back to their feet.

    “I've got to go check in with the DEO, or Alex will send out a search party. I'll tell you everything when I'm done.”

    At Lena's nod, Kara moved to leave, making sure to grab one of the spare phones Lena kept ready for her due to her tendency to break hers in the course of her Supergirl duties and giving her wife one final kiss before making the descent to the lobby and flying to the DEO.

    No sooner had Kara set down on the DEO balcony than she saw her sister storming over to her.

    “Where have you been?” Alex demanded.

    “I just needed a few minutes to clear my head, Alex,” Kara replied.

    Her sister sent her a look that let her know she wasn't quite convinced, but fortunately, she dropped it. Following Alex deeper into the DEO, they made their way into one of the conference rooms where J'onn was waiting for them.

    Alex moved to go sit next to J'onn, while Kara sat down opposite them, closer to the door. Alex was still eyeing her expectantly, as if she was waiting for Kara to tell her what she had been as of yet reticent to reveal, making Kara feel more like this was going to be an interrogation than a debriefing.

    “Kara,” J'onn said, his relief at her return clear, “How are you feeling? If you need to rest more before we debrief, this can wait.”

    “No, but thank you, J'onn,” Kara said, returning his smile, “I'd rather just get this over with.”

    “Very well. Mon-El has already told us what he remembered, but it wasn't much. Please, tell us everything.”

    Starting from the beginning, Kara recounted her story fully, leaving out only anything that might have exposed her and Lena's relationship. As she mentioned that it was Lillian who had been behind CADMUS this whole time, and in fact that she admitted to the attempted assassinations on Lena earlier in the year, she snuck a quick glance at her sister, but saw no indication that Alex had any remorse for the stance she had taken on the topic.

    Alex and J'onn remained dutifully quiet as she spoke, though Alex had visibly had to stop herself from interrupting several times throughout.

    “So let me get this straight,” Alex said once Kara had finally finished speaking, “Neither Hank, nor Metallo are dead, and they both work for Lillian Luthor, the leader of CADMUS, who Kidnapped Mon-El to get to you, forced you to solar flare, where she stuck you with a needle and exposed you to more Kryptonite?”

    “Yeah, that about sums it up,” Kara agreed.

    Alex looked at J'onn, who seemed not to find any issue with Kara's story, before looking back at her sister, “And, can you run that part where you bent an Nth Metal cage less than an hour after recovering from a solar flare by me again?”

    It took everything Kara had not to look away from her sister's intense look. It was a look she had seen only once before, four months previously, after she had escaped the black mercy on her own and disappeared for hours on end, refusing to explain how she had escaped or where she had gone upon her return.

    “They miscalculated. They thought they had three days where I was completely powerless. Not only did they not bother to leave me without sunlight, but I also recovered faster this time. I had to take the opportunity to escape before they came back,” Kara answered carefully, not letting any of the thoughts that had been running through her head about Lena while she strained to escape show on her face.

    Alex considered her carefully, clearly not fully buying her story, but to Kara's great relief, she didn't push it any further. Instead, she stood and walked over to her side, indicating Kara to follow her.

    “Come on, let's get you under the sun lamps.”

    “No, Alex, please. I just want to go home and sleep.”

    Alex turned to look at J'onn, hoping he might advise Kara to follow Alex to the med bay, but instead, he just nodded, “Go, rest. I'll cover for you should the need arise.”

    “Kara,” Alex said sternly, following Kara out of the conference room, “It's been less than 24 hours since you recovered, and from the sound of it you nearly solar flared again escaping, you need to finish recharging.”

    “I'll come back first thing in the morning, and you can make me sit under the lamps for as long as you want, I promise, but right now, I need sleep, and I want to do it in my own bed.”

    Alex considered her for a long moment before giving in, “Fine, but I expect you here first thing in the morning.”

    Kara nodded and turned to walk back to the balcony, but Alex stopped her before she could, “Are you sure you don't want some company? I could come with you and order some food, and we can do sister night, or I can invite Winn and James, and we can do an impromptu game night?”

    She looked back at her sister, who clearly was still worrying about her. While her offer did sound nice, there was really only one thing she wanted right now, and unfortunately, Alex's presence would make that impossible.

    “That sounds nice, Alex, but I seriously am going to go straight to sleep so I can recover. Why don't you try and get everyone together, and we can do game night tomorrow at my apartment.”

    “Okay, I love you. Sleep well.”

    “I love you too.”

    With that, Kara turned and walked away, making it to the balcony uninterrupted this time. Launching into the air, she quickly removed her new phone and sent Lena a text.

    A few moments later, Kara set down in her old apartment for the second time that day. Figuring Lena would take at least a few minutes to arrive, she made her way about the apartment, brushing away the subtle signs that the apartment was unlived in so it would be ready for everyone to arrive the next night.

    It took her nearly twenty minutes without her powers, as she didn't want to push her already strained body any further than necessary. As she worked, she could hear Lena getting closer, and not long after she finished, Lena entered the building.

    Making her way over to the door, she opened it just as Lena's key was about to enter the keyhole.

    “Darling,” Lena greeted, smiling at the familiar occurrence.

    Kara quickly ushered Lena inside, guiding her to sit on the couch while she got them something to drink. Lena had, of course, been here before, but most of the times she had visited National City in the past, they had opted not to linger too long somewhere Kara's family might happen upon them.

    Kara knew that it was risky for Lena to be here, especially as she was under no illusion that her sister's worry would eventually drive her to check in, no matter what she had said, but she knew equally as much that she couldn't spend the night away from Lena, not after the last few days.

    Setting down Lena's drink in front of her, Kara began the task of rehashing what had happened to her for the second time that day. As she listened, Lena's face hardened, glaciating until her accommodations and treatment at CADMUS looked warm and comfortable in comparison, Lena's thumb running soothingly over her hands as she spoke the only sign that the rage building within Lena wasn't directed at her.

    "I won't let her get away with this,” Lena said after Kara had finished, pouring enough furious conviction into the single sentence that Kara almost felt bad for Lillian for the hellfire Lena would rain down upon her.

    Surely enough, just shy of midnight, Kara was awoken by a knocking at her door. Tucking her still-sleeping wife deeper into the covers, so should anyone peek into the room she wouldn't be directly visible, she silently slipped out of bed and dressed, moving to the door before Lena could be awoken by any further knocking.

    She opened the door to reveal her sister on the other side, carrying Pizza and Potstickers, with an apologetic look on her face.

    “I'm sorry,” Alex said as she slipped passed Kara into the room, “I know you said you wanted to sleep, but I wanted to check that you were okay and that you had eaten enough.”

    Kara neglected to mention that Lena had ordered her a veritable feast once they had finished discussing the previous few day's events. However, that had been several hours previously, and Kara never was one to turn down more food.

    Alex watched her intently as she opened the first box and picked up a piece, seemingly waiting for her to either confess whatever details she had previously withheld or else wise agree that she was, in fact, not okay and needed Alex's immediate medical intervention.

    After Kara had finished the first pizza and moved onto the next, Alex finally spoke, “Are you sure you're alright? You went through a lot.”

    “I'm fine, Alex,“ Kara replied around her slice, “Like I said, I just need some sleep.”

    “Good, that's good,” Alex said, visibly relaxing.

    They made benign conversation as Kara finished the second Pizza, Alex catching her sister up on what she and Maggie had been doing before they had realized that Kara had been captured by CADMUS, then how Alex and the DEO had tried to track her down, all while Alex had to keep Maggie in the dark since she didn't know Kara's identity.

    Alex, it seemed, could only wait as long as it took Kara to eat the three pizzas and two orders of potstickers, which wasn't long considering her still recovering state, before she got to the matter that was truly bothering her.

    “Kara, Please. I know there is something you aren't telling me. I went to the CADMUS base, I saw the bars you bent. There is no way you should have been capable of that, not in the state you were in. Tell me what is going on.”

    Kara looked at her sister for a moment. She knew Alex was only worried for her, and she hated having to lie to her sister so repeatedly, but she saw nothing else she could do, not until Alex was ready to accept Lena for her own self and stop condemning her for her family.

    “There is nothing to tell, Alex. It's just like I said. My powers came back sooner than anticipated, and I knew I needed to capitalize on Lillian, Hank, and Metallo's absence to escape while I could.”

    Alex's frustration at her refusal to be forthcoming was evident on her face, but her sister said nothing, instead gathering the trash from the meal she had brought and storming over to the kitchen to dispose of it. However, halfway to the kitchen, she froze, her eyes falling on the purse that had been left on the kitchen island at the same time Kara's had.

    “What's that?” Alex asked, her voice suspicious.

    Kara debated claiming that it was hers, but it would be of no use. Alex knew she never used a purse, and the bag on the counter was far too expensive for Kara to have afforded on a reporter's salary. She sat on the couch, frozen, her mind reeling that she had been so careless as Alex slowly approached the purse and ran a finger over the expensive material.

    “Who's purse is this, and please don't tell me it's whose I think it is,” Alex said, her voice taking the tone that only older sisters could achieve while scolding their younger sibling.

    “She deserved to know that her mother was running a xenophobic criminal organization,” Kara said, seeing no reasonable way to deny Alex's accusation.

    “Why would you involve her in this, Kara? We have no idea where her loyalties lie.”

    “You,” Kara retorted, “are the one that doubts her loyalties. I trust her, and she was devastated by the news.”

    “At the least, you need to check with the DEO before you disclose information like this to civilians, Kara,” Alex said, looking at her sister with a mix of irritation and disbelief.

    “I don't work for the DEO, Alex. I'm not your employee. I'm sorry that you don't like it, but she is my friend, and she deserves to know what her mother is up to.”

    Alex, knowing she was unlikely to make much headway, but too stubborn to let the topic drop completely, pushed on, needing to make sure that if Kara was going to be foolhardy enough to share information with a Luthor, she at least wasn't putting her identity at risk, “And how exactly did you tell Lena that you came across this information. This isn't exactly something you should know.”

    “Supergirl told me,” her sister replied, with a touch of impatience and more than a little sass, “Lena knows we are friends.”

    Alex eyed her sister warily. Honestly, she didn't know entirely why Kara was so vehemently defending a Luthor, even if said Luthor had been her friend once upon a time. Alas, she knew that pushing Kara would only likely cause her to dig in further in Lena's defense, so all she could do was keep her eye on the Luthor and their purported rekindling friendship while doing her best to shield Kara from the inevitably poor outcome of this endeavor.

    “You shouldn't be so trusting of her, Kara.”

    The comment caused a spark to flare in Kara's eyes, the same spark Alex had seen several times previously as she received a tongue-lashing from her sister when she had reasonably pointed out that Lena was not above reproach. This time, however, Kara didn't snap at her, but instead turned away and walked back towards her bedroom.

    As Alex watched her walk away, her eyes snagged on one of the blankets that adorned Kara's couch. This blanket, Alex knew, was one of Kara's favorites, but something was off with it. She wasn't sure if it was the color, or possibly the material, but there was definitely something not right about it.

    Before she could think more about it, Kara spoke from the entrance of her bedroom, “Goodnight, Alex. I'll see you in the morning.”

    Kara waited until the door had closed behind Alex before turning and meeting her wife's eyes, who had woken up at some point during the exchange and doubtlessly heard one of Alex's baseless claims against her.

    “I'm sorry about Alex,” She said, settling back into bed and pulling Lena's body tightly against hers.

    “It's okay,” Lena replied, “it's no more than I expect from her.”

    Kara, finding no sufficient way to display her displeasure at her sister's bullheadedness, simply held Lena tighter as they both drifted back to sleep.

    The next evening, once Alex had finally let Kara leave the DEO after she was satisfied that Kara had spent long enough under the sun lamps, and then an additional hour or two more for “observation”, Kara had returned to her old apartment with Lena to begin setting everything up for the game night she had agreed to host.

    "Well, that's the last of the food,” Lena said, setting out the last dish, “You'd better fly me back to our penthouse before anyone shows up.”

    “No,” Kara said, causing Lena to furrow her brows.

    Kara had been about to agree, but it didn't feel right to her. She wanted Lena to be here, and she knew that if she had to send Lena away now, she would probably end up losing her temper again like she nearly had last time.

    “No?” Lena replied, “Kara, they will be here any minute.”

    “I don't care. I want you here. They'll have to accept this at some point. Maybe this will be a good way to ease them into it.”

    Kara could tell that Lena knew this was a rather thin justification for a decision born more out of ill temper and stubbornness than anything else, but instead of calling Kara on it, she just smiled and said, “They are your family and friends, darling. However you want to handle this, I will support you. Will you do me a favor, though, before they arrive?”

    More than willing to oblige, Kara quickly flew to their penthouse to retrieve the item Lena had requested, but had no time to inquire as to its purpose before the familiar sound of Alex knocking at her door demanded her attention. Setting the bottle down on the counter, she made her way over to the door.

    “Hey Kara, am I the first-” Alex said as she stepped past her sister into the room, only to freeze as her gaze landed on Lena.

    “Why don't I get us something to drink,” Lena said, quickly making her way to the kitchen so Kara and her sister could speak alone on the far side of the room.

    “What is she doing here, Kara?” Alex hissed once Lena made it to the other side of the room.

    “I invited her to come back for game night when she came to get her purse,” Kara hissed back, “She is my friend, and this is my apartment, so you can deal with it, or you can leave.”

    Alex swelled up to respond, but before she could do so, Lena returned, offering her a glass, “Scotch, right?”

    Deflating at Lena's presence, Alex accepted the glass after a moment's hesitation, sniffing it warily before taking a sip. At once, her eyes widened. She looked at the amber liquid in her glass before turning her eyes suspiciously on her sister.

    “This isn't yours, is it Kara? You don't even like Scotch.” Alex demanded.

    “No,” Lena answered smoothly, swirling the scotch in her own glass “I brought it.”

    Alex looked conflicted for a moment, but eventually seemed to decide that Lena could stay, at least for the night. Kara hid her smile by taking a sip of wine, if her wife had any more tricks like that up her sleeve, maybe it would be easier to convince Alex to accept Lena than she had thought.

    Over the next few dozen minutes, the rest of their friends arrived, sans Brainy and J'onn, who had decided to stay at the DEO should CADMUS try and make another move now that Kara had escaped.

    Overall, introducing Lena to her friends hadn't gone any better or worse than she had expected. Winn had geeked out at meeting Lena, though technically they had met when Lena's Gala had been attacked. James affected nonchalance, but Kara could tell that Lena's presence had rankled him. Mon-El had been the most concerning, a significant look passing over his face as he looked back and forth between Kara and Lena.

    “Maggie couldn't make it?” Kara asked once everyone had settled in with their drinks.

    “No,” Alex replied regretfully, “she had some casework to take care of tonight”

    For their first game, they eventually settled on Charades, with boys on one team and girls on the other, much to Alex's displeasure. Luckily, however, they had managed to pull out the win. Kara worked very well with both Lena and Alex individually, despite Alex and Lena not synergizing as teammates.

    Chapter End Notes

    You may have noticed that the fight with Hank went differently than in the shows (Kara got her ass handed to her, and he only hit her like three times for fuck's sake). I refute that this is possible strongly. You cannot convince me that Kryptonians are the strongest thing you are going to run into on earth, so not just anyone can molly wop them, especially not just a randomly augmented human. Relatedly, and to preface this before I say it, I am not telling anyone how to write their own fics or what they should or should not do or anything like that, I really don't care for solar flaring as it is presented in the show (it is 100% just a plot device) and it is continually used that way in fics, to the point where it seems like any old alien can just come along and make Kara solar flare.

    For my personal tastes, and this may be because I like the supergirl/kryptonian aspect more than the human/Kara Danvers aspect (I have been know to tell people that I ship Supergirl (and/or Kara Zor-El)/Lena more than Kara Danvers/Lena, and I do have a lot of complicated headcanons behind this too), Kara isn't challenged to the point where she has to solar flare pretty much at all. Along that end, I actually changed how Solar flaring works, as discussed in this chapter, and gave her a new power related to the Aura she gives off (maybe my most beloved headcanon) that would lead her to a state of complete powerlessness like solar flaring used to. Maybe if you all are interested I could write a ficlet about it to post once this fic is done, as it is not shown in this story.

    Anyway, back to my original point. You may have noticed that Kara can hold her own in a fight, and that she doesn't stumble blindly into traps, and most importantly she is actually a competent superhero. A strange concept I'm sure if you've only seen the shows (especially the later seasons) where superpowered Kara is incapable of solving any problems unless at least 2 people with far less power and experience than her are also present. Touching back on the Kara Zor-El > Kara Danvers bit, I really view all the goofiness/silliness/incompetence tha Kara sometimes displays in the show as just a mask (the mask of Kara Danvers) that she wears to conceal her identity. There are parts of it that are true to her, but I don't think that is what she is actually like under all her masks (I will probably write a fic about this, I plan to write many fics to demonstrate my many, many headcanons)

    On to slightly more mundane things, I never really cared for the whole Jeremiah storyline in season 2, and the way it was used made me like it even less, so none of that here. Also, no one ask me why I had Kara go up the elevator to Lena instead of just landing on her balcony. Let's just say Kara didn't want to fly too much when she was feeling so weak and leave it at that and not think that I didn't think of it until I was doing my final read and I was to lazy to rewrite it at that point. I like what is there and what it demonstrates better anyway.

    In case anyone is curious, that was the smuttiest scene in the whole fic. Don't blame (or thank) me, that was 100% Lena's doing. I had no say in the matter. IN this scene, I went back and forth on whether or not Kara's suit is one piece or two, and I eventually settled on it being two pieces, the top and the skirt (I guess three if we are counting the leggings). I guess this is a good enough segue into the issue I sometimes have with smut. I like smut, and I like plot. I like them together, and I like them separate, but I have trouble combining them. I don't want to write a bunch of smut into a plot centric fic and turn have people not want to write it because of the smut, or have a specific dynamic to the smut that people who prefer smut might not read if it is in a smit centric fic, or similarly have a plot that plot centric people might not read because it is given in a smut centric fic. I don't know that there is a great answer to this, and I do mostly write for myself anyway, I just figured I'd throw it out there.

    Anyway, That was the chapter. Let me know what you think, or if you want to ask about my headcanons I'm always happy to gush.

    Chapter Notes

    I moved and got everything set up just in time to not be late posting this chapter, so lets get into it. However, I haven't really seen any fics that do it the way I would like it done. At lot of fics it seems like it either comes out within the first few chapters, and is almost a side plot, or the fic is more of a oneshot. To be clear, I have no problems with either of these, and everyone is free to write what they want, I'm just stating what I've seen. I did incorporate a few other ideas I've had flowing around my head as well, but that is the main focus. So, it will be a bit later into the fic until it comes out, but in my opinion, the wait is well worth it (as are the scenes in which it comes out and everything that follows).

    Kara's hand was quickly headed for more dangerous territory before it abruptly stopped as the doorbell rang. With a reluctant groan, Kara pulled away from the kiss, peering at the door for a moment before stepping away to go answer it.

    “It's Eliza,” Kara explained, looking back over her shoulder ruefully as Lena did her best to unrumple her shirt.

    Once Eliza settled in, they had all made their way to the couch so they could sit and talk while they waited for the turkey to finish and everyone else to arrive. This was another thing that Lena still hadn't grown accustomed to, having a mother figure in her life who actually liked her.

    She and Eliza had interacted, albeit rarely, in the two and a half years since the wedding, and every time it happened, Lena was filled with such a sense of belonging that it nearly overwhelmed her. Unbeknownst to her, every time this happened, Kara noticed, and in fact strived to arrange them more time to spend together, if only so she could see such happiness on her wife's face more often.

    Although there had been more than thirty minutes left for the turkey to cook in the oven when Eliza had arrived, it seemed no more than mere minutes had passed by the time the timer went off reminding them to take it out.

    “I got it,” Kara said, shooting up from her position beside Lena as she gave them a dazzling smile, “You both stay here.”

    Returning to her conversation with Eliza, they were both laughing at a story Lena had just told Eliza, Kara undoubtedly blushing in the kitchen as she listened in, when the door opened and Alex walked in unannounced.

    In the Kitchen, Kara was experiencing a sharp sense of déjà vu as Alex had greeted her before turning towards the sound of laughter coming from the living area and freezing as she saw Lena and Eliza laughing together. Her sister's eyes flicked to her, then to Eliza, before settling on Lena.

    “What are you doing here?” Alex asked, making no attempt to hide the disdain in her voice.

    “Alexandra Danvers, that is no way to greet a guest,” Eliza snapped in her best disappointed mother voice, thankfully distracting Alex from the way the metal cookie sheet Kara had been about to put away after cleaning it bent like cheap plastic in her hands.

    Briefly, she had hoped that the $30,000 bottle of scotch Lena had brought to game night might've bought her more than a single night of goodwill, but she should've known it wouldn't have been that easy, not when Alex associated all Luthors with her life being in danger.

    Alex, summoned for a lecture by the look on Eliza's face, walked over to her mother as Lena joined Kara in the kitchen to give them some space. Tuning out the furious whispering happening on the far side of the room, Kara turned to Lena as she approached, risking running her hand briefly over Lena's face while Alex was distracted and had her back turned.

    “It's okay,” Lena whispered before Kara could even apologize.

    “It is not okay,” Kara replied, shooting a dirty look at her sister's back.

    Before anything else could be said, a loud knock interrupted both duos, causing all four heads to look up. Kara quickly made her way to open the door, giving Maggie a brief smile before letting her in. Maggie paused in the entryway, clearly picking up on the tension in the room.

    Alex, panicking, turned back to Eliza with wide eyes, clearly not expecting to have to introduce her girlfriend to her mother in the midst of an argument, but Eliza graciously let it drop as she greeted Maggie warmly. Not long after, everyone else began arriving, Winn and James showed up together, followed by J'onn, then Brainy, and finally Mon-El.

    No one said anything about Lena's presence, thankfully, for Kara's patience was still worn thin from Alex's reaction to her wife, but she did note the look that James had briefly shot her, causing her fists to clench. All in all, Kara was immensely glad that Eliza had been the one to respond to Alex's jab, if she had it likely would have escalated into a shouting match, and she didn't know if she could've gotten through the whole night dealing with Alex and James' distrust if Eliza hadn't taken over.

    These social situations were starting to become a lose-lose situation for her, either she had to spend time away from Lena, knowing her wife wasn't welcome, or she had to endure the looks and jabs that were constantly sent her wife’s way. Still, having Lena with her was better than not, but she would be lying if she said there wasn't some degree of bitterness in her as a result.

    There hadn't been much of an opportunity for get-togethers as of late, because even with CADMUS quiet since she had escaped, there had been a constant stream of work between her job at CatCo and everything with the DEO, and neither she nor any of her friends could make the time. A fact for which she had been secretly thankful.

    Still, she hadn't forgotten how hard it was, and with even more people here this time, she couldn't risk so much as a lingering glance or a single unjustifiable touch, lest someone notice, and it was doing nothing to calm her already frayed nerves. Nor did it help that Eliza, the only other person in the room who could comprehend how she was feeling was having her attention completely occupied by Alex, Maggie, and for some reason Mon-El.

    On the far side of the room, James and Winn were talking suspiciously about something, and Kara was tempted to listen in, not that she would actually eavesdrop, but she didn't even need to use her super hearing to know they were discussing Guardian business, still with no idea that she already knew.

    Opposite James and Winn, J'onn and Brainy were deep in discussion, likely discussing something to do with the DEO. Brainy didn't really seem to understand the concept of a holiday where he could just relax and not think about anything work-related, and J'onn didn't seem to mind.

    So, that left Kara and Lena on the couch, politely sharing small talk and catching themselves up to what the other had been up to as if they were actually the recuperating friends they pretended to be, and not the married couple that spent every moment together possible that they actually were.

    Once she thought everyone had spent enough time socializing, Kara stood to start setting the table, Lena standing with her to help. Within a few minutes, they had gotten everything set at the table, everyone sitting down as they realized dinner was about to start, and Kara had sent Lena to sit down as she retrieved the turkey from where she had put it to stay warm in the oven when it had become apparent that dinner wouldn't be starting immediately.

    Before she could retrieve the turkey, however, she had been stopped by the doorbell ringing unexpectedly. She made her way to the door, trying to remember if they had been expecting anyone else. Opening it up, she found herself face to face with Kal and Lois.

    “Hey Kara,” Lois greeted warmly.

    “Lois,” Kara said, returning the greeting and pulling Lois into a hug, “I wasn't expecting you.”

    “I hope you don't mind. We are still having... issues, with my father, and Eliza invited us so we wouldn't have to spend Thanksgiving alone, since Lucy is still at the White House.”

    “Of course not. It's wonderful to see you, both of you. Come in, we were just about to eat.”

    She and Kal stepped into the room, eyes sweeping over the table, when Kal froze in a way that was becoming aggravatingly familiar as he noticed Lena. His eyes quickly flicked back to Kara, then to Lois, before he wisely decided not to broach the issue and continued to the table.

    They quickly shuffled some seats around to fit the extra guests, Eliza apologizing for forgetting to tell Kara to expect them as they did so. In the end, Lois ended up sitting between Eliza, who occupied one head of the table, and Lena, who thankfully had Winn on her other side.

    “Miss Luthor,” Kal greeted as he sat, his eyes quickly glancing over at his wife before continuing, “Nice to see you again. This is my wife, Lois.”

    “It's lovely to meet you,” Lois said, a warm and genuine smile gracing her face.

    “It's nice to meet you as well,” Lena replied, returning Lois' smile.

    Before long, Lena had been absorbed into a conversation with Winn as they all ate, Kal likewise having his attention pulled further down the table.

    Eliza and Lois shared a look, seeming to pass a lot of information silently between them before they both looked at Kara in tandem with concern in their eyes.

    “So, I know you've had a lot going on lately, Kiddo. How are you doing?” Lois asked carefully, her eyes flicking meaningfully towards Lena beside her.

    As Lois spoke, Kara felt Eliza's hand land reassuringly on her knee, drawing a grateful smile out of her.

    “It's been hard,” Kara replied honestly, “Sometimes it feels like I have everything I've ever wanted, but sometimes I'm reminded that not everything I want can always fit together. Some days I'm happier than I've ever been, and other days I feel I can't even be myself.”

    Eliza and Lois shared another look before her mother turned back to her, “Just know that Lois and I are always here for you.”

    “I know. Thank you, both of you. It means a lot to me.”

    The exchange hadn't lasted longer than a minute or two, but Kara latched onto it, feeling a deep sense of relief that there was someone, two someones, that knew and accepted Lena. Knowing they knew was one thing, but them reaffirming their support of her, even when Alex and Kal clearly would be in opposition if they knew was a great relief, especially now, when she felt surrounded by people who would never accept her and Lena, and when she still couldn't reach out for Lena, not even under the table, for fear of being seen.

    Later, once everyone was finished gorging themselves on Thanksgiving Dinner and the pies that Kara hadn't noticed Eliza sneak in, the others had began to leave, Lois dispatching Kal to see those who had perhaps drank a little too much safely home.

    Soon, it was just Lena, Eliza, and Lois left with her in her apartment. It felt odd, watching Lena and Lois interact. They had never met before, and Lois had never even directly acknowledged she knew of her and Kara's relationship, but still, Lois treated Lena as if she was family, despite everything Lex had done to her and her husband, needing only to know that Kara had chosen her to be satisfied as to her character.

    Kara's heart swelled with happiness watching Lena interact with Lois and Eliza, even as it twinged with bitterness that she still had to hide that happiness from everyone else.

    Eventually, Kal returned, and he and Lois had bid them goodnight before heading back to Metropolis. As soon as the door had shut behind them, Kara was at Lena's side in a blur of speed, pulling her wife into a tight hug, feeling her eyes close as relief spread through her, missing how Eliza smiled sadly as she watched on.

    When Kara had originally sworn her to secrecy, Eliza had thought that perhaps she was blowing the whole scenario out of proportion. She had thought that it certainly wouldn't have been a very easy conversation, but that Alex and, maybe to a lesser extent, Clark would've gotten over it, but now she truly understood why Kara had been so severe when making her promise she would never tell a soul.

    The way Alex had reacted upon seeing Lena, then vehemently defended her position without an ounce of remorse, had shown Eliza exactly what Kara had to deal with when it came to her life, and it broke Eliza's heart to think that it was her fault. After all, it had been she who drilled it into Alex's head day after day that it was Alex's responsibility to look after her sister, and now it seemed she had taken that mentality to the extreme.

    And Clark, while not her responsibility, clearly held a very similar sentiment. It was lucky, she supposed, that Clark had Lois and Alex had Maggie to distract them from Lena's presence, otherwise the proceedings likely would've gone off a lot less smoothly.

    Still watching the young couple in front of her sadly as her daughter clung to her wife, seeking any semblance of comfort, Eliza came to the conclusion that they likely wouldn't be parting any time soon, and made her way to the kitchen to start cleaning up while she waited.

    Finally, nearly ten minutes later, Kara sheepishly joined her in the kitchen, informing her that she didn't need to clean up. Batting her daughter's argument aside, they settled into a rhythm, soon to be joined by Lena. Together, the three of them made quick work of the dishes, though truthfully it was mostly Kara, with Lena and Eliza barely able to dry and put away what Kara had washed before there was another dish ready for them.

    When they had finished, they retired back to the living room briefly before Kara and Lena headed out, leaving Eliza to spend the night at their old apartment since they had no need of it.

    Looking once more at the couple sitting on the couch, Eliza could see the weight of Alex's action still sitting on Kara's shoulders, and she could keep her silence no more.

    “Alex will come around,” She said, holding up her hand to forestall Kara's objection, “I know it doesn't seem like it right now, but she is your sister, and she loves you. It might take her some time, but she will come around, eventually.”

    Kara took a moment to think about it before she responded, sharing a significant look with Lena as she did so.

    “I hope more than anything that she does come around,” Kara said earnestly, the conflict within her showing clearly in her eyes, “but until that happens, I will not tell her. I won't risk how she might react if she thinks that this is somehow a trick on Lena's part.”

    “I understand, and I'm not trying to encourage you to tell her, I simply feel I must apologize. I always put pressure on her to protect you, and I fear that this is the result.”

    “Her prejudice is not your fault, Eliza,” Lena responded, running a soothing hand over Kara's arm before she could get too wound up, “All my life, people have only ever seen me as an extension of my family, whether it be for good or for ill. Alex is no different than most people, and that is no one's fault. Instead of apologizing, you should accept my thanks for raising one daughter who sees past all that, for it is truly the greatest gift of my life.”

    Leaving both mother and daughter momentarily speechless, Lena excused herself to make sure that Eliza's room was ready for her, giving the two blondes a moment alone together. Wiping a tear from her eye, She smiled warmly at Kara, who still watched after her wife into the other room.

    A few moments later, Kara looked back at her, wiping her own tears away as she did so. They passed a few moments in silence before Kara spoke, changing the subject lest they tear up further.

    “Mon-El didn't bother you too much, did he? He didn't seem to want to leave you alone tonight.”

    “Not at all, Sweetie,” Eliza responded, “He was just cozying up to me to impress you. Honestly, I felt a little bad for him.”

    “Of course that's what he was up to,” Kara sighed, rolling her eyes, “I heard him ask James and Winn if I had a mate a few months ago, and he has been relentless ever since. Sometimes, I just want to launch him into space just for a little peace and quiet. If Lena hears him take one more pass at me, she might just invent a lead-infused Daxamite repellant spray.”

    Chuckling, she looked up to find Lena on her way back into the room, smiling along, though she likely hadn't known what they were discussing.

    “The room's all ready for you, Eliza,” she said as she reached Kara's side, easily budging the Kryptonian aside to sit back down.

    “Thank you, sweetie,” Eliza replied, smiling at the way Kara easily let Lena move her and the implications that it held.

    They chatted for a few minutes more, but after the sheer quantity of food they had consumed, both Lena and Eliza were starting to get more than a little drowsy, and after the extra dozen helpings that Kara had speed-eaten after everyone else had left, even she was looking a little sleepy. Making their farewells, Kara and Lena left, flying away as Eliza watched on before making her way to the bed and falling asleep.

    Kara sped through the air, her body taking her before her brain had even fully realized what had happened. She had been at work, typing away at her latest article when Lena's heart rate had spiked. She had made her way out of the building as quickly as she could manage without using her powers, but still, it hadn't been fast enough. Before she had even made it to her usual escape point, Lena's heartbeat had gone silent.

    If Lena's heart rate going up beforehand wasn't enough, then the fact that Lena hadn't warned her that she was going somewhere Kara wouldn't be able to hear her, which she had always done ever since the first time, was more than enough to send Kara into a full blown spiral.

    Going the rest of the way much faster than was wise, Kara had changed and was in the air before she had even had a moment to think. She rocketed off towards L-Corp, where she knew Lena should've been that day, and landed on her balcony, finding her office unsurprisingly empty.

    Her panic truly starting to take root, Kara stormed through Lena's office and the doors behind which Lena's assistant was sitting, the doors flying open causing the woman to jump and break her normally professional manner. Recovering from her flinch as the doors slammed into the walls, sending a loud crashing noise reverberating into the space, she looked up and her eyes widened as they fell on Kara in her full Supergirl getup.

    “Jess, where is Lena?” Kara demanded before the other woman could even so much as process her presence.

    If Jess thought it was weird that Supergirl knew her name, she didn't say anything. In the back of her mind, Kara knew that Jess knew about her and Lena, but whether Jess knew that she was Supergirl, or else the force of her presence, no doubt having flared up due to her currently distressed state, had convinced her to just go with it, Kara didn't particularly care as Jess informed her that Lena had been supervising an important delivery in one of the sublevel garages and had yet to return.

    Looking straight through the floor to scan the indicated area, Kara saw no indications of Lena, nor where she had gone. She had expected no less, but needed to look anyway. Turning back the way she had came without so much as a second thought of thanking or explaining the situation to Jess, Kara made her way back to the balcony, activating her comms as she did so.

    “Alex, Lena is missing. I need you to look into it.”

    “That isn't our jurisdiction, Kara,” Came Alex's immediate reply, accompanied by just enough of a sense that Alex could care less that Kara had to take a deep, measured breath before she responded.

    “I know, Alex,” Kara said, knowing it was true, but also knowing just as surely that she couldn't stand by and do nothing while Lena was in danger, not again, “Please. I need your help.”

    Alex didn't reply immediately, no doubt puzzling over the desperation in her voice, “Okay. Brainy and I will look into it. Do you know where she was last seen?”

    “She was in one of the Sublevel Garages overseeing an important shipment, that's all I know.”

    Alex didn't say anything else, likely already getting started on her investigation. Kara knew that Alex could care less what happened to Lena, and was only doing this because she could hear the distress in her voice, but it didn't matter why as long as she was looking.

    That settled, Kara started circling the city, pouring as much as she could into her hearing and straining as hard as she could to pick out even a single beat of Lena's heart, but coming up empty, getting nothing but a vastly increased amount of superfluous noise to filter out.

    Thirty minutes later, after Kara had circled the city numerous times without any luck, she heard her comm crackle on and paused to listen.

    “Kara, we found the security footage, you'd better come in.”

    No sooner had Alex finished speaking than Kara had appeared at her side, earning her a surprised and concerned look from her sister, who, luckily, sensed this wasn't the time to broach the topic. Leading Kara over to the command center, she queued up the footage.

    “How did you know she was missing,” her sister asked as they waited for the footage to finish loading.

    Her eyes flicking briefly to meet Alex's, Kara looked back at the screen before replying, “I just had a bad feeling, and she wasn't in her office when I arrived.”

    It wasn't exactly a lie, but the sensation she was currently experiencing could never be fully encompassed by something so insignificant as a bad feeling. She had felt this same way months previously, when Lena had been abducted by her brother when he had turned the sun red.

    Without Lena's constant and reassuring heartbeat in her ears, nothing felt right, as if the only thing keeping her tethered to reality had suddenly let go. The first time, the deafening silence that had pressed in all around her had frozen her completely, rendering her useless as Lena was in danger. This time, Kara could not, she would not stand idly by and hope Lena came out as unscathed as she had the first time.

    No, Kara was determined that she would save Lena, no matter the cost. She meant what she had said when talking to Eliza after Thanksgiving dinner, she would rather not her relationship with Lena come out until her sister had time to realize that she wasn't anything like her family, but she would take that confrontation any day over this, so rather than regulating every emotion and choosing her words carefully, she poured everything she had into trying to find Lena, and she would deal with the consequences later.

    Once the video had finished loading, Kara watched in horror as Lena was taken. She had been in the garages, just as Jess said she would be, but as Lena had turned to return to her office, she had been intercepted by Metallo, and in less than a minute had been inside an unmarked black van that had driven away without opposition.

    Kara felt the walls press in on her, her breathing becoming rapid and shallow as she fought off the urge to throw up. Lena had been taken, while Kara had been in the same city, and she had been too slow to stop it. She didn't even have the excuse of the red sun to justify it this time. Lena had needed her, and she had failed.

    The metal of the console she was leaning against screeched as the metal crumpled as if made of tin foil. Without a word, she walked back to the DEO balcony, once again listening as hard as she could, trying to pick out any faint scrap of sound that would tell her where Lena was.

    Alex watched as her sister's hands slowly unclenched from the console she had just destroyed, falling instead so that one hand rubbed ceaselessly at her other wrist as she stalked away. If it had been anyone else, she might've honestly been concerned by this reaction, but this was Kara they were talking about. Kara, who would defend someone, even a Luthor, with everything she had.

    Five days ago, when she had walked into her sister's apartment to see her mother socializing with the very same Luthor they were now looking for, if you had asked Alex if she would have lifted a single finger to aid Lena in any regard, she would have laughed in your face, but now, Kara was clearly distressed, and there was nothing Alex wouldn't do for her sister.

    So, here she was, working side by side with Brainy after having informed J'onn, doing exactly that which she never thought she would be doing, actively attempting to help a Luthor. Still, even as she did so, she couldn't suppress the fact that Lena hadn't exactly resisted being taken. For all she knew, this was some sort of trap for Kara, and the hesitation on Lena's part as she was corralled into a van by Metallo had been for the camera's benefit.

    Nearly an hour later, they hadn't made significant progress, and Alex decided that rather than shoot a concerned glance at her sister, who hadn't moved from her vigil the entire time they had been searching, she should go check on her.

    Approaching her sister where she stood stalk still on the Balcony, Alex reached out a reassuring hand to place on Kara's arm as she spoke, “We still haven't found anything, but we'll keep looking Kara, it'll be okay.”

    Kara's eyes snapped open, and she looked at Alex as if she hadn't even realized she was there, their eyes meeting briefly before Kara looked back over the skyline of National City. Alex, however, kept her eyes fixed on her sister. It had been brief, but she had seen an unmistakable glimmer of mania in Kara's eyes before she had looked away.

    For just a moment, Alex had considered pressing, asking Kara why she seemed so unsettled by Lena's disappearance, but now wasn't the time. She knew that Kara wouldn't react well to any such line of questioning when she perceived one of her friends to be in danger.

    Before she could think of what else to say, James had appeared, having arrived at some point whilst she and Brainy worked, and asked to have a private word with Kara. Looking like abandoning her post on the balcony was paramount to abandoning one of her friends in need, Kara nonetheless assented and followed James to a corner of the room.

    Returning to her computer, she got back to work, slyly glancing at James and Kara in the corner, watching Kara grow more and more irate with James as he voiced his opinions with the subtlety of a ten foot tall red skinned alien lizard trying to blend in on Earth.

    Content to let James dig his own grave for now, Alex tried to focus until Kara's presence, which had already grown to a rather oppressive level, flared even higher, causing every person in the command center to fall silent under its assault, just in time for Kara to reply to whatever James had said, her voice colder and harsher than Alex had ever heard it.

    “Lena is not a member of CADMUS.”

    Looking over at the pair, she saw the pure shock on James's face as it was bathed in heat as Kara's eyes glowed with the telltale signs of an eminent heat vision emission. However, before she could intervene and save James from whatever tactless thing he had said, Brainy had announced that he had managed to locate Lena, and Kara had appeared at his side in a flash, leaving a gaping James by himself on the opposite side of the room.

    “Where is she,” Kara demanded.

    Brainy brought up a map on the large view screen, a singular red circle in what appeared to be otherwise undeveloped wilderness the only thing in sight.

    “Here,” Brainy said, needlessly indicating the red circle, “Given its location, it is likely one of Lex's bunkers that was never found, until now.”

    Without further delay, Kara turned to leave, only stopping when Alex's hand grabbed hers from where it had been rubbing at her wrist. Kara turned back to look at her expectantly, and Alex almost floundered under the look she received, but she needed to ask, hoping it wouldn't end in her own eyebrows singed like it had for James.

    “Kara, are you sure you can trust her? There is no hard evidence that she didn't go along willingly. This could all be a trap.”

    Kara didn't respond for a moment, looking as if she was debating what to say, before her face settled, harder than Alex had ever seen it, and she spoke definitively, leaving no room for question, “She is my friend, and I believe in her.”

    Before Alex could respond, Kara was gone, leaving her gripping only air.

    As Metallo closed the door behind her and made his way to the driver's seat, Lena looked up and was met by the sight of her mother looking back at her. Feeling a spike of rage flare through her as she remember Kara's treatment at Lillian's hands, Lena nonetheless schooled her features into a blank mask, perfectly matching the one on the other woman's face.

    “Mother,” Lena greeted blandly.

    “Lena,” Lillian returned cordially, “How have you been?”

    “Alive, no thanks to you,” She said, her face remaining impassive despite the hatred that was roiling ever stronger right beneath the surface.

    “Yes,” Lillian agreed, “I will admit that I was angry when I found out that you covered up Lex's death, and I do not deny ordering the attempts on your life, but believe it or not, I reconsidered, and I do regret my actions. You and I are the only two Luthors left, we could do so much if we worked together. We could finish what your brother started.”

    Deep under the revulsion that had joined the hatred simmering within her, Lena felt a moment of pause, for Lillian had truly sounded remorseful for her actions. However, she also knew that her mother was a master of emotional manipulation, and after years of Lillian toying with her emotions, she refused to let herself be swayed.

    “I'm not a Luthor, as you never let me forget,” Lena replied, ignoring Lillian's offer rather than addressing it directly.

    “But you are. Lionel is your true father. Years ago, he had an affair with an Irish woman, and you are its result. You are more a Luthor than I am.”

    Lena fought not to let the revelation or shock show on her face, but from the sly smile that appeared on Lillian's face, she hadn't been entirely successful.

    “I know I wasn't a great mother,” Lillian said, attempting to press her perceived advantage, “It was unfair of me to punish you for your father's sins, but I do love you, Lena.”

    Leaving Lena to think on it, Lillian made her way to the front seat. It was a lot to take in, but in the end, it truly didn't matter. The Luthors had long since stopped being her family. Kara was her family, and her wife was doubtlessly searching for her. All she needed to do was bide time.

    Less than an hour later, the van pulled into a hidden bunker, and Lena stopped her subtle attempt at prying away a metal panel to open a channel for some sound to escape. The door opened, revealing Metallo looking at her distastefully as he indicated she get out. Lena's eyes dropped to his arm, where one of his veins had begun to flicker green. His look of distaste deepening, Metallo quickly rolled his sleeve down to cover it up.

    Stepping past him, She slowly made her way to where Lillian awaited, feeling a fresh surge of anger at the smug look on her mother's face. She came to a stop, both Luthor women looking towards the panel sitting conspicuously in the middle of the space.

    “Where are we?” Lena demanded, trying to keep the edge out of her voice.

    “One of Lex's bunkers,” Lillian replied easily, “filled with all sorts of weapons and technologies that Lex gathered or invented to ward off aliens, and more specifically, Kryptonians. All we need to do is open it.”

    “Then why do you need me?” Lena asked, ignoring the biometric lock on the panel in front of her.

    “The lock scans for Luthor DNA. And you, my daughter, are part of that family tree.”

    “I should have known better,” Lena said, playing the part of the scorned child to buy more time, “You only ever tell me you love me when you need something.”

    “Don't be childish,” Lillian scolded, “Just because I need you doesn't mean I don't love you.”

    “There you go again. Now I know you're lying, because no matter how many times you say you love me, we both know that's not true.”

    Lena turned to walk away, but instead ran into a strong body. She looked up and saw a man whose face was half gone, showing only the metal underneath. It was Hank Henshaw, the human-turned-cyborg that Kara had told her about.

    “I'd prefer you cooperate,” Lillian said, her face returning to the cold, haughty mask Lena was used to, “But one way or another, we are getting in that vault.”

    “I'll never help you,” Lena spat, her voice dripping the venom that had been building within her ever since Kara had told her what CADMUS had done to her.

    For a brief moment, Lillian's mask fell, and Lena thought she might've seen true sadness, but then it was back, harder than ever.

    “So be it.”

    One of Hank's hands grabbed her arm, forcing it towards the lock. Lena resisted, but it was futile, and there was nothing she could do as the floor slid away and a large, cubical vault raised into the room.

    Hearing what was in the vault was bad, but seeing it all, Lex's war suit, various weapons that she had seen used against Superman, some with devastating results, and knowing that Lillian intended to use them all against Kara made Lena feel sick.

    She could do nothing, still held firm in Hank's grip, as Lillian walked into the vault, looking disturbingly like a kid in a candy store, picking up various implements and tucking them in her pockets. After a few moments, her eyes settled on something, and she paused her perusal to walk over to it.

    “Oh Lex, my darling boy, this will be perfect,” She said, picking up an advanced prototype atmospheric dispersal device.

    “No, I don't think I will,” Lillian replied haughtily as Metallo's Kryptonite heart flared in preparation, “But now that you're here, Supergirl, there is something I've always wanted to try.”

She blinked hard, trying to force her brain to start working again, and when she opened her eyes, Kara was crouching protectively over her prone body, radiating heat and anger as she faced down Metallo and Lillian, her eyes ablaze.
