Lena felt Kara put a hand on her back, keeping her down as she lifted her cape to intercept the shot. It didn't do much to temper the effect of the Kryptonite, if Kara's grunt of pain was anything to go by, but it was still better than taking it directly.

    Once the blast had stopped, Kara dropped her cape and shot back a beam of heat vision, brighter than Lena had ever seen before, the edges beginning to tinge orange as it flooded to large cavernous space with heat. Kara's aim hit true, striking Metallo on the outer casing of the Kryptonite in his chest, melting off part of the housing that helped to contain and focus it.

    The three of them looked to it in unison as it began to glow, not in the normal contained way that it would before firing, but wild and uncontrollably, building up power, now without any way to safely release it. Lena knew with dawning horror that it was about to explode, and at such a close distance, neither of them would have any hope of surviving.

    Her eyes met Kara's as her wife came to the same conclusion. Kara tried to pick her up, but already the massive Kryptonite exposure leaking from Metallo's damaged heart was tearing her apart at a cellular level faster than she could recover.

    Still, Kara pulled Lena into her arms, and with a shout of exertion, launched them both into the air towards the hole she had created upon her arrival. It wasn't really flying, more like Kara had simply thrown them both through the air in a desperate attempt to escape. Just before they passed through the gap in the ceiling, Metallo exploded, sending a blast of Kryptonite out that washed over Kara.

    The force of the blast caused them to start tumbling over themselves midair, but just before they hit the ground, their spin stabilized as Kara clutched her close and positioned herself so she would take the brunt of their landing.

    Kara crashed into the ground, somehow not losing her grip and allowing Lena a rather soft landing. She, on the other hand, had plowed a trough into the ground, and after the lengthy Kryptonite exposure, had taken quite a beating due to the forces involved.

    Prying herself out of Kara's weakening grip, Lena tried to rouse the Kryptonian, but only received a groan of pain for her efforts. Before she could think of anything to do, the sound of something landing beside her caused her to turn quickly, putting herself between whatever it was and Kara.

    For a moment, as she looked into the face of the new arrival, she had thought Hank had come back for them, but it lacked any of the cold metal or harsh hatred of Hank, instead radiating warmth and comfort.

    “Miss Luthor. My name is J'onn J'onzz,” the man said, causing Lena to step aside as she recognized it was Kara's self-proclaimed space dad, “I'm here to help.”

    “Supergirl,” Lena said, nearly spluttering as her brain tried to say the wrong name, “You need to help her. The Kryptonite. Metallo exploded.”

    With a look of dawning understanding, J'onn leaned down to pick Kara up, looking intently at her unconscious form.

    Taking in Kara's glowing veins, J'onn turned briefly back to her as he scooped up the unconscious Kryptonian, “Agent Danvers should be here soon, she can take you back to National City. I'm going to fly her ahead so she can get treatment as soon as possible.”

    Without waiting for Lena's reply, J'onn shot into the air. Feeling a slight bit of relief at the thought that Kara would be receiving medical attention soon, Lena set to work martialing her emotions, lest Alex got an idea just how deeply Kara's condition worried her.

    Fifteen minutes later, an unmarked black SUV pulled up in front of her. The window rolled down, and she could see Alex's face looking back at her from the driver's seat.

    “Get in,” Alex ordered in a no-nonsense tone, “The other agents will secure the facility. I've been ordered to take you back.”

    Sliding into the front seat, Lena couldn't help but ask, “Supergirl, is she okay?”

    “She's fine,” Alex responded with a heavy look, “She's already recovering under the sunlamps.”

    With that good news, Lena settled herself in for what would likely be a long and uncomfortable ride, turning away to hide her relief.

    Slowly, and in an excruciating way that could only mean heavy Kryptonite exposure, Kara blinked her way back to consciousness. It took a few moments for everything to come back, but as soon as it had, her eyes snapped open and she sat up, causing a spike of pain to run through her head.

    “Hey,” came Alex's harsh scold, a hand settling on her shoulder to push her back into the bed, “Lay back down, you shouldn't be moving too much yet.”

    Kara let Alex settle her back in the bed, but only because her senses had instinctively reached out and found that Lena was okay. She was farther away than Kara would have preferred, likely at home rather than the DEO, but she wasn't in any danger.

    “How's Lena doing?” She asked regardless.

    Alex looked down at her sister, having just woken up and already asking about the Luthor, when she herself was lucky to be alive.

    “She's fine,” Alex said, not bothering to mask her displeasure, “Minor concussion and a few scrapes, nothing serious.”

    As she spoke, Alex saw her sister relax and sink into the bed. She knew that Kara was protective of her friends, and that she regrettably assigned this designation to Lena, but this level of devotion to the other woman's health and safety was starting to get to be too much, even for Kara.

    “And Metallo, is he dead?” Kara asked after a moment, drawing Alex from her thoughts.

    “No, at least not yet. The internal casing was still intact, so the blast was concentrated outwards. Whatever they did to him, it made him pretty durable. He's en route to a DEO holding facility until we can decide what to do with him.

    Alex watched her sister fidget for a moment, first gripping her wrist briefly before fishing in her neckline for something, quickly losing whatever semblance of calm she had regained after waking up in a panic.

    “Where's my necklace?” She demanded, frantically feeling around her neckline and under her suit looking for it.

    “Calm down Kara, the techs had to take it off to do a scan,” Alex said placatingly, knowing how important the necklace was but not wanting to cause Kara undue distress.

    She quickly made her way over to the other side of the room, fingers reaching for the necklace, but freezing before she picked it up. It was, noticeably, not Kara's mother's necklace like Alex had been expecting, but instead, Kara's own house crest, just as it appeared on her suit, cast in metal.

    “What is this?” Alex asked, equal parts confused and demanding, holding the necklace out so Kara could see it, “Where is your mother's?”

    Kara didn't respond immediately, instead holding her hand out until Alex passed the necklace over to her.

    “I couldn't exactly wear my Mother's necklace out and about while I am Supergirl,” Kara explained as she clasped the chain behind her neck, “It wasn't made to withstand that sort of abuse. I had this made when I became Supergirl, something that would remind me who I was and where I came from, so I could still be connected to my family without risking my most precious possession.”

    Alex eyed Kara warily. What she had said certainly made sense, but as with a lot of things when it came to her sister lately, she felt as if she wasn't getting the full picture. What, exactly, was going on, she wasn't sure, but now wasn't the right time. She would let Kara, who had almost fallen back asleep immediately upon putting her necklace back on, rest under the lamps a bit more, then there would be time to straighten a few things out.

    A few hours later, Kara had awoken again, and after running her through a battery of tests, Alex was assured that she was mostly recovered. However, determined not to easily let her sister off the hook for the unexplained happenings that had been going on lately, she had insisted that they have an impromptu sister night, her tone giving Kara no choice but to accept.

    Back at her apartment, Alex watched her sister as she finished up the last pizza. The more she thought about everything that had happened over the last few months, the more questions she was starting to have. Nothing was lining up quite right, and she wanted to get to the bottom of it.

    Noticing her intense look, her sister looked up at her, giving her a disarming smile, “So, what did you want to do tonight? There are a few movies I've been meaning for us to watch.”

    “We aren't watching any more Disney movies, Kara, we've seen all of them,” Alex said, trying not to let a smile poke through at Kara's summoned look of outrage, “No, I'd like to talk.”

    Kara's face didn't change, but that in and of itself was revealing enough to Alex that she knew Kara was nervous.

    “Sure,” her sister said, “Are you and Maggie doing alright? I know you guys haven't had a ton of time together lately.”

    “Maggie and I are fine, I'd rather talk about you.”

    At this, Kara's composure cracked slightly as she swallowed a little too hard to be normal, “Okay, Yeah. Sure.”

    “I just need things to make sense, Kara, and nothing is lining up. I need you to tell me what is going on with you. You escaped CADMUS in a way that should be impossible, even for you. And the necklace, Kara. I've seen you looking at it before, every time you get upset, you stare at it like it alone holds the answers to your woes. I've never even seen you look at your Mother's necklace that way. Then, the way you reacted today when Lena was in danger. That was too much, Kara, even for you. I mean, you nearly fried James for merely suggesting that Lena couldn't be trusted.”

    Honestly, Alex had expected Kara to yell at her, to get defensive and shout, but instead, she just looked sad, and Alex, despite everything she knew about her sister, couldn't fathom why.

    “She's my friend, Alex,” Kara said eventually, looking as if she would've liked to say something else instead, though what that was Alex couldn't guess.

    “You haven't been friends in years, Kara. Don't you think it is a bit sudden that Lena came back into your life?”

    “What are you implying, Alex,” Kara said, the fire Alex had been originally expecting now making an appearance.

    “Don't you think that it is pretty convenient that Lena shows up in National City, and less than two weeks later CADMUS announces themselves to the world? Don't you think it's at least a little suspicious that the very first thing she does upon coming back to the city is to resume her friendship with you, one of the only people in the city that poses a significant threat to CADMUS's operations? It seems an awfully lot like she already knows who you are.”

    “Of course she doesn't,” Kara replied testily.

    “It seems to me,” Alex bulldozed on, ignoring Kara's objection entirely, “like Lena is working with her mother at CADMUS, and she is using your relationship to take advantage of you to get away with it.”

    In her entire life, Alex could count on one hand how many times she had ever been as scared as she felt in that moment. Once, and it was happening right now as Kara was looking at her with heat threatening to glimmer in her eyes.

    “You have no idea what you are talking about, Alex,” Kara said fiercely, though Alex got the distinct impression that Kara was being a lot calmer than she wanted to be, ”I was friends with Lena for years before everything with Lex happened. You have no idea how hard it was for her, every day, to deal with the hate she got for something completely beyond her control. She didn't ask to be born a Luthor, and I refuse to judge someone by the circumstances of their birth. I judge Lena by what I know of her character. She is one of the kindest, most goodhearted people I know, so maybe instead of suspecting her of every act her family perpetrates, you could trust that I know what I'm talking about.”

    Without waiting for her response, Kara turned and walked to the door, only stopping when Alex said meekly behind her, “I'm just trying to protect you, Kara.”

    “I know,” Kara said without turning around, “but I don't need protection, not from Lena.”

    With that, Kara was gone, the door clicking closed behind her, and Alex was completely at a loss to understand what had happened. Kara hadn't yelled, and some part of her wished she had, but this cold, quiet fury had been much worse.

    From what Kara had said, it sounded as if she and Lena had been much better friends than Alex had thought, less acquaintances and much more best friends. Part of her knew that there was a good chance that Kara was right, but part of her couldn't bring herself to completely let go of the need she felt to protect Kara, even from herself. However, for her sister's sake, Alex would try.

    Kara was still testy by the time she made it home, and while some part of her knew that if she didn't tone it down, Alex would put it together eventually, part of her could not be bothered to care. It was starting to feel like Alex might never let off on Lena and it would be better to just come out now and take whatever may come of it rather than wait and hope.

    As soon as she walked through the door, though, she forgot about Alex entirely as her eyes met Lena's. Instead, all the worry and anxiety she had felt over the past day came rushing back at her.

    In a blur, she was in front of Lena, cupping her face in her hands and looking deep into her eyes, looking for any signs of pain or disquiet.

    “I'm okay,” Lena said before Kara could even ask.

    “I was so worried,” Kara replied.

    “You were worried?” Lena asked incredulously, “You're the one that nearly died.”

    “They took you,” Kara said, her voice tremulous and nearly broken, “and I wasn't fast enough to stop them.”

    “It's okay, darling. I'm here. I'm safe.”

    Reasonably, Kara knew this. Lena was right in front of her. Her heartbeat was thrumming steadily in her ears, her skin felt warm and soft beneath her hands, she could even smell the floral shampoo wafting from Lena's still-damp hair, but it was one thing to know it, and it was something else entirely to feel it.

    Lena had been taken from her, and the feelings that had elicited within her were not so easily dismissed. Leaning down, she kissed Lena, perhaps more harshly than needed, but Lena didn't complain, only opening her mouth to Kara so their tongues could meet in the middle.

    Feeling Lena's lips against hers, feeling Lena's tongue rub over hers helped, but it wasn't enough. She tried holding Lena firmly against her, peppering her face with kisses, sucking marks into the skin of her neck, but still it wasn't enough.

    She hoisted Lena up, walking her back to the bedroom, stripping both their clothes and pressing their bodies together in as many places as she could, but it wasn't enough, and Kara was starting to fear that nothing would ever be enough.

    Finally, hours later, after a long while feeling Lena writhing beneath her tongue and pulsing around her fingers, after kissing Lena through orgasm after orgasm, after Lena had pulled her close, running her hands through her hair, starting up a rhythmic, repetitive chant of, 'I'm here, I'm okay, I'm safe,' did Kara start to feel even the least bit better.

    Chapter End Notes

    So, as per usually my thoughts on this chapter. Does anyone buy the fact that Kara didn't know James was Guardian? I certainly don't. There are dozens of ways she could have found out if she wanted to, and that's all assuming that the voice modulator worked in the first place.

    I would like it known that every time that Lois shows up in this fic is 100% to be accredited to my fiancée. She read some of my early drafts of the first few chapters and she liked the way I wrote Lois so much that I included her as often as I could just for her (Also it's an excuse to have Kal show up for even more drama. Who doesn't love more Drama?)

    I believe I mentioned this in the opening notes of chapter 1, but some of the events of season 2 are happening slightly out of order, and this was one of them.

    So, the main thing that I've been thinking about lately, and I think is well exemplified in both this chapter and the fic as a whole (Honestly I think this is a headcanon that grew out of this fic that I will take forward into most of the rest of my fics from here on in) is that Kara isn't normal for a human, and she isn't supposed to be, especially when it comes to her emotions. I've mentioned this somewhere, maybe in a comment, but it feels like watching the show sometimes, and in other's fics, that Kara is just a human, but stronger. This isn't a criticism that I am trying to levy at anyone, though it is somewhat of a known thing throughout a lot of sci-fi, that aliens aren't all that alien. I've just accepted that Kara is extremely human presenting (I grew up on star trek, so I don't really have any issue with very human looking aliens, even if it is potentially unrealistic). I do like to change her up a bit culturally. One of my big ideas coming into this fic is that Kryptonians are life-long monogamists. When I say that, I mean they mate for life. I don't have any issue with it being a life-long polygamy instead of a monogamy, the important part is once they have found their partner(s) it is for life. I do think that Kryptonian society would have heavily favored monogamy however.

    I've touched on this earlier, and like the other stuff with Kara, it will come up again, but I've always viewed Krypton as a sort of emotionally repressed place. Not like the vulcans where they are completely suppress their emotions, but just where the so called "primal" instincts are frowned upon and the more "intellectual" parts of society and emotions are more positively viewed. However, Kara kinda taps back into those feelings that most Kryptonians suppressed, the most relevant for this fic is the protectiveness of their partner, which is deeply ingrained into them due to the nature of their relationships (It's a biological thing, not just a societal/cultural one). Hence, super duper extremely protective Kara

    It's sort of hard to put all this into descriptive words, I think it is all better seen by reading the way I write Kara. The thing about Lena, which I love, is that she meets Kara step for step when it comes to these things. There is an argument to be made that having someone like Kara being so protective would be irritating/annoying/concerning, and in real life I would probably agree, but fuck real life, this is fiction and I love this dynamic. For those reasons, you will most likely never find me writing a fic about Lena feeling stifled or anything like that when it comes to Kara's behavior. They meet and match each other perfectly. If someone else wants to write about that, they certainly can, it's just not for me.

    Another thing you will never catch me writing is something happening to Lena while Kara is in the same city and Kara not even noticing. This one I think is rather rare, and I can only think of a few examples, but with how I view Kara as a character and how I headcanon her powers as working it would never happen. Like in this chapter, bad things can happen to Lena while Kara is near by, but only because she is too slow to respond, not because she didn't notice (or if an outside reason caused her to not notice, like her hearing being incapacitated/blocked).

    Also, a few little side notes, why does Lena always let Lillian and Lex get under her skin? I refuse (at least when Lena is emotionally stable like in this fic). Heaven help me I love the protective pose Kara takes over Lena.

    Anyway, I've somehow written another huge note. I just love this fic so much that I want to go over every little detail and hold them all up so people can appreciate them as much as I do (Even though I know I should probably just let them be so everyone can pick them up naturally). If you ever have any questions about anything, feel free to throw them in the comments and I will gladly do my best to give you an answer/explanation

    Chapter 7

    Chapter Notes

    Well, I meant to post this chapter earlier today, but I completely forgot. Whoops. Anyway, like I said, I think I want to upload on Fridays.

    After dropping some rather large notes in the last chapter, I fell rather strange about not having much to say this time. Oh well, we are officially past the half way point now, so lets just get down to it then.

    Kara paced back and forth restlessly. She had been out on patrol when the DEO had reported a disturbance at Al's. By the time Kara had arrived, every alien in the bar had been dead, except Mon-El, who was now in critical condition. Alex had determined that it was most like some form of biological agent, and as a result, ordered Kara into immediate quarantine.

    So, she had been relegated to sitting in a clean room in the DEO, waiting impatiently for the results to come back as to whether or not she had been infected. Alex had sent J’onn for Eliza, a renowned astrobiologist, for help analyzing whatever agent had caused this outbreak.

    Kara was sure that CADMUS was behind the attack, but until they could isolate the causative agent and determine why all the aliens at the bar had died while the humans present went unaffected, she was to stay put. After several hours, the door behind her opened, and she turned to see Eliza and Alex walk in.

    The argument she and Alex had a few days previously still fresh in her mind, Kara gave Alex a hard look before turning to Eliza, who looked between her daughters before letting the issue drop for the more pressing matters currently at hand.

    “We've isolated the causative agent,” Eliza started, “it's a virus of some sort, but its composition is unlike anything I've ever seen. It isn't transmissible organism-to-organism, only through a short-lived weaponized aerosol, but it has somehow been engineered to target anything that isn't Kryptonian or Human. It's the only reason that Mon-El is still alive, because of his biological similarities to Kryptonians.”

    “Humans makes sense,” Kara said, “But why would CADMUS program a virus that wouldn't affect Kryptonians?”

    “That's the thing. The coding in the virus seems to have two separate origins. The code that protected humans was of terrestrial origin, but the virus itself, I believe was designed on Krypton,” Eliza replied gently

    “A Kryptonian Virus?” Alex asked disbelievingly, “How did CADMUS even get their hands on it?”

    “That's what they needed my blood for,” Kara said, “They must have used it to somehow spoof the Fortresses Biometric Identification protocols.”

    “I need you to see if you can find the file they took. I can't develop a cure, not just from what I managed to isolate from Mon-El, and he won't last much longer without one.”

    “Okay,” Kara agreed, “I'll go now and see what I can find.”

    As she walked by, Alex looked like she was going to say something, but Kara brushed past her before she had the opportunity. Heading straight to the DEO balcony, she took off without a backward glance and flew straight for the fortress, accelerating sharply once she cleared the last populated land.

    Setting down in the atrium of the fortress, Kara could immediately tell something was amiss. Normally, Kelex would be waiting to greet her as soon as she arrived, but this time, nothing.

    Finally, after a few long minutes, Kelex appeared, but his interfaces were all red, indicating he was in alert mode.

    “Kelex, status,” Kara queried.

    Kara received no response other than a stinging blast from Kelex's defensive weaponry.

    “There is an intruder in the Fortress,” Kelex alerted angrily.

    “Kelex, it's me. Kara Zor-El,” Kara shouted, but to no avail.

    Dodging the next few shots, Kara made her way to cover.

    “Kelex,” She commanded in Kryptonese, “System reboot.”

    Kara heard the tell-tale signs of Kelex shutting down, and by the time she had made her way back, he had returned to his normal, helpful self.

    “My apologies, Kara Zor-El. It appears my programming was tampered with,” Kelex informed her, his speech program returning to its regular soothing tones.

    “Don't sweat it, Kelex. Someone came here recently and accessed a file from the archives. I need to see that file.”

    “At once, Kara Zor-El. Pulling up the file, codename: Project Medusa”

    Rather than a normal information file, like Kara had been expecting, Kara found herself face-to-face with a projection of her father.

    “Father,” She said, reverting to Kryptonese out of reflex.

    “Hello, Kara. What do you wish to know?”

    Kara knew it was only a memory crystal, not truly her father, but the shock of seeing him still caused her words to falter, “I, uh, I need... What is Project Medusa?”

    “Medusa, a weaponized virus I created for the defense of Krypton.”

    “Wait, you- a virus you created?”

    Kara felt no small amount of shock at the revelation. To this day, she had still struggled with the knowledge that her own mother had been the one to sentence Astra to Fort Rozz, and now to find out that her father had been no better. It reminded her of Lena, who still struggled to overcome the stigma of her family's name. If Lena could persevere despite the sins of her family, then so could Kara.

    “It was a joint partnership between the Military and Science Guilds. We bio-engineered the virus to attack non-kryptonian physiology. In the event of an invasion, Medusa could be deployed to kill the alien combatants while leaving Kryptonian civilians and infrastructure safe from harm.”

    “You told me your work saved lives,” Kara accused, even though this was merely a recreation of her father and not the real man.

    “I was saving lives. Kryptonian lives. It was the perfect weapon,” her father's hologram said, with a smug emphasis that reminded her a little too much of the way that Lillian spoke of humans.

    Angrily shutting off the hologram, Kara turned back to Kelex, “Prepare me a data crystal with all information pertaining to Project Medusa.”

    With the crystal in hand, Kara made her way back to the DEO. She found Eliza and Alex, side-by-side in the lab, working valiantly to try and make a cure.

    “Eliza,” Kara said, as she entered the lab, “How's Mon-El doing?”

    “His immune system is fighting hard, but without a cure, he won't last long,” she replied.

    “Here is everything I found at the fortress,” Kara said, handing over the data crystal, “You should be able to use it to synthesize a cure.”

    “We'll get started right away.”

    Although Kara supposed she might've been of some help, she decided it might be better to let them handle it, especially considering her own father had been the one to design it, she felt the further away she was from it the better.

    “Someone needs to go question Lena,” Alex said, momentarily distracting Kara from the thoughts of her family.

    “I'll go,” Kara said, trying and failing to tamp down her frustration with her sister, ”I know her, I bet I could get her to tell me what she knows.”

    Alex looked at her for a long moment, clearly debating whether or not she could trust her seeming willingness to actually question Lena, but eventually, she seemed to make up her mind, “Fine, let us know if she has anything pertinent to say.”

    As Kara walked out of the lab, Alex turned back to focus on her monitor, valiantly attempting to ignore her mother's eyes boring into the side of her head.

    “So,” Eliza said, clearly unaffected by Alex's displeasure at the coming conversation, “Care to tell me why your sister is so upset with you.”

    “Not really,” Alex replied, peering closer at the molecular structure of an unfamiliar amino acid.

    “Alexandra,” Eliza chided, finally pulling Alex's attention away from the screen.

    “I may have implied that Lena was using their friendship to further her own nefarious purposes,” Alex said casually, before continuing under Eliza's unimpressed glare, “Okay, fine, I didn't imply it, I stated it outright. And now Kara is mad not only because I said it, but also because she thinks I don't trust her.”

    “Hmmmm,” Eliza replied, not actually using a word, but leaving Alex with the distinct impression that she was disappointed nonetheless as she returned to her work.

    Turning back to her own work, Alex set the DEO mainframe to the task of breaking down and analyzing some of the unfamiliar amino acids that Medusa was composed of while she turned her attention to trying to reverse engineer how CADMUS likely intended to weaponize Medusa.

She had no intention of questioning Lena for something she knew her wife had nothing to do with, but at least this way she could spare Lena from having Alex or some other DEO agents questioning her. She would admit, however, that the news she had just learned about her father was laying heavily on her, and Lena was the perfect person to talk to about it.

    Setting down at L-Corp, the sound of her boots on the balcony caused Lena to look up.

    “Darling,” Lena greeted, a smile splitting her face, “I wasn't expecting you.”

    Kara didn't respond, just taking a moment to take her wife in.

    “Darling?” Lena asked, the smile slipping off her face, replaced by concern, “Zhao, what is it, what's wrong?”

    “You heard about the attack?” Kara asked, waiting for Lena's nod before continuing, “Well, we managed to isolate it, and it was Kryptonian in nature. My father made it.”

    “Oh, Zhao,” Lena said, closing the distance between them and reaching up to cup one of Kara's cheeks, “You of all people should know that your family doesn't define you. You were the one that taught me that.”

    “I know,” Kara said, letting Lena tilt her head down so their foreheads were resting together, “That doesn't mean that it didn't hurt to learn. First Astra told me that my own mother had used me to capture her, then my father tells me that he made the virus. You should've heard him Lena, he was so proud of it, the perfect weapon he called it. The way he talked about it saving lives, Kryptonian lives, That isn't what Krypton stood for, or at least not the Krypton I remember.”

    Kara wasn't sure how long they stood there, foreheads together, before the crackle of her comm broke her out of it.

    “Kara,” Alex said, “We've identified the last component CADMUS needs to weaponize Medusa on a mass scale. It's a binding and dispersal agent called isotope 454. It's made exclusively by L-Corp.”

    “I'm still here,” Kara replied, “I'll make sure they don't get their hands on it.”

    “What is it?” Lena asked.

    “Isotope 454, CADMUS needs it to weaponize Medusa. They are-”

    Before Kara could finish her sentence, an alert went off on Lena's computer. Quickly making her way over to it, her wife looked up at her.

    “It's a security alert,” Lena said, “One of the sublevel storage rooms. That's where isotope 454 is stored.”

    “I'll take care of it,” Kara said, making her way back to the balcony, “You stay here, and stay safe.”

    Making her way down to the lobby, Kara managed to intercept Hank before he could escape.

    Eying the case in his hand, she looked up and smirked into his dented face, “That doesn't belong to you.”

    Dropping the case without a second thought, Hank growled angrily as he launched himself at her, unleashing a ferocious barrage of attacks. Kara easily dodged the first few strikes, blocking those that she couldn't avoid. Unlike the first time they had fought, when Hank and been cold and precise in his attacks, this time the blows came with a fiery intensity that made him sloppy.

    Less than a minute into the fight, Kara managed to pin both his arms to the sides, delivering a headbutt directly to Hank's unprotected forehead, sending him flying across the lobby into the opposite wall. While he was dazed, she sped over to the case carrying the isotope 454 and quickly hid it out of sight.

    "Too bad your buddy Metallo isn't here to help you out,” Kara said tauntingly as Hank staggered back to his feet.

    “I don't need his help to defeat you, alien scum,” Hank snarled back at her.

    “Your face says otherwise,” Kara said with faux sweetness, gesturing to her own undented cheek.

    With a bellow of rage, Hank sped back to her, throwing blow after blow in quick succession, sloppier than ever. Compared to fighting Alex in the DEO training rooms, this felt like child's play.

    Landing another blow that sent Hank sprawling towards the entrance to the lobby, Kara prowled after him unconcernedly, when suddenly his eyes started glowing. She easily caught the blast with her cape, but when she looked back, Hank was gone.

    “That's new,” she said to herself.

    She could likely find him before he went completely dark, but securing L-Corp and isotope 454 was the top priority. Returning to where she had stashed the case mid-fight, she made her way back up to Lena's office, unlocking the secured door with the hidden biometric panel before walking in.

    “He's gone,” Kara informed Lena, who was looking her over anxiously as she set the case on her desk, “You can lift the lockdown.”

    “Are you okay?” Lena asked, keying a few panels on her computer to end the alert, “the last few times you fought him-”

    “He was never a threat without kryptonite,” Kara broke in, “I'm fine, not even a scratch.”

    Lena's eyes ran over her one more time, as if to validate her claim before they fell to the case Kara had set on her desk, “You said this is the last ingredient CADMUS needs to weaponize Medusa?”

    “Yes, why?” Kara asked.

    “Because,” Lena answered, a devious smile on her face, “I have a plan.”

    “I have what you want.”

    Lena waited for a response, but none came. After a few moments, the call ended, and she thought that maybe Lillian wouldn't take the bait, but before long her phone dinged with an incoming message, an address.

    Without delay, Lena tucked the case beneath her arms and headed out.

    The address led her to a warehouse down near one of National City's many ports. Slipping inside, Lena was met with a CADMUS goon. Following him through the sprawl of the warehouse, she found herself in a room deep within its depths where her mother was waiting for her.

    “Lena,” Her mother greeted coolly.

    “Mother,” Lena returned impassively.

    Lena felt no less hate for Lillian than she had the previous time they met, but she couldn't allow it to show. If her ploy was to work, she needed Lillian to believe her, so she buried it as deeply as she could.

    “I'll cut straight to the chase, Lena. Why should I trust you, when you clearly have a soft spot for that Kryptonian.”

    The way Lillian said Kryptonian, as if it was the worst insult she could imagine, made the rage twist back into her heart, but rather than push it down, Lena used it.

    “Because you were right,” Lena said, letting the venom drip into her voice, “When I told her I was a Luthor by blood, she turned on me. She said she could look past me being a Luthor by adoption, but sharing their blood was 'too much' for her to overlook. She showed me the rotten, fetid core of her kind. Earth would be better off without them.”

    The words tasted foul and bitter in her mouth, but they seemed to do the trick, for Lillian smiled at her, a truly gleeful, horrid thing.

    “Very well. Let's get started.”

    Kara landed back on the balcony of the DEO. Her wife had a plan to stop Lillian, all Kara had to do was keep the DEO from interfering until Lena was done. She quickly made her way over to where her mother and J'onn were conversing.

    “Any progress on the cure?” She asked.

    “Nothing so far,” Elize replied.

    “There must be something on the data crystal that could help,” Kara said.

    “Most of the information we've uncovered so far has to do with creating and disseminating the virus. I don't think they ever considered a cure,” J'onn informed her.

    Kara tried not to let that particular fact bother her, instead focusing on the conversation at hand as J'onn kept talking, “What about Lena?”

    “What about her?” Kara said testily, turning her ire on J'onn.

    “Her mother synthesized the virus,” He said, completely unphased by Kara's anger, “If Lena is helping her-”

    “Lena wouldn't do that,” Kara said before he could finish.

    “Would you stake Mon-El's life on that,” J'onn asked, his tone firm but non-accusatory.

    “I looked into Lena's eyes,” Kara said, sparing her mother a brief glance, “She has nothing to do with this.”

    Before anything else could be said, an alert in the command center drew their attention. They all made their way to Brainy's station, Alex joining them as they all clustered around the Coluan.

    “What is it Brainy?” J'onn asked.

    “The radiological alarm,” Brainy informed them. I set an alert to detect the radioactive signature of isotope 454 ever left L-Corp.”

    “And let me guess,” Alex broke in, “now it has.”

    “Indeed. It appears that CADMUS is getting ready to release Medusa at the Port of National City.”

    “Get a strike team ready,” Alex ordered, “Let's go.”

    “No,” Kara said, causing everyone to look at her in alarm.

    “Supergirl, we don't have time,” Alex said, "if we wait, CADMUS will wipe out all alien life in the city, maybe across the whole globe.”

    As if to reinforce her point, the disembodied mask CADMUS always used in their videos appeared across all the screens in the DEO, announcing their intention to reclaim Earth and its future for humanity.

    Alex opened her mouth, undoubtedly to utter an 'I told you so', but Kara beat her to the punch, “I left Isotope 454 in Lena's hands. If she gave it to CADMUS, that means she has a plan, and-”

    “Or she could be helping them,” Alex broke in.

    Leveling her sister with a pointed look, Kara continued, “I trust her. If she has a plan, then she needs the time to pull it off, and we need to give her that time. The question is, do you trust me?”

    Alex gave her a hesitant look. Clearly, she wasn't fully on board, but under the weight of the gaze Eliza was now giving her, she finally gave in.

    “Fine. Do you know what this plan is, since you seem to know so much about her?” She said, definitely less than happy with this turn of events.

    “No, I don't,” Kara lied, “but I have faith that Lena won't let anything catastrophic happen. We just need to be ready to spring the trap closed at the right time. I'll go ahead, wait for my signal.”

    Walking back to the balcony, Kara couldn't help but feel a surge of hope at the fact that Alex might finally be starting to come around. Surely helping to stop Lillian once and for all would gain Lena some goodwill in Alex's books.

    When she arrived at the port, she stayed out of sight, but lingered close enough that she could intervene when needed.

    Nearby, she could hear Lena and her mother talking.

    “What are we doing here?” Lena asked.

    We are going to carry out our mission,” Lillian said, and Kara could hear the sound of a cloth tarp being pulled off something as she spoke, “This will end the alien scourge, once and for all.”

    “A rocket?” Lena asked incredulously, a single brow creeping up into her hairline.

    “Indeed. With Medusa, Lex's dispersion device, and the Isotope 454 you provided, the earth's atmosphere will be rendered forever uninhabitable for all alien life. Earth will finally be freed of this infestation. And you'll be the one to do it.”

    “What?” Lena asked, clearly shocked.

    “Prove you're with me. Unleash Medusa and end Earth's alien menace once and for all.”

    Hearing Lena's heart rate speed up, Kara figured that was her cue.

    Flying up and around so it looked like she was coming from the city, she flew straight towards them, landing directly on top of Hank and rendering him unconscious, causing both women to turn around at the sound.

    “Supergirl,” Lillian said smugly, “You're too late.”

    “Don't do this Lena, you are better than this,” Kara said, completely ignoring Lillian.

    “No, I'm not,” Lena spat at her hatefully, “Or don't you remember? I'm a Luthor. Isn't that what you said? You said you could never trust me, and I guess you were right.”

    Kara knew that it was an act, but even so, the words and tone hurt, and that hurt must have shown on her face, because Lillian's smirk deepened.

    “Do it, Lena,” She ordered.

    With a quick twist of her wrist, Lena turned the key in the launcher's control panel.

    “What have you done?” Kara asked.

    "We've won. Soon you and all your like will be dead, and earth will be free of your menace,” Lillian replied with maniacal glee as strange glowing orange particulates began to rain down around them.

    Lillian looked away to survey her handiwork, and Kara took the opportunity to silently signal Alex over her comms. After a few moments, Lillian looked back at her, and the glee melted off her face to be replaced by a dawning look of horror.

    “You should be dead,” She said, looking around confusedly, “They should all be dead.”

    The confusion leaked off her face just as quickly as it had come, replaced this time by a look of realization as Lena stepped up to her side and half a dozen DEO SUVs arrived on the scene.

    “You,” Lillian said, “You switched out the isotope. You made the virus inert.”

    “I did,” Lena said smugly, looking over at Alex, who had arrived just in time to witness Lillian's accusation, “And I called the police.”

    “They'll never trust you, you know. To them, you'll always be a Luthor, it doesn't matter what you do,” Lillian said, one last barb to try and dig under Lena's skin.

    “That's where you're wrong mother,” Lena said with a smirk as Lillian's words bounced ineffectually off her armor.

    Kara watched amusedly as Lena turned her back on her mother and walked away. If only Lillian knew how much she trusted Lena. If only she knew that Lena had known both her and Kal's secret identities for years, that Kara had willingly offered her life to Lena, had gone so far as to kneel at Lena's feet as she held a kryptonite knife to her throat and offered no resistance, and Lena had done nothing. She would love to see the look on her face if she did.

    Looking back at her sister, she saw Alex's eyes trained on Lena's retreating form as she tucked Lillian into the back of one of the cars beside an unconscious Hank. Alex had heard Lillian's admission that Lena had tricked her, but she had clearly also heard Lena's smug claim to Kara's trust, and the two new pieces of information were warring within her head, and Kara was unsure which would win out.

    Unfortunately, she didn't have to wait long to find out. After the majority of the DEO agents had left, leaving only Alex and a few others, Brainy had joined them to help analyze the evidence left behind.

    As Kara had been helping some agents shift the launcher that had fired the Medusa-laden rocket, she heard Lena's name as Alex was talking to Brainy.

    “... Lena. It just seems too good to be true. I mean, she is a Luthor? Am I the only one that remembers that. You should've heard the way she all but told Lillian that Kara trusted her implicitly.”

    “Genetics is not causation,” Brainy said flatly, “Considering that Lena is only Lex's half-sibling, and has no blood relation to Lillian, the simple fact of sharing a name does not indicate guilt. And, after analyzing Kara's behavior, I would agree with Lena's conclusion that Kara trusts her implicitly. Furthermore, taking into account Lena's actions, I am inclined to agree with Kara.”

    “Well, I don't like it,” Alex stated plainly, “They may not be full siblings, but they grew up in the same house, and we have no way of knowing that Lena wasn't molded by it the same way Lex was.”

    Having finished loading the launcher, Kara made her way quickly to where her sister and Brainy were talking, rounding the corner before Brainy could reply to Alex's latest remark.

    “It's a good thing Lena was here,” Kara said, breaking off their earlier conversation, “A lot more people might've gotten hurt if it wasn't for her.”

    Brainy looked back and forth between the sisters for a moment, the tension between them so thick that not even he could miss it, before excusing himself to another task and leaving them by themselves.

    “I know you were listening, Kara,” Alex said, clearly displeased.

    “I thought you trusted me,” Kara replied, neither confirming nor denying Alex's statement.

    “I do trust you, Kara, but I can't trust her.”

    “She just saved the day, Alex. What more do you want from her?”

    “Yes, she did,” Alex agreed, “But I don't know why she did it. Did she do it to get in our good graces, or was it just to get her mother out of the way so she could assume control of CADMUS? Until I understand her motivations, Kara, I will not blindly trust her.”

    “Have you ever considered that she did it because she is a good person who wants to do the right thing,” Kara demanded, her hope crumbling in her chest at Alex's words.

    “Of course I considered it,” Alex said, “But it's not an assumption I am willing to make. I don't know why you refuse to admit that she might-”

    “I don't know why you are so keen to blame her without any proof,” Kara shouted, her anger starting to get the best of her.

    “I shouldn't need proof, Kara. She is a Luthor,” Alex said, completely unphased by Kara's anger, now that she had the certainty that she was defending Kara from a threat that she had lacked previously, “You clearly aren't going to be objective when it comes to her, so I will do what you can't until you are ready to see the truth.”

    “Does that mean I am a bad person?” Kara asked, taking Alex by surprise.

    “Of course not, Kara,” Alex replied, albeit with no small amount of confusion at Kara's new direction.

    “Why not? After all, I'm a Zor-El,” Kara said sardonically, “My mother, Alura Zor-El threw her sister in prison when she tried to save our planet, and she used me, her own child, to do it. My father, Zor-El, created the virus that Lillian nearly used to wipe out all aliens on Earth. If you condemn Lena for her family's actions, why don't you condemn me for mine?”

    “Because I know you are a good person, Kara. I can't say the same for Lena,” Alex answered her after a long moment.

    “Well I can. I have looked into her eyes. I know she is a good person. What I don't know, is why you refuse to believe me.”

    “Because you are the one who would die if you are wrong.”

    Kara looked at her sister, knowing the hard expression on Alex's face was undoubtedly mirrored on her own. She should have known better than to get her hopes up when it came to Alex trusting Lena. Alex would never back down, not when it came to a perceived threat to her safety. However, knowing that didn't mean that having that hope taken away hurt any less, nor that Alex's bullheadedness when it came to her wife made her any less angry. Without another word, Kara took off, cracking the concrete under her feet as she did so.

    Kara's eyes cracked open before the sun had even risen. The city had been completely silent in the nearly three weeks since Lillian's arrest, and with the holidays quickly approaching, Kara had been enjoying the quiet days spent with Lena in their home.

    She hadn't talked to her sister outside of work since their confrontation at the port, but she was considering reaching out today. It was Christmas, after all. But, before she did that, she wanted to spend as much time with Lena as she could.

    They had been out late the previous night celebrating the third anniversary of Kara proposing at one of the most expensive and exclusive restaurants in the city. Content to let Lena sleep, Kara laid holding her wife in her arms for nearly half and hour before slipping silently out of bed and padding her way to the kitchen. She enabled the soundproofing in the bedroom, not so much that she couldn't hear Lena's heartbeat, but just enough that any banging she made while preparing breakfast hopefully wouldn't wake her wife.

    She bustled around the kitchen peacefully for over an hour before she heard the tell-tale signs of Lena waking up. Quickly making her way back into the bedroom, she slipped back under the covers before Lena was fully awake, mindful of her promise never to let Lena wake up alone again.

    Lena's eyes flickered open, quickly focusing on Kara's face.

    “Good morning, darling,” Lena greeted sleepily, wrapping her arms around Kara's neck to pull her down into a soft kiss.

    “Good morning,” Kara returned, “and Merry Christmas.”

    “Merry Christmas,” Lena agreed, smiling up at her gleeful Kryptonian.

    “I made you breakfast,” Kara said, disappearing in a flash and reappearing with plate in hand, “it's an egg white omelet, with all your favorites, and yes, before you ask, I even put kale in it.”

    “Wow, you even used kale,” Lena teased, “You really know how to make a girl feel special.”

    “You better,” Kara said, wrinkling her nose in faux disgust.

    Rolling her eyes playfully, Lena took a bite, nearly rolling her eyes for real at the flavor.

    “Kara, this is fantastic,” She said.

    Kara smiled at her wife. She had never been known for her culinary talent, especially when it came to making healthy foods, mostly skating by due to her pure ability to multitask and move at incredible speed, but she had been working on broadening her abilities now that she had Lena's diet to consider.

    “Thanks,” Kara said, nearly glowing at the praise, “I've been practicing.”

    “And who do I owe a nice meal for enduring your previous attempts?” Lena asked playfully, before raising her eyebrows at Kara's avoidant look, “Kara... did you eat kale for me?”

    Her wife asked it in such a soft and warm tone that Kara would've gladly endured eating kale for the rest of her life to hear her speak like that again.

    “I did. I had to make sure you'd like it,” Kara replied with a soft smile before her tone turned more teasing, “but don't expect anything else from me, this is your only present this year.”

    That was, of course, a lie. After they had both finished eating, Kara buried Lena in a deluge of gifts, outweighed only by the number of gifts Lena showered her with in return. Kara wasn't sure exactly how Lena had managed to sneak in and hide so many items from her, but she was starting to suspect that there might be a hidden fourth floor that she didn't know about.

    Later, once they had gotten through all their presents, they lounged around, content to simply spend time peacefully in each other's presence. Kara was sprawled out on the loveseat, Lena lying on top of her, both of them nearly fallen back asleep when an alert Kara had never heard before sounded.

    “Alex just entered the elevator in your apartment building,” Lena informed her after checking her tablet.

    “Okay,” Kara said, “I better get over there before she's waiting at my door. I love you.”

    “I love you too,” Lena replied.

    Leaning down for one last kiss, Kara made her way towards the balcony door.

    “I'll be back tonight,” She promised, smiling at her wife and receiving a smile in turn before making her exit.

    Quickly making the flight back to her apartment, Kara slipped through the window, shutting it behind her just as Alex knocked on the door.

    “Coming,” Kara called out, taking her time to walk over to the door.

    Opening the door, Kara put her best surprised face on as she greeted her sister, trying not to let her less-than-pleased feelings towards her sister shine through, “Alex, I wasn't expecting you. I figured you and Maggie would spend the day together.”

    “Kara,” Alex replied, looking as if Kara's attempt had been less than successful, “Maggie and I had a lovely morning together, but I thought you and I could spend the afternoon together. I know we've got... other stuff going on right now, but I'd like to spend Christmas with my sister, if that's okay.”

    “I can do that,” Kara said, feeling some of the tension she had failed to suppress melt away, “Come in.”

    “Thank you,” Alex said genuinely, “Would you mind if I invited Maggie over in a bit? She has no family that still talks to her, and I don't want to leave her alone for too long. Then I thought maybe we could invite Brainy and J'onn round for dinner.”

    Kara smiled through the painful irony of Alex not wanting to leave her girlfriend alone too long on Christmas while unknowingly forcing Kara to do the very same to her wife. But, Alex had requested her sister, and as much as Kara was tempted to deny her, she knew Lena wouldn't want her to do that purely on her behalf.

    “Of course,” Kara responded with as much joviality as she could muster, “Winn and James still visiting family?”

    “Yes,” Alex said, “But they sent me their presents for you.”

    “And Mon-El?”

    “Still recovering. Medusa did a real number on him.”

    As much as part of her was still angry with Alex for what she had said about Lena, she couldn't deny that she had missed her sister, and she was happy they were spending this time together.

    They decided to exchange their own presents first, before anyone else had arrived. In another twist of irony, one of the gifts Alex had presented her was a frame containing the very first article she had written earlier that year, one of the same presents, albeit with a different frame, that Lena had given her a few hours previously.

    When it was her turn, Kara had handed Alex her present, a completely inconspicuous, definitely non-bottle-shaped package that Alex had accepted with a smile. It was a bit of a tradition at that point. Everyone who knew Alex knew that she'd never be upset to receive a good bottle of scotch.

    However, upon opening it, Alex's jaw dropped. She'd never received a bottle quite this good as a gift before.

    "How did you afford this, Kara?” Alex asked, shock written all over her face as she looked up.

    “I'm getting paid a lot more as a reporter than I was as Cat's assistant,” Kara replied.

    Technically, it was true. She had gotten a noticeable raise in her salary, but it certainly hadn't hurt that Lena paid for the majority of their day-to-day expenses, freeing up Kara to be able to spend more lavishly on herself, not that Lena didn't already do that, and her friends and family.

    “Still, Kara. This must've cost a lot.”

    “It wasn't nearly as much as the bottle Lena brought to game night,” Kara said dismissively.

    Immediately, Kara regretted what she said. She had meant it to distract Alex from thinking too hard about the topic of her finances, but instead, all it did was remind them both of the tension that had defined their relationship for the past few weeks.

    Neither of them said anything for a few moments, until a knocking at the door broke them both out of it.

    “I'll get it,” Kara said unnecessarily, jumping to her feet and making her way to the door.

    “Hey Little Danvers,” Maggie said as she stepped into the apartment.

    “Hey Maggie,” Kara greeted her warmly.

    Making her way over to the couch where Alex still sat, Maggie looked at the bottle in her girlfriend's hand before letting out an appreciative whistle before waggling her eyebrows suggestively at Alex, “Damn, that's a nice bottle. Hope you're in a sharing mood, Danvers.”

    “With you,” Alex said, “Always.”

    Kara let out a relieved breath. Alex seemed sufficiently distracted by the arrival of her girlfriend, and the topic was dropped.

    After they had settled in, Kara could feel Maggie looking back and forth between them before she spoke, “I'm glad to see you two happy together again.”

    Kara smiled at her, “It is Christmas after all. It's a time for family.”

    While Maggie didn't know the underlying source of their argument, as it would be impossible to explain without first telling Maggie her secret, it was completely unsurprising that she had noticed the less-than-sisterly attitude between Kara and Alex as of late.

    “So it is,” Maggie agreed, her eyes crinkling sadly as she smiled.

    All things considered, Christmas Dinner had been a rather enjoyable experience, featuring Brainy imbibing far too much eggnog while thoroughly praising the beverage before promptly falling out of his chair, unconscious. Luckily, he had recovered rather quickly, and the dinner had gone on unperturbed.

    Alex and Maggie were the last to leave, but not before Alex loosely arranged a sister night between her and Kara. Once they had left at last, Kara quickly cleaned up before making her way back home to Lena.

    Lena was sitting on the same couch she had been when Kara left, looking almost as if nothing had changed since in the intervening time.

    “Darling,” Lena greeted her, “Did you have a good time?”

    “I did,” Kara replied, moving to sit down next to Lena and bringing their foreheads to rest together, “But I wish you could've been there.”

    Lena didn't respond, only shifted so Kara could lie down atop her, her head coming to rest directly over Lena's heart. With her stomach full of food, her ears full of Lena, and Lena's hand running soothingly through her hair, it didn't take long at all for Kara to fall asleep.

    Lena walked into her office a few days after New Year's, having let Kara sweet talk her into taking time off from before Christmas until after New Year's. The look of shock Jess had given her when she informed her assistant she was taking nearly two weeks off, even around the holidays, had given Kara's argument that she worked too much more credence than she would've liked.

    As she approached her desk, she noticed Kara had left something on her desk. It explained her wife's less-than-straightforward answer when she had asked where she had gone that morning. She recognized the box immediately. It was from her favorite pastry shop in Dublin, and right beside it were cappuccinos from Pavé in Milan.

    Though Kara was always thoughtful, it never ceased to amaze Lena when her wife did something like this, flying halfway across the world just to make her day a little bit brighter.

    Lena ate all the scones, a move she would no doubt regret later, but how could she not when Kara had gone all the way to Ireland to get them? Just as she finished, her intercom buzzed and her assistant's voice filled the room.

    “Miss Luthor. I have an Agent Danvers here to see you. Shall I send her in?”

    Lena suppressed a smile at Jess' tone. She knew that Jess was well aware of exactly who Alex was and what this visit would most likely entail, and it meant a great deal to her that Jess sounded ready to get physical with a government agent to prevent it.

    “Send her in.”

    Lena swiped the last of the crumbs off her desk, wanting to present a completely professional front to her wife's sister.

    “Alex. What can I do for you?” Lena said in greeting, her tone even, but not unfriendly.

    “Miss Luthor, I had a few questions for you, if you didn't mind. Just a few last-minute details about the incident with your mother,” Alex said, equally impassively.

    If Lena hadn't been dreading the likely true reason Alex had appeared, she might've been tempted to smirk. In different circumstances, Alex would've made a fantastic poker opponent.

    “Absolutely, whatever I can do to help.”

    “During the course of our investigation, we noticed Lillian, and by extension CADMUS, had been utilizing several L-Corp facilities and proprietary technologies. Do you have any idea how they gained access to these resources?” Alex asked, her implied meaning clear.

    “As a matter of fact, I do. Lillian used to be a board member at L-Corp, and when she resigned, she somehow retained access to many of our systems. That, along with several employees of the company at all levels who were still loyal to either her or Lex, allowed her access to the resources you mentioned. Ever since Kara told me Lillian's true allegiances, I have been systematically removing her access and stripping the company of all employees that assisted her in any way. My CFO, Sam, who works in Metropolis has been heading up the investigation. I could have her forward her findings and notes to you, or the agency you work for, the FBI was it?”

    At the mention of involving the FBI, or in reality the DEO, along with Lena's trademark eyebrow raise, the first crack in Alex's armor appeared. Panic flared briefly in her eyes before the mask of calm settled back over it. It didn't surprise Lena, however, who had already surmised that Alex wasn't here in any official capacity.

    “That won't be necessary,” Alex said, and Lena thought she picked up a hint of disappointment, as if she had been expecting Lena to simply confess.

    “Very well. Is there anything else I can help you with?” Lena asked, still waiting for the other shoe to drop.

    Lena watched as Alex puzzled with how to approach her next question. It was as if the woman forgot the disdain that had colored their previous interactions. The pretense of civility was frankly unneeded.

    “Allow me to cut to the chase,” Lena said after a few moments of silence, “I know you don't like me, and you don't like that your sister and I are friends, so why pretend otherwise, or that this is a work visit? Just ask me what you came to ask me.”

    “Fine,” Alex said, dropping the act completely, “You're right. I don't like you, and I don't trust you around my sister. I don't know if you are using her for her connection to Supergirl, or what your angle is. So tell me. Tell me why you came to National City. Tell me why Kara.”

    Lena looked at Alex for a few moments, allowing herself to gather her thoughts before she spoke. This truly was not an unexpected line of questioning, but that did nothing to diminish the aching feeling in her throat that it caused.

    “I came to National City to escape Lex's shadow. There was no way I could rebuild my family's name or company while Lex's crimes were looming over me. And yes, Supergirl did play a factor in my decision, but not in the way you think. In National City, instead of being just another Luthor, I could be the Luthor who shared her home with a Kryptonian. If we could work side-by-side, together, we could make the world a better place, not tear it apart while mindlessly continuing a feud that had nothing to do with us.

    “My brother was a madman. I do not deny that, nor do I excuse his actions. But I won't let what he did rule my life. My every action had been to repair the damage he has done, and that includes your sister. She and I were friends before Lex started to show who he truly was. Your sister is one of the kindest, warmest, best people I have ever met. Lex's madness drove a wedge between us that we couldn't overcome, but now he is out of the picture. Your sister is the best thing that ever happened to me, and Lex took that away. I just want my friend back.”

    It was as close to the truth as Lena could tell it without exposing her and Kara's relationship, and Lena could only hope that it was enough. She knew how heavily this all was weighing on her wife, and she wanted nothing more than to relieve that burden.

    Alex looked at her critically for a long moment, mulling over what she had said.

    "I hope what you are saying is true,” she said eventually, “For Kara's sake.”

    With that, Alex turned and walked out without so much as an apology or goodbye.

    Lena waited for a few minutes, watching through the security cameras until Alex had left the building before she tapped her intercom button, “Jess, when is my first meeting?”

    “Fifteen minutes,” came Jess's immediate reply.

    “Push it back thirty minutes, and give them my apologies for the delay.”

    “Of course, Miss Luthor.”

    That done, Lena checked the time. It was early, before Kara needed to be to work.

    “Kara,” She said into the empty room.

    A few moments later, Kara appeared at her side, blowing her hair out of her face with the speed of her arrival.

    “Lena, what is it, what's wrong?” Kara asked, scanning her face for any signs of trouble.

    “Your sister just paid me a visit.”

    “What?! Why?!” Kara demanded, looking ready to storm out of her office looking for a fight.

    “Exactly why you'd expect,” Lena replied, lightly grabbing Kara's wrist to keep her from going after her sister.

    “I'm sorry,” Kara said, wrapping her free arm around her wife.

    “It's okay,” Lena said, tugging lightly to lead Kara over to the couch in her office, “Come sit down so I can tell you about it?”

    Kara dutifully followed her, letting her recount the encounter in its entirety without pulling out of Lena's grip to go yell at Alex, as she was clearly tempted to do.

    “I'm sorry,” Kara said again once she had finished talking.

    “No, I'm sorry Kara, Lena replied, “If I wasn't who I was, you wouldn't have to deal with this.”

    “Don't say that,” Kara said, reaching out to tilt Lena’s chin up so she could look into her eyes, “Who you are is my favorite person in the whole world. There is nothing I would trade if it cost me you. As much as I love Alex, I love you more. You are my wife. Nothing will ever take precedence over that. Besides, I don't fault you for this at all. I fault Alex. If she wasn't so prejudiced against you, I would've told her a long time ago, and none of this would be happening.”

    “Thank you, Kara,” Lena said, collapsing into her wife's arms, “Thank you for never letting me forget.”

    “Always,” Kara whispered into her hair, pressing a kiss down after the word, as if trying to seal it in.

    Chapter End Notes

    I don't know about you guys, but I love slipping in little lines from the show into my fics. It just makes me happy for some reason, both to find them in other fics and to "hide" them in mine. Slightly relatedly, I wonder how many choices that authors (myself included) make that either don't really get noticed or are just dismissed as a minor thoughtless change when there was actually some modicum of intent behind it. For example, in this chapter, you may have noticed I diverged from the show in that Lena stayed in her office when hank broke in, so she was never near the fight to get a concrete logo thrown at her. This wasn't just some last minute something that I changed, or otherwise decided to remove without thought. It was in my outline long long before I ever wrote this chapter, and I quote, "Lena never gets shit thrown at her because she wisely waited the fuck where Kara told her too for once." I wrote that as a little funny jab, but at the same time there was definitely thought about it in terms of Kara would absolutely tell her wife to stay safe, as that is her priority, and I love writing a Lena that understands Kara's protectiveness rather than feeling like it chafes her freedom.

    Another example in this same scene, than some of you may not even realize I skipped (I wouldn't blame you if you totally blocked it out) but in this same episode Kara and Mon-El kiss when Mon-El briefly wakes from his coma. Now, I don't think anyone would blame me for not including that, but I didn't just erase it willy nilly like I could have, because while in no way do I ever want Mon-El to lay his lips on Kara, Mon-El is the one that initiated, not Kara. So, while my Kara also has no interest in ever kissing Mon-El, he still has interest in kissing her, so if she had been in the same situation as Kara was in canon, she either would have been kissed, or likely would have dodged it, because you know, she has a much hotter wife for kisses, and she has superspeed (seriously, why do people with superspeed get surprised by shit so often, he was literally in and out of a coma, I don't think it was exactly the speediest thing). So, Mon-El still wants the kiss (and I specifically didn't want to undo that, because possessive Lena), so instead I just removed Kara from the whole situation by having her stay with Lena the whole time, thus removing the possibility of the kiss ever happening.

    Anyway, I'd like to give Alex's stubborn ass a shoutout for driving the fic forward. I couldn't gave pulled this fic off without her.

    As always, feel free to let me know your thoughts or ask any questions you may have in the comments.

    Chapter 8

    Chapter Notes

    Well, here we are. The next few chapters are the real core of this fic. Everything has been building to this point. They are also three of the shortest chapters because a lot is going to go down in a short period of time.

    It had been two months since Lillian's arrest, and her trial had only just begun. There wasn't much they could do to influence the outcome one way or another, but it still had Kara jittery because she knew Lena would be called to the witness stand, and it had been weighing heavily on her wife as the date drew closer.

    However, for tonight, she was doing her best to push it aside. It was Alex's birthday, and they were all going out to Al's to celebrate. Well, all of Alex's friends that was. Doing her best not to let it drag her down, lest she ruin Alex's celebration, Kara bid Lena goodbye with a kiss and made her way to Al's.

    Finding she was the first to arrive, she staked them out a booth in the corner, ordering a water to sip while she waited. Alex and Maggie arrived next, zeroing in on Kara immediately and moving to join her. They were followed shortly thereafter by James, Winn, Brainy, and J'onn.

    Mon-El even made an appearance, having only recently gotten back on his feet after his near death from the Medusa Virus. The experience seemed to have changed him. He had stopped with a lot of the childish behavior he had displayed previously, and he even seemed like he was taking Kara's offers of training more seriously. He had also stopped throwing himself at her without subtlety, though he definitely was still expressing interest.

    Once everyone had been seated, except Mon-El, who in an interesting turn of events was not actually there for the party, but because he had gotten a job at Al's, Kara got up to fetch drinks for them from the bar. Luckily, Mon-El had seen her coming and started in on their orders, already knowing what they preferred.

    “It's the good stuff,” He informed her, indicating a glass of scotch, “For the Birthday girl, from me.”

    “Thanks, Mon-El,” Kara said, smiling warmly at the gesture.

    Making her way back to the table with the tray of drinks, Kara set them all down, making sure not to mix up Maggie's and Alex's scotch glasses. The night went well, all external circumstances considered. They drank, they played darts, Maggie organized a pool tournament, which Alex swept rather easily, and Mon-El even managed to pop over from time to time to share a joke or grab refills.

    All in all, despite having to dodge Mon-El's gaze when it lingered just a touch too long and doing her best to tune out James' comments at the replay of the day's progress of Lillian's trials, Kara was having a wonderful time. She and Alex had been doing well after Christmas, even in light of Alex's surprise visit to question Lena, but tonight felt like another step forward for them, almost back to how things had been before Lena had moved to National City.

    So, naturally, it was a perfect time for something to go wrong. Deep into the night, once everyone was truly a few drinks too deep, and even Kara, who was the hero on call for the night, was starting to feel a little tipsy at the light alien wine she had been sipping all night, all the DEO agents’ and Kara's phones had gone off in unison.

    Winn, James, and Maggie all noticing the interruption, turned to Kara for an explanation.

    “Lillian Luthor has escaped custody,” Kara informed them.

    Kara landed on the balcony of her and Lena's home, reluctantly making her way inside to tell her wife the news. She had gone to the prison that had been holding Lillian during her trial, but there had been no trail to follow. The escape had been quick and well executed. In addition, Metallo had also escaped, having been held at the same facility.

    The scene had left an odd prickling feeling over her skin. Both doors had been ripped completely off their hinges, allowing their prisoners to walk free. It could've easily been done by Hank, but Kara had a suspicion of what the tingling feeling meant. Somehow, Metallo had gotten another Kryptonite heart to fuel him.

    J'onn had shown up not long after her, seeming to have thrown off the effects of the alien alcohol through pure force of will alone, and had combed the scene for any further evidence before flying to the DEO to start trying to track them down as Kara went home for the night.

    Opening the door, Kara walked in to find Lena, as she often was, sitting on one of the couches with a book in hand. She smiled briefly at the image of her wife. She really should have been in bed, but without Kara there to reinforce the behavior, she never seemed to be able to keep it up.

    “Kara,” Lena greeted, the smile freezing on her face as her eyes landed on her wife, “What's wrong?”

    “Lillian escaped custody,” Kara replied.

    “I see,” Lena said, setting her book down.

    Seeing the serious look settle over her wife's face, Kara knew they were both thinking the same thing, but Kara wouldn't allow it, not again.

    “I won't let them lay another hand on you,” She promised, her voice low and rough.

    “I know, Zhao, I know,” Lena said, beckoning Kara join her on the couch.

    Unable and unwilling to deny Lena's summons, Kara made her way to her wife's side, sitting down and pulling the other woman into her arms, where she belonged. As she kept her arms wrapped protectively around Lena, she made up her mind. If anyone tried to take Lena from her again, she would do whatever it took to protect her, no matter the cost.

    For the previous three days, Alex had barely left the DEO. Ever since the abrupt end to her birthday celebrations, when she had been unceremoniously forced to go home to sleep the alcohol off, Alex had been unable to focus on anything but recapturing Lillian. Any threat to her sister needed to be quickly and completely silenced; locked in a DEO black site with the keys forgotten.

    Number one on that list currently was none other than Lillian Luthor, and Alex would not rest until she was back in custody. Nothing could sway her from the course, not J'onn, not Maggie, not even Kara, who had been conspicuously absent from the DEO since the news dropped, claiming an uptick of responsibilities at work.

    At the moment, Alex was watching the footage of Metallo and Lillian's escape that had just been recovered. Kara's initial assessment of the scene had been correct. Someone had managed to sneak a kryptonite heart into the facility, and Metallo had used it to escape. The question, then, was how someone had managed to do that in a maximum-security prison.

    Alex was watching the footage for what must have been the hundredth time, trying to suss out any detail as to how the heart had gotten in or where the escapees had headed. She was so absorbed in it that she almost didn't hear the familiar footsteps behind her until they were nearly on top of her.

    “Maggie,” she said, a touch exasperatedly, “I've told you that I'll rest when we've found them. I'm fine.”

    “First of all,” Maggie replied, “You are not fine, you've been awake for nearly three days straight. Secondly, that's not why I'm here. There is something you need to see.”

    Turning fully to face her girlfriend, Alex accepted the tablet she offered and pressed play. As she watched, Lena Luthor opened a safe in her office, pulling a Kryptonite heart out and setting it into a container, the very same container she had seen in the footage from the prison but had been unable to find at the scene.

    All at once, her every suspicion was confirmed. She had known that Lena couldn't be trusted, despite all of Kara's insistence that she wasn't like her family. She had been right, and Kara's blind stubbornness had almost caused her to drop it, invariably leaving Kara to Lena's deadly clutches.

    “Kara isn't going to like this,” Alex muttered to herself, already trying to make sure her sister stayed out of it until she could handle the situation.

    “I knew it!” Maggie exclaimed, her tone at odds with the harsh emotions swirling in Alex's head.

    “What?” She asked, looking up at the excited expression on her girlfriend's face.

    “Sorry," Maggie said, seeming to remember herself, “You said, 'Kara isn't going to like this,' and I think I know why. It makes sense, really. Every time there is some drama with Supergirl, you get all quiet and broody. It makes sense if she's your sister.”

    Alex looked at Maggie, completely at a loss for what to say. Her initial reaction was to deny it, but things between her and Maggie were getting more and more serious, and she didn't want to lie to her.

    “It's okay,” Maggie said, interrupting her spiral before it could get out of hand, “I know it wasn't your secret to tell. I've suspected for a while, but I didn't want to push you on it if it might have caused some friction between you and your sister.”

    “I love you,” Alex said in response.

    “And I love you,” Maggie replied.

    Smiling at her girlfriend, Alex reached out a hand for hers, “Let's go. We've got work to do.”

    Lena sat working at her desk, having only just managed to convince a reluctant-to-part Kara that she couldn't keep showing up late for work less than an hour previously. She heard Jess's intercom crackle and looked up, waiting to hear what her assistant needed to tell her, but nothing came through.

    Lena reached out for her own intercom button to check in, but before she could press it the doors to her office swung open aggressively.

    “Alex, Maggie,” Lena greeted the newcomers, trying her best to sound friendly, but unable to help a spike of worry at the visage of her wife's sister in her full DEO tactical gear, “What can I do for you?”

    At that very moment, Lena's phone beeped, and she glanced at it briefly. Evidently, her worry had been audible to Kara, causing her to text to check-in. Lena quickly replied that she was fine, unwilling to have Kara fly in angry and protective and risk her endangering Alex or Maggie's health or her relationship with her sister. Doing her best to keep her heart rate under control, she put her phone back down and refocused on her uninvited guests.

    “Sorry,” she said, trying to keep the snark out of her voice, “I've got a busy schedule this morning. What was it you needed?”

    "Care to explain this, Luthor?” Alex asked, not masking the accusation in her voice at all as she passed Lena a tablet.

    Watching the presented video, Lena couldn't help but feel shocked and outplayed. Undoubtedly, this was her mother's doing. She hadn't struck back at her physically, as Kara had been afraid, but she had done her very best to drive a wedge between her and Kara. The blow hadn't fallen where she intended, but it would doubtlessly be just as effective in disrupting her life.
