    “I understand your concern,” Lena said, trying her best to be diplomatic, “But I can assure you this video is fake. You can look in the safe, or anywhere else in any of my facilities. I do not have any Kryptonite, nor do I possess the capabilities to make any.”

    It wasn't technically true, she did have a few small samples for testing that Kara had provided her, but her word washed ineffectively across Alex anyway. Looking at Maggie, Lena saw that she'd get no support there either.

    “Nice try,” Alex said, an edge of venom to her voice that was all too familiar to Lena, “But do you really think I'd trust anything a Luthor has to say? For all I know, you could have a dozen secret facilities, just like Lex did.”

    A slim part of her wanted to just tell Alex the truth, lay it all to bear and see what came of it, but she wouldn't do that, not even for herself. It was, and always would be, Kara's call when it came to her family. Plus, she had no doubt, especially given what Alex had just said, that the other woman wouldn't believe her anyway.

    When Lena said nothing in her defense, Alex pushed on, her voice taking a taunting, haughty air, “And to think I almost believed you last we talked. You almost had me convinced that all you wanted was to be an altruistic member of society. I should've seen through it then, but I see you for the Luthor you truly are now.”

    “Call Kara,” Lena said desperately, “She can tell you the truth.”

    At the mention of Kara's name, Alex's entire demeanor changed, going from smug condemnation to hard, lethal rage, and in that moment, Lena realized her mistake. In her haste and desperation, Lena had asked for her wife, who would have no idea about anything to do with kryptonite, or its uses in breaking Metallo and Lillian out of prison, at least, not unless you knew her secret identity.

    “I am not bringing her into this,” Alex said, her voice so cold that even Lillian would've been proud of it, ”You have no idea how adamantly she defended you. This is going to break her heart, and it's your fault. So no, I won't call her.”

    Lena would never beg for herself, but for Kara, she would do anything, “Please, don't do this to her.”

    “I'm doing this for her,” Alex replied, her resolve not wavering in the slightest, “I won't let you drag her into this. Whatever you have planned, whatever you've done to her to convince her of your innocence, it ends now. You will never see her again.”

    Lena knew that, unless Alex killed Kara, there was no way her statement was true, but even the very thought sent a spike of pain so intense through her that she nearly collapsed.

    “Please, Alex,” Lena said desperately, thinking only of how this would affect Kara, “You don't know what you are doing. You don't know what this will bring.”

    At her words, Alex bristled, clearly taking Lena's statement as a threat. Lena could see there was no hope of convincing Alex, and still unwilling to drag Kara into this, there was nothing else to do but allow it to happen.

    She mutely held out both her wrists, shocking both women. Though Maggie, seemingly, had mostly came as support for Alex, not so much as muttering a word the entire exchange, it was she who stepped forward and slipped the cuffs on Lena's outstretched arms, clicking them in place just below the twin bracelets adorning Lena's wrists before leading her back through the doors to her office.

    “Jess, please clear the lobby and make sure the path out of the building is empty,” Lena said to her assistant as she walked past the stunned woman.

    Watching Jess jump to action as they entered the elevator, Lena heard Alex let out an amused huff behind her, no doubt assuming Lena had done it in a vain attempt to maintain her image. Lena knew that the second Kara got word of the arrest, she would put a stop to it, violently if need be, and Lena was unwilling to be the one to pit Kara against her family. It was ironic, she supposed, that even the actions she took to protect the women arresting her would be turned against her.

    Alex escorted her through the empty lobby out onto the street, where an unmarked black SUV so plain it could only be from a covert government agency waited. Roughly seating Lena into the back, Alex made her way around to the driver's seat while Maggie took position on the passenger's seat.

    They rode in silence, but as they drove past the police station Lena felt her trepidations start to mount. She had hoped, however unlikely she knew it would be, that she wouldn't end up locked in the DEO. But, as they pulled up to a bland-looking skyscraper on the other side of the city, she knew that her luck had run out.

    They made their way into the building, taking an elevator up to what Lena could only assume was the command center of the DEO. As the doors swung open, she was greeted with a familiar sight of red and blue, and for a moment she thought that Kara had already come for her, but her hopes were quickly dashed.

    The figure was much too tall and bulky to be Kara. Instead, Superman looked back at her, a near-manic energy to his gaze as he stared her down. Though it likely wouldn't do her any favors, she met his gaze, refusing to back down from it.

    "Supergirl should have known better,” he said after a few moments, “Trusting a Luthor.”

    The words hurt no less as they twisted into her heart than they did when Alex had expressed her own similar sentiment earlier, but now with the inevitability of her predicament settling in on her, she had to fight harder than ever to keep her heart under control.

    It was, however, fated to be for naught. Alex pulled her through the room, drawing looks from the many agents scattered throughout as she headed for the opposite side. Leading Lena deeper and deeper, past rooms containing cells of various shapes and sizes, Alex opened a hidden panel, causing a section of wall to slide away.

    Alex turned to her before entering the room, her face somehow harder than it had been before as she unclipped the cuffs from Lena's wrists. Her attention caught briefly as she did so, but she looked up without comment.

    “Despite her insistence that she hasn't told you,” Alex said as she guided Lena less than gently into the room, “I know you know. Kara won't be able to find you here. I'll be back to question you once I deal with her.”

    As she saw the room she was being pushed into, Lena finally lost the battle as her heart started racing just as the door slammed in her face.

    Chapter End Notes

    Whoops... Did I tag protective Alex? I mean Overprotective Alex

    Just a heads up. I will be out of town next week for my mother's funeral, so the next update will be in two weeks

    Chapter 9

    Chapter Notes

    Alright, I am back. I'd like to thank everyone for their patients for my longer-than-normal break and for all the kind comments. There are a few things I'd like to mention before I get into the new chapter however.

    I know I've mentioned this a few times before (I think just in the comments though) but I am going to throw this out there for posterities sake. In all the secret relationship fics I've read, it always seems like the secret causes a bunch of internal angst between Kara and Lena and that was the main focal point of the story. I really don't care much for that concept, and I like it even less for this fic. It's pretty much the antithesis of what I want when I read a secret relationship fic. However, as a result, I still needed some angst (because I like angst) so Alex and Kal had to pick up that burden, because I still wanted the relationship to be the crux of the fic, and I do love me some familial drama between Alex and Kara (plus this gives me a perfect excuse to have Kara stand up to Alex, another thing I love but rarely see). So, just keep in mind that was what I was thinking when writing this fic so that is why some things are playing out the way they are (and never forget that I just wrote what I wanted to read, so there may be a bit of strangeness because I had to do my best to justify where I wanted the fic to go, and some of it may not be perfect).

    Also, minor spoilers for what you are about to read (so if you want go read it then come back here). I threw Barry in this fic (similar to how I replaced Winn with Brainy) for no other reason than because I wanted to. Barry and Kara are my brOTP, and I love their friendship and interactions dearly. Also, I decided to bump Barry a little bit further down his timeline so he was faster. This is just a thing of mine, but I think powerful speedsters, when they get closer to their potential are just stupidly faster than even a powerful Kryptonian like Kara. I think young Kara could probably keep pace with young Barry, and a more experienced Kara could probably keep up with intermediate Barry, but pretty much no one can compete with a very experienced very powerful speedster except for another very experienced very powerful speedster. Also, don't mind me giving Barry the same competence boost that I gave Kara so he doesn't get surprised by people traveling at the speed of a light breeze anymore

    Kara, after regretfully parting from Lena's side at her wife's insistence, was doing her best to plod through her day at work. She had been assigned some puff piece to write for the next edition, but she had sped through it, far too impatient at the moment to write it at her normal pace. This had just made things worse, however, as now she had nothing to occupy her time and she had to wait a suitably long amount of time until she could submit her article, lest it appear suspicious.

    She had decided, after a few long, excruciating minutes of boredom to plan something for her and Lena's upcoming Valentine's date. Though this was their third Valentine's Day as a married couple, and the fifth since they had gotten together, they had never managed to spend the day together, partly because Alex frequently wanted to spend the day with her since she thought they both had no one to celebrate it with, and partly because Lena was usually very busy.

    This time, however, Kara was determined that they would spend the day together, and she wanted to make it special, even in light of everything that was happening. She hadn't gotten very far into her plans before Lena's heart rate suddenly spiked across town. It didn't sound like she was in danger, more likely stressed or anxious, which was the only reason Kara wasn't already landing on her balcony, but regardless she wasn't taking any chances.

    Taking out her phone, she quickly sent Lena a text to check in, getting ready to depart quickly if she didn't receive an answer. When her phone pinged with Lena's confirmation that she was okay, Kara got back to her task, but she would be lying if she said that part of her wasn't still acutely tuned into Lena's heartbeat.

    Though she was extraordinarily tempted, she didn't listen to what Lena was saying. While Lena hadn't objected to Kara listening to her heartbeat, listening to her conversations without consent felt a step too far.

    After the initial spike, Lena's heart had mostly returned to normal, so Kara did her best to return to her task, but she couldn't fully engage in it. Every once and a while, Lena's heart would speed up for just a moment before returning to the just-above-normal rate it had been hovering at, and every time the urge to go to her grew greater. The only thing keeping her rooted in her seat was Lena's reassurance via text that she was okay.

    Over the course of the next twenty minutes, Lena's heart kept up its unusual rhythm, and Kara tracked it as it moved away from L-Corp. She wasn't sure exactly where Lena had come to rest, somewhere in the general direction of the DEO, but after a few minutes in the new location, Lena's heart rate had spiked even higher than the first time then cut out completely. The sudden silence overwhelmed Kara. This could not be happening.

    Not again.

    It took her a moment to gain her bearings again, but when she did, she decided she had had enough. She was done pretending she couldn't hear when Lena was in danger, she was done pretending Lena wasn't the most important thing in her world, and she was done playing nice.

    Without a second thought as to where she was or who might see, Kara vanished in a blur, going from CatCo to the DEO balcony before her brain had even fully processed her intention to do so.

    She stormed into the building towards where she could sense Alex, not noticing how the DEO fell still in her wake. Her arrival in the command center caused a hush to fall over the usually bustling room. Kara's eyes swept the room, taking in the faces with looks ranging from shock to fear looking back at her without much thought as she looked for her sister.

    Alex wasn't present, and the DEO was such a quagmire of soundproofing and lead-lined rooms that locating her precisely would likely be impossible, but she was definitely here, and if she wasn't already on her way back, Kara had no doubt that her unsubtle arrival would cause her sister to be summoned, all she had to do was wait, ticking each eternity by one by one.

    In the Depths of the DEO, Alex exited the corridor leading to the holding cell she had just locked Lena Luthor in to find J'onn and Kal waiting for her in a tense silence.

    “Alex-” J'onn started, but Alex already knew what he was going to say.

    “I did what I had to do to protect Kara, and I'd do it again,” She said, cutting off his reprimand.

    J'onn leveled her with a hard look, but Alex didn't back down, and after a few moments he spoke, “Very well. What's done is done, but next time I expect to be informed beforehand, understood?”

    J'onn turned and walked back towards the command center without waiting for her reply, leaving her and Clark standing together looking at his retreating form.

    Making their way after him, Alex paused a few levels below the main room, causing Clark to look at her questioningly.

    “Back in the cell,” She said in explanation, “I saw Lena had two plain silver bracelets on either wrist, you don't think...”

    “Absolutely not,” Clark said definitively, looking somewhere between relieved and angry at the notion, “Kryptonian wedding bands would be golden, not silver, and they would have the House of El crest on them, at the very least. Plus, tradition dictates that there would be one per person, not two.”

    As Clark spoke, he rolled up one of the sleeves of his suit, revealing a bracelet that looked similar to the ones she had seen only in size. Alex nodded thankfully, she had never really considered it a possibility, but she appreciate the confirmation nonetheless. The thought slipped from Alex’s mind as she approached the door to the command center, replaced rapidly by a mounting sense of trepidation, accompanied by a cold drip of fear down her spine. She could feel Kara, even through the reinforced walls, and her sister was not happy.

    Alex tried to wrap her head around it. She knew that Kara wouldn't be pleased, but there was no way that she should've noticed this soon. She had closed Lena in the cell less than ten minutes ago.

    Resigning herself to dealing with this now, rather than on her own terms, Alex steeled herself before pushing the door open. Kara looked over from where she was standing by J'onn, rubbing incessantly at her wrist, her eyes momentarily snagging on Clark before locking onto hers.

    “Lena's in danger, we need to find her,” her sister said without preamble, her worry evident in every word.

    Alex looked at J'onn, who had definitely had the opportunity to tell Kara, but got nothing in return. His meaning was clear. This was her mess, that she had started for her family. It was her responsibility to deal with the fallout.

    Returning to her sister's insistent gaze, Alex decided to rip off the Band-Aid, “Lena is not in danger. She's here, at the DEO.”

    “What?! Why?!” Kara said, more a demand than a question.

    Looking around at the various agents and other personnel that were watching on with a mix of fear and curiosity, Alex decided that some privacy was needed, since this was definitely going to escalate from a Supergirl-related issue to a Kara-related issue relatively quickly.

    “Give us the room,” She ordered, waiting for the various personnel to filter out, leaving only J'onn, herself, Clark, Brainy, Maggie, Mon-El, who must've shown up when she was downstairs, and Kara, looking at her with a dangerous expression.

    Once everyone had filtered out of the room, Alex turned back to her sister, “Kara, there is something you need to see.”

    Reaching for a nearby tablet, Alex pulled up the video of Lena handling Metallo's heart from the DEO database and handed it to her sister. Deep down, she hoped that Kara would finally see reason when it came to Lena, but that hope shattered at the same time as the tablet in Kara's hands.

    “This is obviously a fake,” Kara said, her anger pushing more viciously against Alex's skin, ”Lillian is framing her.”

    Alex watched her sister look around at the others desperately, looking for any sort of support, only to find nothing.

    “Brainy,” Kara pleaded, turning back to the Coluan, “Watch the video again, please. If anyone can find something, it's you.”

    “I have analyzed it several times. I have been unable to find any signs of tampering,” Brainy replied, his smooth, neutral tone completely at odds with the situation.

    “Then keep looking,” Kara said, desperation tinging her voice.

    “When are you going to accept that Lena isn't who you thought she was,” Alex demanded, drawing Kara's attention back to her.

    “I thought you could trust me,” Kara said after a moment, a look of such pain crossing her face that in a different circumstance Alex might've thought there was Kryptonite present, “I've told you time and time again that Lena isn't a villain. Since when is my word not good enough for you?”

    “About anything else, it would be. But not this,” Alex met her sister's eyes for a long, hard moment, watching as the pain in her eyes drained away, replaced only with rage, “You aren't thinking clearly about this, Kara. No matter what she does, you refuse to see it.”

    “You are the one who isn't thinking clearly,” Kara spat back at her, “You never needed proof to condemn her.”

    “That may be so, but now I do have proof. Regardless of how you or I feel about it, that video condemns her by itself. Even if you weren't my sister, I'd still have no choice but to treat her as a hostile agent.”

    Kara stared blankly at her sister as she felt her anger fester and grow within her. Clearly, this conversation was not going how Alex had envisioned it, and her sister was taken aback by how angry she had become, but Kara was tired. She didn't care to hide it anymore.

    “You are wrong, Alex. I-”

    “No, Kara. You are wrong. Lena isn't who you think she is. She has Kryptonite, and she would gladly use it on you given the chance. This time, I need you to trust me.”

    “She wouldn't do that to me, she...”

    Kara had been about to say loves me, but at this point, she didn't know what good it would do. She had no doubt at this point that Alex wouldn't believe her, or even worse, would assume that Lena had only been using her to accomplish her goals.

    It occurred to her, at that moment, that she had waited too long. If she had told her family earlier, it might've been very unpleasant, but all this might've been avoided. Now, she was stuck, because she could not let this stand, but she knew that Alex would never back down.

    “She wouldn't what,” Alex said angrily, breaking Kara out of her thoughts, “She wouldn't use you the way Lex used Kal. She wouldn't lie to you to get what she wanted? She wouldn't manipulate you for years? Use your friendship against you? Just. Like. LEX!”

    “She is nothing like Lex,” Kara snapped at her sister, a dull heat beginning to gather in her eyes at Alex’s words and Kal's agreeing nods.

    “I don't know what she has on you,” Alex said, either not sensing the looming danger or otherwise ignoring it, “I don't know if you just can't see you are being manipulated, or if she made some form of Kryptonite that is clouding your judgment, but until I can figure it out, things are staying as they are.”

    There was a dull ringing sound in Kara's head as she processed Alex's words. Without thinking, she took a step forward, a snarl ripping out of her throat. Alex sprang back, a look of terror Kara had never seen before on her face. The sight sobered her a little, and she suddenly felt her eyes were burning.

    Looking at her sister, now stationed protectively behind Kal, J'onn, and Mon-El, even Brainy looking as if he were getting ready for a fight, Kara knew that the moment she had long been dreading was finally upon her, and as much as she wanted, deeply and desperately, to fight for Lena, she couldn't.

    She could take any one, maybe even two of them, but not all four, and she knew if she failed, Alex would lock her away until she was satisfied that whatever nonsensical thing she thought Lena had done to her had been reversed.

    And, just because she chose Lena, didn't mean that she looked forward to or didn't worry about the prospect of potentially hurting her family if there was any other way.

    With great effort, she tamped down the rage burning in her eyes, leveling her sister with one last, desperate look as her arm instinctively clamped over her wrist, right where the phantom of her bracelet still lingered, “Please, Alex, please don't make me do this.”

    Her sister looked back at her, still shocked that Kara nearly fried her, but her face quickly went back to the hard and resolute expression she had been wearing before, “No, Kara. I will protect you from her, even if I have to fight you to do it.”

    Seeing that Alex would not be swayed, Kara turned and left without looking back, desperately racking her brain for a path forward that wouldn't put her at odds with her family. She knew she couldn't leave things as they were, and she knew exactly what she needed to do to get Lena back, but she still feared that she would hurt her family in the process.

    Though leaving Lena locked up for even a moment longer than she needed to be was the hardest thing she had ever had to do, Kara spared the time to get a second opinion. She considered going to Eliza or Lois, but they were both too close in one way or another to the issue to be completely objective.

    She needed someone else, someone that knew enough about her and what her life was like to be able to understand what she was going through, but most importantly, she needed a friend. One that didn't hold the same bias against her wife that her family did.

    Barry stared down the Meta in front of him, watching as the light flickered just a bit too slowly off his metallic skin as he used a touch of his speed to slow things down, preventing the surprisingly agile Meta from getting the drop on him.

    “Tony,” Barry said, “You don't want to do this.”

    The other man looked resentfully back at him. It was odd, Barry supposed, fighting your ex-bully turned meta human that had somehow been resurrected, but he was The Flash, and he was used to it.

    As the hulking meta opened his mouth, Barry settled deeper into his speed, letting everything slow down even further as he focused on his foe, ready to fight if worst came to worst.

    He was so focused on the meta in front of him, however, that he didn't notice the approaching presence from above until it was too late. As Tony's first syllable slowly slid into the second, someone appeared in Barry's peripheral vision, and the sight momentarily took him by surprise, preventing him from doing anything before Kara's fist came down quick and hard on the Meta, knocking him out cold.

    Barry blinked as he let go of his hold on his speed, looking back and forth rapidly between the now flesh-colored lump that was Tony Woodward, and Supergirl, standing over the unconscious meta, looked heavily back at him.

    “I had that,” Barry said, unable to keep the slightest bit of petulance from his voice.

    It wasn't that he didn't appreciate seeing Kara, nor having her help, but they normally asked before interceding in the other's villains, and she hadn't even given him a chance to finish his trademark 'you're better than this' speech before laying Girder out flat.

    “I know,” Kara said, gesturing at the unconscious meta in an uncommon display of impatience, “can you just...”

    Still too shocked to argue, and sensing that Kara's arrival signified something a lot more significant than just a friendly team-up, Barry complied, channeling the full might of the speed force to run Tony to the pipeline and return as fast as he was able.

    Upon his arrival, he felt Kara's gaze drop back onto him, heavy and full of something that he couldn't quite decipher.

    “Can you...” Kara started, before gesturing to him, then back at herself.

    Guessing her intention, Barry dropped himself into flashtime, causing everything around him, even Kara, to become completely still. He moved to Kara's side, meaning to bring her into flashtime with him, but he paused. Clearly, there was something that Kara was dealing with that she had come to him for help with.

    Her demeanor was at odds with the friend he was used to, both in and out of the suit. She lacked the complete confidence that she always exuded as Supergirl, and she had completely lost the compassion and empathy that she always shown her foes during their showdowns, but what most worried him was the differences he saw in his friend, Kara.

    Even still as a statue, Barry could still see the emotions raging in Kara's eyes: desperation, agitation, rage, pain, and greatest of all, an all-consuming darkness. He knew that underneath her warm and cheerful interior, the darkness had always been there, similar to the one that lived buried inside him from the death of his mother, but Kara's was orders of magnitude greater, losing not only her mother, but her whole planet and people.

    But Barry, however, had never, not even once seen the darkness weigh on his friend, for as great as it was, it had always been outshone by her light, by her determination to shine, not instead of the darkness, but despite it. Whatever was causing Kara to feel this way, it was clear that Kara had come to him for something, for some reason, and he would do his best to help his friend.

    Reaching his hand up, he gripped Kara's shoulder, watching as the energy of the speed force enveloped her, allowing her to enter flashtime with him. Once Kara had adjusted, regaining her bearings quickly after the sensory shock of losing the constant assault of stimulus, Barry saw some of the agitation and restlessness drop from Kara's shoulders as her issue was put on a temporary pause.

    Barry watched her as she looked around briefly. He knew they shared the same sort of time-warping effect when they moved at superspeed, but flashtime was a whole new level of slow.

    “You've gotten so fast,” Kara said, “I barely even noticed when you left earlier. You're definitely faster than me now.”

    Barry wanted to preen under the praise, but Kara was looking at him again, and he saw it for the deflection that it was. Even now, Barry could see the mania starting to slowly re-enter Kara's every movement, but still she deflected.

    “Thank you, but I have a feeling that isn't what you came here to talk about,” Barry replied.

    "No,” Kara said after a moment, “No it's not.”

    She didn't say anything else for nearly a minute, starting again slowly just as Barry was about to prompt her to continue, “When I was younger, I met someone. We were friends for years, long before I ever became Supergirl. Her family was... problematic when it came to aliens. That threatened to drive a wedge between us, because even before she knew I was an alien, she knew that my family and I supported aliens on Earth, and she figured I would assume the worst of her, like everyone else did, but I knew her better than that.

    “Our families, however, grew too far apart, until all that was left was us. No matter what happened, I refused to let it affect our friendship, even when every time it came up my family warned me that she would go the same way as her family one day, if she hadn't already. Eventually, we grew… closer, and we decided that it was better if our families didn't know that we were together. To protect ourselves from their scrutiny, we decided it was best that they didn't think we were friends anymore.

    “Still, even without any proof, my family always thought the worst of her, so I decided I could wait to tell them. I could wait until they were ready to see the truth, but I waited too long. Her family came back, and now my family is convinced that she is on their side, no matter how much I tell them she isn't, nor how many times I vouch for her, they are convinced she is evil.”

    “Are you telling me that you... and Lena Luthor...”

    He hadn't meant to say it quite like that, but everyone knew that Supers and Luthors didn't mix. Even if Kara hadn't said it outright, there wasn't really anyone else that could fit that criteria.

    “She is nothing like them,” Kara snarled at his tone, her eyes flashing dangerously with heat.

    “Woah, woah,” Barry said, putting both hands up in front of him placatingly, “I'm sorry, I didn't mean it like that, I was just surprised.”

    “Yeah, well. Now you see the problem,” Kara said as her eyes dimmed, “no matter what Lena does, everyone always assumes the worst of her.”

    Barry felt badly now, for he too would likely have assumed the same of Lena, at least to start with, but from the way that Kara said her name, he knew without a doubt that Lena could be trusted. Kara was an excellent judge of character, and she clearly loved Lena with all her being, and that was good enough for him.

    “Okay, so Lena isn't evil, but your family thinks she is, then what happened?” Barry asked, earning him a grateful look from Kara.

    “Her mother, Lillian is the head of CADMUS. I'm sure you heard she just escaped from prison,” Kara asked, waiting for Barry to nod affirmatively before continuing, “One of her goons, Metallo, uses a Kryptonite heart as his power source. After our last encounter with him, his heart exploded. He survived, but was rendered powerless and captured. When Lillian was arrested, she was in the same holding facility while her trial was underway. Someone managed to sneak him a new heart and they broke out three days ago. Today, a video was released showing Lena with the Kryptonite heart, and Alex refuses to believe the video is fake.”

    “Have you considered just telling them the truth about you and Lena?” Barry asked.

    “Of course I have, but I don't think they'd believe me anymore. Alex seems to think that Lena is manipulating me, so she would probably assume I was tricked or coerced into marrying her somehow. It doesn't matter what I say anymore, she is convinced Lena is using me to further her agenda. I waited too long.”

    Barry pondered the situation for a moment. Over the years, he had several encounters with Metas that caused him or his team to turn against each other. It was always a messy affair, but at least for them, it was temporary, this seemed to be a more sustained problem, and it wouldn't have such a quick solution.

    “Maybe,” Barry said hesitantly, still unsure of all the complexities of the situation, “Maybe you and Lena need to get away for a while, let everything calm down. Maybe if you give it some time, your sister might come around.”

    “I've given her time, but it doesn't seem to matter. It's too late for that, anyway. Alex arrested Lena earlier this morning, she won't let me near her.”

    Barry imagined himself if it was Iris locked away and team flash that wouldn't let him see her. The agony he felt, just at the thought, was nothing compared to the pain in Kara's voice.

    “I know she isn't in any danger,” Kara continued, “But I don't even know where she is, and Alex won't let me see her. I can't even hear her. I can't abide it, but there is no way that Alex will let me take her without a fight.”

    Barry could see the desperation that had tinged Kara's voice start to infect her every mannerism. She began pacing back and forth, the concrete cracking under her heeled boots as she lost control of her usually tightly maintained strength. Suddenly, he realized why she was there. She didn't need advice on what to do, because there was only one thing she could do. Kara was here for permission. She needed to know it was okay, and she needed someone who could fathom what an impossible decision this was to absolve her, and most importantly, she needed reassurance that she wouldn't hurt or destroy her family.

    “Do what you must for your wife,” He said firmly, “Your family loves you, they will come around, and you love them, so you won't hurt them. You won't lose control.”

    His words rippled across her features, washing away the indecision and all her other jumbled emotions and leaving behind the Girl of Steel, will hardened and resolute.

    “Thank you, Barry,” Kara said, flying away as the flashtime bubble dissipated.

    “Good luck,” Barry replied, watching her as she shrunk into a speck in the distance.

    Once she cleared Central City, Kara made a beeline straight back to National City, flying much faster than she normally would, even over the mostly empty land between Central City and National City. As she streaked through the air, she felt the last of her trepidation and fear bleed out of her, replaced with a lethal calm.

    She knew what she was up against. She had no doubt that Kal, J'onn, Mon-El, and Brainy would support Alex to keep Lena locked up. She knew that Alex, in her misbegotten attempt to keep her safe would do anything as long as it outweighed the risk she perceived Lena as, but not even the anger she felt at her sister using Kryptonite was more than a blip, quickly washed away in the storm of calm that had overtaken her.

    As it was, she wasn't strong enough to free Lena, but she knew exactly what she needed to do. The only thing stopping her had been the shackles of fear, but now she was freed of them, and nothing could hold her back from Lena.

    Entering the city suburbs, she slowed, but not enough. The buildings shook as she rocketed past them, but the cries of the startled citizens of National City never reached her ears. She didn't slow until she reached the hidden lab she and Lena had been using in their off time.

    She waited patiently for the airlock to open before making her way slowly into the lab, locating what she needed and making her way back toward the surface. Despite her speed in returning to National City, as she made her way into and through the lab, she found the anxiety she had been feeling before she had talked to Barry gone. No longer did every passed second grate against her fraying nerves. The decision had been made. Lena would be free. It was no longer a question, but a matter of time.

    Alex watched the retreating form of her sister, feeling deeply unsettled at what had just occurred. Even with direct proof, Kara had still refused to see the truth, had in fact insisted the proof was fake, and gone so far as to nearly attack her when she pointed out the obvious. Clearly, whatever Lena had done to her had been even more effective than she thought, and Alex was more determined than ever to keep Kara away from her until she could figure out whatever it was.

    “I need you to get me something,” Alex said, turning to Clark and pulling him from his own shocked thoughts.

    “Absolutely not,” Clark said firmly.

    “I don't like it either, and it's a last resort, but she isn't thinking clearly. If- when, She comes back, there is going to be a fight. I don't want to hurt her, but we need to make sure she stays away from Lena, no matter the cost.”

    “Fine,” He said, “I'll go get it, but only as a last resort.”

    As Clark turned to leave, Alex summoned the rest of the DEO agents back into the room, tasking them to track Supergirl's location.

    Heading towards where Maggie and Brainy were clustered around Brainy's station, she caught the tail end of what he was saying, “... It doesn't fit any of the statistical models. None of the scenarios I have run account for her behavior. We must have made a false assumption.”

    “If I didn't know any better, I'd think Little Danvers-”

    At that moment, one of the DEO agents called over to Alex, cutting Maggie off before she could finish, “Ma'am, it looks like Supergirl is headed for Central City.”

    Nodding to the agent, she looked back to Maggie, who had broken off from talking to Brainy and was waiting for her with an understanding look on her face. Alex didn't say anything, and the two women settled into a rhythm, working through the preparations for Kara's return as quickly as they could.

    “She went to talk to Barry,” Alex said bitterly after a while, causing Maggie to stop her work and look up at her, “I don't know why she won't talk to me.”

    “I don't have any sisters,” Maggie replied soothingly, “But I think typically after getting into a huge argument with your sister, one would probably prefer venting to a friend over trying to have a rational conversation with the object of your ire.”

    “I know, but this isn't a one-time thing. She has been keeping something from me, something to do with Lena, all year. I don't know what it is, but I don't trust her.”

    “Have you considered-”

    “Ma'am, Supergirl is leaving Central City,” The same agent from earlier called out.

    “Where is she headed?”

    “Trajectory puts her on a return course to National City.”

    “ETA?”

    “Her speed is high-hypersonic. We have less than ten minutes.”

    “Keep me updated.”

    With a nod, the agent returned to her monitor, and Alex turned back to Maggie. Kara had been there for less than a minute before leaving. Now she was flying at unsafe speeds over the Continental United States. Any small hope she had harbored that Barry might talk her down had been dashed. Kara was coming back, and she was coming with a vengeance.

    “What's going on?” Clark asked as he walked back into the command center.

    "Kara is on her way,” Alex replied grimly.

    With a look of understanding, Clark handed Alex the package he had retrieved from wherever it had been hidden. Opening the heavy-lidded box, she removed the black sheathe and strapped it over her back, its weight acting as a sobering reminder of what was coming.

    Nodding at Clark, she turned to watch the display tracking Kara's return to National City, leaving the agents to finish up preparations. As Kara approached National City, she slowed, but she still hurtled through the city at supersonic speeds, a loud boom heralding her return as the building shook lightly.

    Sharing a look with Clark, she turned her attention back to the display, but Kara had disappeared. It wasn't exactly a shock. They tracked her, and any other Kryptonians, by the power they exuded naturally under a yellow sun, a minute burning off of energy that their cells constantly underwent. Kara knew this, and was capable at any time of suppressing it, either to hide the feeling of power it gave off to those around her in her human persona, or in cases like this, if she didn't want the DEO to track her.

    Before long, however, she reappeared, soaring up and out of Earth's atmosphere into low earth orbit. She hung there for a few moments, far, far above the city, suspended in a moment of peace while the DEO scrambled beneath her getting ready before she began to descend. She went slowly at first, almost floating downwards as if a leaf on the wind, but then she began to plummet, falling faster and faster.

    Somehow, Alex knew her sister wasn't flying. She was freefalling down to earth. But it didn't matter, her destination was the same. She was coming for the DEO.

    “Brainy, what's her status?” Alex asked after a while, causing the Coluan to look up from his station.

    “She is accelerating past terminal velocity,” He said after a moment, “We have 30 minutes, most likely less. But...”

    "But what?” Alex asked, deeply concerned by Brainy's hesitance.

    “Her power signature is higher than it has ever registered.”

    “Exactly how high is it?”

    Brainy looked back at his display, but before he could check the current level, the computer erupted in a burst of sparks. Alex shielded her eyes from the sudden brightness, and when she looked back, brainy had turned to her, looking slightly singed but somehow more serious than she had ever seen him.

    “Her power level appears to be off the scale.”

    From anyone else, it might have been funny, but Brainy didn't do funny.

    Shortly after Brainy's computer, the rest of the technology in the DEO began to fritz out, some erupting in showers of sparks, some just going dead completely without warning.

    Alex was about to ask what was happening, but suddenly she didn't need to. She could feel it. Everyone stopped dead in their tracks. They could all feel it. Even dozens of miles above the DEO, they could already feel her coming. And it felt different. Not like the normal warm, reassuring presence that she and Clark exuded, this was something else, something more potent.

    She looked at Clark, but he shook his head. This was not normal, not even for a Kryptonian. Whatever Kara had done, this was going to be a lot harder than she had hoped. The weight on her back grew heavier, seeming less and less like a last resort and more and more like a necessity.

    Meeting Clark's eyes, she nodded resolutely, the Kryptonian mirroring the movement. Whatever Kara had done, it didn't matter. They would protect her, even from herself.

    However, as three dozen miles shrank to two, then one, Alex felt her surety begin to fade. She knew what needed to be done, but she found herself less and less sure that it could be done. When the wave of Kara's power had hit them, even from so far away, it had battered against Clark's, the two competing against each other to establish dominance over the room, but soon Kara's had grown, far beyond what Clark could keep up with. And it didn't stop, not when it reached the point that Clark's own ramping presence began to falter, not when Kara started to drown him out, not until Kara's power was like a raging storm on the ocean, completely masking the single drop that was Clark.

    She turned to the Balcony, waiting for Kara to land. At her sides, all their friends joined her. Maggie, J'onn, and Brainy off to one side, Kal, Mon-El, and James, suited up in his full Guardian attire who had arrived to help with the preparations as soon as he had heard, on the other. They stood together, facing the wave of Kara's power, the herald of what was to come.

    All too soon, however, the wait was over. Kara had arrived.

    Chapter End Notes

    Whoops, another cliff hanger, how'd that get in there? Just kidding, it's there because I'm mean and I love leaving people on cliff hangers (I think most authors do too, and if you'd believe it I did want to make split this chapter into two to make another cliff hanger, but I couldn't make it work), it makes me cackle with glee. Anyway, I do have good news, you only have to wait a week for the next chapter, not two like you did for this chapter.

    So, let me know what you think, guess what you think it is that Kara's done to herself (there are clues in previous chapters, though I claim no credit for then, I was just writing something and the solution just presented itself to me on a silver platter, I only delivered it), or hurl expletives at me in the comments for leaving you on yet another cliff hanger, your choice

    Chapter 10

    Chapter Notes

    I think you all have been seeping on the cliffhangers for long enough. If it makes any of you feel any better, I have been waiting to share this chapter for three years. So, without further ado, here is the chapter it's all been building towards.

    Alex groaned as she sat up. Wiping the dust off her face, she looked around. The building was still rumbling around her as more concrete powder and shards rained down all around her. Last thing she remembered, she had been looking at Clark, who for once looked no more confident than she felt when there had been a sudden shift in Kara's power and she had been blasted off her feet.

    She shook her head to clear her thoughts. There were warning alerts going off all throughout the DEO command center. She looked from side to side, seeing everyone else slowly getting their bearings and making their way back to their feet.

    In her dazed state, she momentarily wondered what had happened. Had it been an earthquake, some other natural disaster? But then it clicked. Raising her eyes to the DEO landing pad, there was Kara. She had arrived, much in the same way as any natural disaster; with a violent amount of destruction. If the DEO hadn't specifically been designed to contain the might of a raging Kryptonian, the entire building would've been reduced to rubble.

    But, the woman on the landing pad wasn't Kara, not anymore. This was Supergirl, a vengeful Supergirl. She was glowing with an intense, burning corona of light, so bright that it was nearly impossible to look at her, as if she had been forged out of sunlight, or as if she was the sun, incarnated into Kryptonian form.

    Her aura of power, somehow, had become even more intense. It dominated the room, heating every corner and pushing oppressively against Alex's every sense, radiating a sense of never-ending rage throughout the whole building. The air around Kara simmered with power, stirring the air in the room into a roil, blowing everyone's hair around their faces.

    After a few long, lingering moments, Kara dimmed, the light around her no longer blinding, but still shimmering from beneath her skin and from her suit. It did nothing to ease her harsh presence, however, which still grated against Alex's every nerve, taking every drop of her will not to listen to her primal instinct to flee.

    What was revealed, however, did nothing to reassure Alex that the sister she knew was anywhere to be found. The normal warm planes of her face had been replaced by cold, harsh angles. Her eyes, usually twinkling with kindness, simmered with wrath, her irises blazing like twin blue stars.

    Worse than the anger she was radiating into every corner of the DEO, worse than even the boundless wrath in her eyes, was the incredible, lethal calm with which Kara waited. Watching them stumble back to their feet and regain their composure with her head cocked ever so slightly to the side, like a predator watching its prey stumble unaware into its trap, like a malevolent goddess, waiting patiently for them to kneel before her, as if she had all the time in the world to subjugate them.

    Some of the others had yet to fully regain their senses, and while Kara was content to wait, Alex took the time to take in her suit. It was not a suit she was familiar with, but it seemed similar enough to the one she knew. The only main differences, beyond a few aesthetic changes, were the pants, and the golden filament that ran throughout most of the suit.

    Just like Kara, it seemed to glow with light, as if someone had taken gold and infused it with pure sunlight. The filaments started from the lining of her crest and her belt, both of which were made of the same material, and ran throughout the entire suit, up around the clasps of her cape, across her chest, following the lines of her arms down to her hands, and down her legs underneath her boots.

She looked up at her sister with a completely shocked and bewildered expression, taken completely by surprise by this turn of events.

As they reached open air, Lena spared one last look back at the destruction that Kara had unleashed for her and the crumpling, devastated look on Alex's face.

    She was glad that Kara had chosen her, yes, but she had never wanted this, never wanted Kara's family and life to be torn apart for her sake. Before she could ruminate any more on it, Kara took off, never once looking back.

    Chapter End Notes

    Well well well. There it is. I have been building towards this chapter for so long, I can only hope the wait was worth if for your guys (I know it was for me).

    As with most chapters, I have some thoughts to share. I think this chapter, while lovely to read and write, would have lent itself better in some ways to a visual format more than a written one. Specific aspects, like Kara's aura and more specifically the way it stirs up the room, and blows everyone's hair about their faces would look so much cooler in animated or live action than it can ever be represented in text (just feels a bit clunky to me) but oh well. There is plenty in this chapter that also wouldn't translate well to screen (the way the aura makes people feel, the bolded text (thank F.C. Lee for that, I got the idea from the Kiyoshi novels)). Suffice it to say, there isn't one perfect media format to tell stories in, and I don't like to stick in things that work best in my format, I take inspiration from all formats.

    There are a few things else to note as inspiration for this chapter in particular (it is also applicable to the rest of the fic and a lot of other things I plan to write). Someone did mention in the comics that Kara's power up made them think of dragon ball, and I will admit that is a big inspiration for the aura (though not the only one). I've also read a lot of romantacy type books, and some of those carried over into my style and trope uses (There is a specific book this is partly inspired by by SJM. If you've read it you will know). Also maybe applicable to those who've seen the whole show, 6x19 was a huge tease for Supergirl Prime making a brief appearance, and I was so disappointed that they didn't do it that I wrote that I wrote it into my fic because I love it. And to those who may thing Kara was too OP in this chapter, that was indeed the point.

    It occurs to me some of you may be glad to see Clark get his ass beat in this chapter, but I like to think I don't do it out of malice. As much as I don't like certain character (Mon-El and James) I try to be somewhat true to their characters and then bash them for that, rather than just bashing the for bashing's sake. So while I know a lot of people want to see Kal get his ass beat (and I certainly don't mind it either) I want to make sure it's at least plausibly deserved first.

    I did break the laws of physics at least once in this chapter (at least on purpose, I may have done it other times on accident, I'm not perfect), let me know if you can spot it. While I generally frown on such things, I did it because it was cool and it was purposeful, not from lack of understanding of physics (unless of course I also made a mistake, that would make me look silly right about now). As much as I love sound in space, it isn't really. It's fine for cool sound effects sake, but it grates my nerves as a plot point because I don't think people realize that it can't happen. Also in terms of physics, I was rather surprised that you can actually solidify air (I googled this like 10+ times to make sure I wasn't going crazy, and yes that was a wall of solid air, not ice. I didn't even want to think about doing the math to see if there was that much water in the air. For those curious, its about -210 Celcius, -362 Fahrenheit, or 54.3 Kelvin, which isn't even that crazy considering some of the stuff in the comics). I don't know what I thought dry ice was, but I guess I know now.

    I am super excited though that all of you finally got to read this chapter. Like I said in the foreword, I wrote this three years ago (the first half of the chapter is the first thing that ever got written out instead of being just an outline). Pretty much every decision I made about the characters, the plot, and everything else was designed to bring us to this point, and I'm glad to finally see it pay off. This chapter is my baby, it's my favorite thing I've written in my entire life, and up until a few months ago not even my fiancée had read it. That being said, I'd love to hear your thoughts on it. What was your reaction? How did it make you feel? Did you see this coming, did you not? Are there any small details you picked up on in this chapter or any before it that you want to share? Have you any questions? I'd be happy to talk about any- and everything to do with this chapter or the fic as a whole to anyone who is interested (no pressure to do so, though. I am just passionate about it! Like seriously, I was just rereading it before I post like I do with all my chapters, and I gave myself goosebumps!)

Additionally, there was the matter of the DEO agents and Maggie trapped behind the wall of solid air that had been deposited into the room in the wake of Kara's freeze breath.

    There were no doors in the area that had been enclosed, and while the wall had started to sublimate immediately upon its formation, it was rather thick, and would take a long time to completely disappear, leaving those on the other side trapped for far too long.

    Luckily, personnel from other parts of the facility, as well as some off-duty agents, started to trickle slowly into the command center to help. Assigning most of the agents to break through the wall, she took the rest to help her get the injured to the medbay.

    To her great relief, her initial assessment seemed to be correct, no one was seriously injured, but had simply been rendered unconscious by blunt force. While this wasn't exactly preferable, it did seem as if precisely the right amount of force had been applied to each to render them unconscious with minimal or no extra damage. Kal was a little worse off, his arm completely broken and a few other fractures from his brief fight with Kara, but nothing a few hours under the sunlamps wouldn't straighten out.

    As soon as she had gotten everyone settled, she had left the medbay under the supervision of one of the other medical personnel and returned to where the rest of the agents had nearly broken through the wall.

    Progress was slow, as the agents had to be careful not to spend too long close to the intensely cold wall and not to inhale the still super chilled vapors sublimating off the exposed surfaces, but before long, there was a path through which the trapped persons could escape. Once again, it seemed they had been lucky. As everyone filtered out, there were no signs of serious injury, and as Maggie filed out last, completely unscathed, if a bit cold, Alex felt the tensions that had begun building the moment she had felt her sister through the doors of the DEO hours earlier slowly start to unravel.

    7 months ago

    In the months since she had moved permanently to National City, she and Kara had spent a significant amount of time, at least whenever they could both get away, sequestered in the secret lab space she had built for them. Kara hadn't seemed to have much of a goal in her time there, at least not one that she directly mentioned, appearing for the most part to be reorienting herself after years spent outside of a serious lab, and with the much more advanced equipment, comparatively to what she used in her college labs, now available to her.

    Lena, on the other hand, had a much more goal-oriented list of tasks. She had begun by analyzing samples of Kara's blood and skin cells. Kara, of course, knew most of what there was to know about her biology under a red sun, and was quick to affirm Lena's theories that the two unique organelles in Kara's cells, one of which generated energy from light, similarly to chloroplasts in plants, and the other which stored said energy, were responsible for her powers.

    It was known as well to Kara, since her people had traveled to planets with yellow suns before Krypton's demise, that these organelles had a unique interaction with yellow sunlight, but many of the specifics of these mechanisms were new to her.

    Lena had started with a baseline to confirm these organelles absorbed the light produced by a yellow sun more efficiently than any other wavelength of light. They had both been surprised to discover, however, that the more yellow sunlight these organelles absorbed, the more efficient they became. Even more startling, however, was that the limiting factor to this interaction, however, seemed not to be the organelle, but a simple issue of surface area.

    She had channeled more and more light into the sample, able to create conditions in the lab that would never natively occur to a Kryptonian unless they dove into the sun, and the cells absorbed every spare photon, until they were glowing themselves, radiating heat and light throughout the cavernous lab space.

    Kara, distracted by the light, had come over to examine the results herself, and they had both come to the same conclusion. However powerful Kara was, it was a far cry from her full potential. While she waited for the sample to return to normal, she moved on to a different experiment, banishing her wife to the far side of the lab as she removed the small shard of Kryptonite that Kara had provided for this experiment. She was fairly certain that if she could determine the mechanism by which Kryptonite did its damage, she could determine a way to create a countermeasure.

    She watched with morbid fascination as the sample in front of her withered and died, cell after cell lysing as the radiation annihilated the cell walls. It wasn't a single-sided battle like she might have assumed, however, as Lena saw the energy storage organelles in the affected cells flaring, the energy seeming to stitch the cells back together, but it was a battle of attrition, one that Kara's cells were not winning, especially as the kryptonite's radiation also destroyed the organelles generating and storing the energy, the only two things that were counteracting the onslaught in any way.

    On a whim, Lena took the kryptonite over to the still hypercharged cells, watching in wonder as the Kryptonite seemed to have no effect whatsoever. All this information was spinning in her head, and Lena had decided to finish up for the day, making sure the equipment recording the experiment was working before heading out for the day.

    The next day, Lena had returned, finding the cells no longer glowing, but still alive, despite seeming to have lost the ability to process sunlight at all. She set them aside for the time being, interested in how they would persist over time, before turning back to other experiments she could run in the meantime.

    Over the next few weeks, there was no noticeable change in the cells, but Lena had made noticeable improvements in other areas. She had managed to create a lead and Nth Metal nanoweave that could be introduced into Kara's suits that would partially negate any Kryptonite weaponry or exposure that Kara may encounter. It wouldn't completely stop the damage, but it would at least greatly diminish the immediate lethality of any such devices.

    Not long after she had managed that, Lena came to the lab one day to find that the cells she had been studying had returned completely to normal, as far as she could tell, with no noticeable changes from before she had supercharged them. Eventually, Lena decided to repeat the experiment, but almost immediately as the cells burnt off the rest of the extra energy, Lena noticed that the majority of the cells were dead, and upon further examination and time, it seemed that the rest would never recover their ability to process solar energy.

    Lena ran the experiment several more times, but it didn't seem like there was anything that she could do to change the outcome. She tried modulating the amount of sunlight the cells received after overloading, and while she managed to speed the recovery after the first overload, she never prevented the catastrophic results of the second.

    For now, this was all theory anyway, she had no realistic way to administer such a level of power into Kara's body, and Kara herself was a much more complicated system than a group of cells on a plate. However, such is the blessing and curse of her mind, part Lena was always working the problem, and one day part way through November, it clicked.

    She had encountered the technology needed already. An alloy that could store and redirect solar energy and the nanotechnology to go with it that could be used to control the direction and intensity of the energy, something that was wrapped around her very wrist at that moment. At the time, there had been more important things on her mind than thinking about all the uses of the technology, but now her mind was spinning with it.

    She, of course, wanted to respect the significance of the alloy to Kara's people, and if Kara didn't like it, she would drop it immediately. Kara, however, had been on board, so Lena had made her a new suit, one that could flood her system with an extremely highly concentrated amount of solar energy, granting her an enormous boost to her abilities for a short time.

    Throughout the whole process, they had discussed at length the severity of using this at all, and the cost it would exact on Kara. They both had concerns, not the least of which was while they knew it would boost Kara's powers significantly, they didn't truly know what that would look like, nor what it would do to her, and there was no feasible way to test it.

    In the end, they had come to the conclusion that it should be used as a last resort only, if there was no other way forward, and Lena had designed a second suit specifically to help aid Kara through the recovery. It was all black, so it could absorb as much light as possible, with silver strands of the solar storage alloy running through it, much like gold ran through the other suit, designed to help modulate the amount of light Kara was getting to speed her healing without overloading her systems again. It wouldn't do much, but at least it might ease her suffering a little.

    Alex watched as Clark stirred slightly under the sunlamps. If he was similar to Kara in terms of healing, he should be regaining consciousness any moment. Surely enough, a few minutes later, his eyes fluttered open, and he looked around briefly before sitting up suddenly, nearly shoving his head through the sunlamps overhead.

    "Whoa, whoa, Clark, you're okay," Alex called out calmingly.

    He blinked a few more times, his eyes focusing more clearly on her as the fog seemed to clear from his mind.

    "What... Where's Kara?” He asked, “What even happened? No one I've ever fought has made me feel that powerless, that weak.”

    "I don't know, but for now it seems she's gone,” Alex informed him, “J'onn's already awake, but I want to wait for everyone else to wake up before we all debrief on what happened.”

    He nodded his agreement, looking around to the rest of the medbay to see everyone else was still unconscious. As his eyes settled back on Alex, he noticed the deeply mottled bruises on Alex's throat, but the look in her eyes convinced him now was not the time to broach that subject.

    “Did she...” He drifted off, not really wanting to ask the question.

    “No, thankfully,” Alex said, sounding just as relieved as he felt, “there were a few minor injuries, but nothing serious. There is a lot of damage, but nothing that can't be repaired.”

    He followed Alex's attention as she gestured through the medbay windows to the DEO surrounding them, where there were personnel repairing the rather extensive damage done to the area. Her statement might’ve been true of the building, for the rest of it, he wasn’t so sure.

    “Well,” He said after a few moments, flexing the arm he distinctly remembered snapping “if I'm not needed here, I'm going to go home and tell Lois, let me know when everyone else is awake and I'll come back for the debrief.”

    Receiving a nod of confirmation, he made his way gingerly past the repairs just getting underway, looking in amazement at the pulverized concrete that revealed the bent Nth metal skeleton underlying the room, careful not to do any more damage to the weakened structure. Seeing the completely decimated landing pad, he decided it was better to just lightly float the rest of the way out of the building, before taking off and flying back to Metropolis.

    His mind relived the whole series of events as he made his way back home, but nothing seemed to explain anything. He didn't know what Kara had done, nor did he understand how or why she had done it. He could only hope that Alex could shed some light on it when everyone had woken up. He got the sense that she definitely knew more about it than he did.

    At last, he landed on his balcony, opening the door to find Lois lounging on the couch, typing away on her laptop.

    “Hey,” she greeted, clearly absorbed in her work.

    “Hey,” he returned, making his way towards the bedroom, hoping to shower before telling Lois what had happened.

    However, something about his tone had made Lois stop and look up at him, the smile fading off her face, replaced by a worried, if a bit critical expression.

    “Clark,” she called, the tone causing him to stop in his tracks and turn back towards her, where she was now appraising him closely, “What happened in National City? Is everything okay?”

    No longer left with any choice but to answer Lois's question, Clark moved to sit next to her on the couch. He recounted the events of the day, starting at the phone call he had received from Alex earlier that day requesting reinforcements for whatever the outcome of arresting the Luthor had been, all the way through Kara's attack on the DEO and then him waking up several hours later.

    When he was done, he looked to where Lois had walked almost as soon as he had begun speaking. He had expected worry, maybe sympathy, confusion, something, but not what he saw; pure disappointment and anger.

    “You did WHAT?!” She asked, her tone carefully controlled, but still with plenty of warning evident.

    Clark opened his mouth to retort, but Lois didn't give him the chance, “Kal-El, it's not my place to spell this out for you. Instead, let me give you some advice. Go fix this.”

    With that, Lois returned to writing her article, and when after a few moments Clark was still there, she looked back up at him, cutting off his questions with a single raised eyebrow, and an expression that let him know that she meant to go fix it, now.

    Lena wasn't much surprised that Kara had fallen unconscious after the strain, but the sooner she could get Kara into the recovery suit, the better. Luckily, it seemed as if Kara had been prepared for this, because after Lena had quickly gotten dressed, prepared to drive to the lab to recover it, she had found Kara had already retrieved it and placed it where Lena could easily see it.

    Returning to Kara's side, Lena reached down to place the suit storage device on Kara's naked chest, but she stopped a few inches short. The heat, even from this distance, was intense, far more so than it had been when Kara's powers were still under control.

    Kara was burning, her systems cannibalizing themselves in an attempt to repair the damage the overload had done. Lena had been afraid something like this might happen, even though there had been no indications from the skin samples she had tested, there were so many more complicating factors when dealing with the whole organism rather than a small sample.

    Quickly, and with great caution, Lena set the device over Kara's chest, activating the room's sun lamps as she did so, watching as the nanotech unspooled from it forming the black suit in its wake, the silver filaments beginning to shimmer immediately as the regulated the flow solar energy into Kara's prone form. Checking the diagnostic systems built into the suit, she saw Kara's temperature was greatly elevated, and while the rest of the readings indicated that Kara's systems were stressed, she didn't seem to be in any imminent danger.

    Now, however, there wasn't much Lena could do. Reluctantly, she made her way out of the room, making herself a quick meal before deciding to turn in for the night, retiring to one of the guest rooms and leaving her technology to do its job as she tried to sleep the day off. It was for naught, however, with Kara in the state she was in, Lena slept fitfully, if at all, frequently checking on her wife and feeling worse and worse as nothing changed.

    Eventually, it was morning, and Lena sat eating a bland meal after checking on Kara when she suddenly heard her wife scream. She didn't even need the suit's readouts to understand what was happening, not as she saw Kara writhing in agony, sobbing as pain racked her body, not as the tears were boiling before they could even fall from her eyes.

    In the past few minutes since Lena had last checked, Kara's temperature had skyrocketed, and despite the exceptional ability of the suit to absorb and redirect thermal energy, it wasn't enough. The heat being generated by Kara's body combined with the energy being channeled into Kara by the sunlamps and suit was too much. Lena couldn't risk turning those down, the amount of sunlight being fed into Kara's system had been carefully calculated, and changing it could further compromise Kara's condition.

    “AC on full, lowest possible temperature,” Lena commanded the penthouse AI, feeling immediate relief from the blazing heat as near-freezing air blew into the room from the vents. Briefly, she left Kara's side, running to the kitchen to make an iced saline solution in a mixing bowl and grab a rag, before returning to Kara's side.

    Soaking the rag in the solution, Lena did her best to dab off Kara's face with the cool cloth as her wife writhed in the bed, hissing in pain and nearly dropping the rag when steam erupted the moment the damp cloth made contact with Kara's forehead. Lena dipped the cloth back into the freezing solution and started dabbing Kara's forehead again, repeating the process for a few minutes before the water started getting too hot and Lena had to return to the kitchen to make a new cold solution.

    Lena kept it up for the next hour, dabbing, dunking, running, over and over again until Kara's temperature finally was down to a more acceptable level. Setting an alert to go off if Kara's temperature got too high again, she stood to leave.

    Taking a break from the sensory assault of being in the room, simultaneously freezing and sweating, Lena barely made it to a couch before she collapsed. There had been a time when Kara had explained to her the sensation of helplessness that she had felt when Lena had been in danger and there was nothing she could do about it. At the time, Lena hadn't really been able to understand, now, however, she understood all too well.

    Clearly, though not as much as previously, Kara was still suffering, and there was not a single thing that Lena could do about it. No drug Lena could give her would have any effect, and even if it did, Kara's metabolism would burn through before it gave her any true relief.

    Eventually, she had to accept that there was only so much she could do. Using special ports built into the suit, Lena set up a cold saline IV to keep Kara hydrated and hopefully help keep her temperature down, and, though Kara's robust biology probably didn't need it, a G-tube to provide any nutrients she might need, just in case.

    Throughout the rest of the day, Lena flittered back and forth between Kara's side to the living room, doing her best to take care of Kara, checking all the equipment to make sure it was still functioning, or dabbing her forehead if her temperature started rising again; otherwise trying to soothe Kara, cooing softly to her, or doing her best to kiss the woe from her overheated skin; or sitting around, feeling helpless and doing her best, and failing, to pass the time when she wasn't at Kara's side.

    Finally, somehow, it was the end of the day, and Lena was by Kara's side one last time before she tried valiantly to get some sleep. Looking down at the troubled visage of her wife, she leaned down to smooth the crinkle from Kara's forehead before tucking a lock of hair behind her ear. This was definitely not how she had anticipated spending her first Valentine's Day with Kara.

    Yesterday

    Kara waited patiently as the door to the airlock to the outside opened. There was still no sign of the frenetic energy that had inhabited her before her talk with Barry, and Kara felt the decision cement even more firmly in her head. Masking her presence, which she knew Alex would be tracking, she moved outside, dropping the recovery suit off at home, before rocketing up into low earth orbit, releasing the dampener on her power as she did so.

    Closing her eyes for a moment, she let the pure, unadulterated sunlight wash over her, warmth perfusing her entire body, but despite the calm she felt, it wasn't as reassuring as it normally would have been. Opening her eyes, she placed the disk she had retrieved on her chest, marveling for a moment as the suit unfurled, replacing and supplanting the old suit bit by bit.

    The moment the suit completed forming, Kara felt the power surge into her, energy and heat flooding through her veins, drowning out the feeling of the sun, until the suit itself was glowing, until she was glowing with power. At first, the sensation was overwhelming, far more so than anything she had ever experienced, and through it, Kara could feel a scream building in her throat, but it never came.

    All at once, as her body accepted the power, everything stopped. She looked down at the earth, frozen in place, and it occurred to her, that everything hadn't stopped, she'd sped up. And it wasn't just her body, she could feel the electrical impulses in her brain speeding up, faster than the thoughts racing through her mind, faster than light.

    The feelings, the sensations that had been bombarding her were no longer overwhelming. They were minuscule, finite, nothing compared to the infinite reach of her mind.

    Whatever lingering fear she'd had about injuring her family fell away. While previously she might have worried about accidentally hurting her family, she no longer was. Never in the entire time she had been alive had she felt such a sense of power, but with it came an unmistakable, irrefutable sense of control.

    Slowly, she came back to normal time, until the earth under her was once again spinning. In a blink, she circled the earth, coming to rest in the exact spot from which she had left. She looked down at the earth, picking out an ant crawling through its nest, delivering its morsel of food into the hive. Extending her hands out in front of her, she flexed one hand, knowing like the rest of her powers, her strength had increased to an inconceivable degree.

    Satisfied that it had worked, Kara let herself fall. She could have been on the ground in an instant and searched the entire earth for Lena in the next, but that wasn't the point. Simply recovering Lena, even inexplicably and without notice, wouldn't convey to her family that Lena was off limits. As much as she still didn't like it, her head was clearer than ever. She knew that Alex would never accept it without a dramatic display, and if that was what was needed, so be it.

    She fell until the atmosphere was dense enough to carry sound waves, all at once hearing billions of conversations in thousands of languages, but was unsurprised not to be able to hear Lena. She knew, wherever Alex had put her, would be completely inaccessible to her senses, even magnified to this degree. It didn't really matter, however. She knew Alex would keep her close.

    Focusing on the DEO, she heard her sister asking Brainy after her, surrounded by the hustle and bustle of the DEO as they prepared for her arrival. Finally, she accelerated, faster and faster as she watched the DEO agents scurry about the building getting ready, useless as their preparations would be.

    When she had been retrieving the suit she had now donned, she had known it was only a matter of time before Lena had been freed, and that time was now. Despite her surety that Alex wouldn't tell her, she would give her sister, and all those below one last chance, then she would tear the DEO down brick by brick until she found Lena.

    Alex sighed heavily as she closed the door to her apartment behind her. She was long, long overdue for some sleep, but somehow she knew tonight would be no more restful than any of the previous four. Telling everyone else the news about Kara's marriage had been an ordeal, not helped by the way everyone kept sneaking glances at the dark, mottled skin of her throat, the bruise shaped unsubtly like Kara's hand.

    Surprisingly, Mon-El had taken it the best of them. He, more than any of them, truly knew what a Kryptonian marriage meant to those who upheld Kryptonian tradition as Kara did, and so he had the easiest time coming to terms with Kara's behavior, both in freeing Lena and in the way she had never given a concrete reason for dismissing his advances.

    Clark, in contrast, had been livid to learn that Kara had not only married the sister of one of his greatest enemies, but had hidden it from them for years, seemingly with his own wife's help. Despite Mon-El's explanation that Kara held more to the old ways of Krypton, just like his parents and their aberrant marriage had, he could not fathom that Kara had chosen a Luthor of all things, after everything they had done.

    Maggie hadn't responded too strongly one way or another. The previous day's events had terrified her, her usually unwavering confidence shaken to its roots. But, despite her rattled state, she had put it aside to support her girlfriend, and Alex was ever grateful for it.

    Next to Mon-El, Brainy had taken it the best. He seemed at once both perturbed and comforted, as if he didn't understand how he had missed it, but was pleased to know the solution to his earlier quandary nonetheless.

    James, if she wasn't much mistaken, had felt betrayed, and very possibly emasculated by the revelation, having spent much of the previous year and a half attempting to woo Kara, only to find out he had been outdone by someone that he very clearly held in low regard.

    Of everyone, J'onn's reaction had been the hardest for Alex to bear. Though he hid it well, she could tell the news had rocked him to his core. As someone who had lost his wife, even centuries previously, he knew well what Kara, who loudly and proudly proclaimed him her space dad, must have felt. Though he didn't often acknowledge it, Alex knew he cared for both herself and Kara very deeply, and though Alex maintained that she had done the right thing, it still hurt to have caused him such pain.

    The meeting had ended not long after it had started, with J'onn announcing that there was to be no action on behalf of the DEO towards either Kara or Lena without his direct approval, and that the matter was to be considered closed.
