    With the sudden end to the meeting, Alex had taken Maggie up on her offer for a ride home, but was glad that Maggie seemed to understand she needed some time to process and had dropped her off before heading home herself.

    Truthfully, Alex wasn't sure what to feel, but she was starting to feel more and more like Clark was right. Kara had told her much the same things as she had told him. She had thought that she and Kara were family, and on Krypton family was supposed to be everything, but Alex clearly hadn't understood a word that she had said. That, or maybe Kara had just lied, just like she had for every day for years on end, every time Lena or the Luthors had come up in conversation, seemingly like she had every other time she had ever opened her mouth.

    No, Alex wouldn't be getting any sleep tonight. She made her way to the kitchen, skipping grabbing a glass and going straight for a bottle.

    At first, Kara was aware of only one thing. The sensation was growing, radiating from her core outwards, until it felt like she would combust. The heat grew and grew as her body consumed itself to feed the infernal blaze, and just as it had started to become too much, when Kara was sure that there would soon be nothing left of her but a blackened, charred husk, she became aware of something else.

    A presence.

    It flittered around her, above her, wiping at her forehead, slowly coaxing the heat from her body, bringing her back from the edge of immolation. But, when she cooled and blood once again flowed through her veins instead of fire, the presence stayed near, doing its best to ease the aches and pains that still plagued her body.

    The presence wasn't always above her, sometimes growing farther away, but she knew that when she needed it, it would be right back at her side. The thought brought comfort to her, and she smiled as the presence ran its hand through her damp hair.

    At some point, she knew not when, the presence had started to make a sound, a sort of repeated, rhythmic thumping. She latched onto the sound, letting it settle her in a way nothing else had quite managed to, especially when the presence moved further away from her and she could no longer hear the unintelligible, but no less soothing sounds that had started up not long after the thumping, the sounds that were replaced with a oddly sad gasping and whimpering sounds whenever the presence left.

    Eventually, the presence began filling her other senses. She breathed a deep lungful of it, drawing it in so deeply she could taste it on her tongue. She reveled at the friction as the presence ran its hands over her skin, at the slight tug when fingers combed through her hair, at the delectable press of lips to her head, her hands, to every part of her that wasn't covered. As she became more and more aware, she realized that it wasn't some random presence above her, but Lena.

    Her Lena.

    But, try as she might, she couldn't wake up, couldn’t open her eyes and take in the magnificence that was her wife, her body simply wasn't ready. Nonetheless, despite the spikes of pain that occasionally flared deep in her skull, Kara was content to bask in Lena's presence, enjoying as Lena spoke reassuringly to her, ran her hand through her hair, kissed her forehead, or any of the other dozen ways she showered Kara with love while she recovered.

    The longer this went on, however, the more Kara yearned for Lena. It pulled at her, a fiery beacon in the darkness that grew and grew until her weakened body could resist no longer. Slowly, her eyes blinked open, causing her to wince as light flooded her vision.

    “Kara,” Lena said, causing Kara to look over.

    Lena had dark bags under her eyes, she had clearly been crying often, and was doing so again. She looked rumpled, worn, and completely drained, and she had never looked more beautiful to Kara than she did in that moment.

    “Lena.”

    A long moment passed as Lena looked at Kara in amazement, then faster than Kara's sluggish mind could react, Lena was in her arms, holding her tight. Kara was more than glad to take the time to luxuriate in Lena's presence, but all too soon her wife got up, insisting on running Kara through a series of physical and psychological tests to determine how her body was recovering from the experience.

    An hour or so later, slightly more poked and prodded than she would have liked, Kara was nonetheless content with Lena's assessment that there were no signs of permanent repercussions. She still had a stabbing pain in her head every now and again, and her powers were still diminished compared to normal, but Lena assured her those would pass with time.

    Having completed assuring Kara's health was up to par, Lena promptly collapsed into her wife's arms, all the pent-up anxiety and emotions of the past weeks coming out all at once. Kara held her as she quietly sobbed, running her hand up and down her wife's back and cooing reassuringly while she let it all out.

    Kara waited patiently as Lena pulled herself back together. Lena had been all business when after she had awoken, focusing all her effort on her assessment, and as such had yet to provide Kara with any information as to how long she had been out and what had happened in the interim.

    "Three weeks,” Lena said as she pulled back, seeming to sense Kara's desire for answers.

    Kara took a moment to process that before she looked back at Lena, “Have... have you heard from Alex?”

    “She texted you a few times, but I didn't read them, but I...” Lena trailed off, looking down at her hands, before continuing softly, “I wanted to tell you how sorry I am about everything that happened, how much I regret that you were put in that position.”

    “Hey,” Kara said, reaching out for Lena's hands, “I don't blame you. If anything, I blame myself. It was my choice not to tell Alex. Whether or not keeping this from her was the right choice, it was me that made it, not you, nor anyone else, and I take responsibility for it. But we can talk about all that later, right now, I have something more important to deal with.”

    At Lena's questioning look, Kara reached out for her right wrist, thumbing lovingly over the metal there before tugging it down and off Lena's wrist. Holding it out to Lena, she bared her own wrist in turn, feeling more relieved than she could possibly express as the bracelet slid back onto her wrist.

    The two women looked down at it for a long moment before meeting each other's eyes.

    “I've worn it for so long, but I'm glad to see it on your wrist again,” Lena said with a beautiful smile.

    “Thank you for keeping it safe for me,” Kara said with a matching smile, “But I'm never taking it off again.”

    Chapter End Notes

    It is important to me that you all know that deposition is the opposite of sublimation. I don't know why it is so important that you know, but when I said "behind the wall of solid air that had been deposited into the room" I didn't choose that word at random, but because that is the physical process by which the wall was made.

    I don't know there is anything I love more than a good coma. Definitely my favorite negative health result (and there are so many fun things to do with them). Comas are the best (so are commas).

    So, for those who may be curious, my headcanon about Kryptonite is that it is in fact pieces of Krypton that are irradiated, but not just any random rock. Specifically, it was parts of the Nth Metal infrastructure on Krypton that was irradiated and turned into Kryptonite. That's one of the reasons that it is so threatening in the form of a physical weapon especially a bladed one over a projectile. If you asked me, before the kryptonite fully sapped Kara's powers, something made of common metal or stone powered by human strength doesn't have what it needs to hurt a Kryptonian, Nth metal though, very different story. Now, I will admit that I am not a physics (I lean more towards chem and bio, though I probably know more physics than the average layperson), so some of the finer points of material physics, metallurgy, and sharp force interactions may be illuding me, but oh well. Suffice it to say a Nth metal weapon is probably better than a non Nth metal weapon, but by how much I'm not 100% sure. Anyway, that's why Lena needed an Nth Metal and Lead weave to stop it. Nth metal to counter the Nth metal the Kryptonite is made of and Lead to block the radiation component. There is a lot more to this than just that, but I haven't the time or character limit to get into it here.

    Some of you may be interested in why I chose for there to be not only serious repercussions to Kara's boost, but also limit it's uses. If you are interested in world building with magic, I would direct you to look into Brandon Sanderson's laws of magic, and more of the things he has said about magic systems. My main concern with this ability of Karas was how to challenge her afterwards (I have no real plan to write a sequel to this story, as I think the ending is perfect already, so this is more an exercise in well thought out world building than anything that affects the plot). If Kara could just go Super Saiyan ultra with no downsides or limits, plot seems kinda pointless after that. There is something to be said about power scaling and different ways to challenge the protagonists than with a who can punch harder contest, and that is certainly valid, but also Kara got not only more physically power, but her brain got an equal boost. Plus, setting a limitation on the number of times this can be done makes this an even more significant act (this concept will be explored a bit next chapter) on Kara's part. Essentially, it was not really needed for me set that limit, but it served the story, and I consider it a good worldbuilding practice.

    You know I had to give Kara the black and silver recovery suit. It's a classic. It's not the only reference to the comics in the chapter though (not that I read the comics, I just read some stuff on some wiki pages or saw tiktoks, don't @ me).

    I know that scene with Lois might not have been what you all were expecting, but I love it, and I think it is true to the characters in my head.

    I can not express to you all how loudly I cackle with glee every time I read that line about it being valentines day. It's just so rude of me to do to the characters and I love it

    So, I'm taking a vote. Raise your hand if you are not surprised by Alex's continued stubbornness (though she has found something new to be upset about, so there is that)

    As always, if there is anything I didn't explain well enough or you wish for further clarification from me (or if you are just curious and wany me to expand on my writing/headcanons) just let me know and I would be glad to do so in the comments (or on tumblr if that is easier, link is at the top).

    Chapter 12

    Chapter Notes

    Well well well. Once again here I am, posting a chapter much later in the day than I had intended. In my defense, it's not my fault. No one turned my brain on this morning, so I kinda just skidded through the first few hours of the morning without getting anything done.

    I don't think I have too much to say about this chapter before we get started (Ignore this is there is a bunch more text before the chapter starts). It is probably my favorite after chapter 10. I will say there may be some scenes in 13 I like more, but there is some parts of 13 that sort of just have to be there (just wrapping some stuff up), so overall I like chapter 12 more. As many of you have been looking forward to, I present, two sisters yelling at each other! There isn't a ton of actual yelling (and I think Kal is the only one who actually shouts/yells), but this chapter is pretty much 5k words of mostly argument, a bit of exposition, and as always, a healthy helping of headcanons. This is the emotional confrontation to chapter 10's physical confrontation. Kal is also here, but more focus is on Alex then on him, he's going to be the easier of the two to convince.

    This scene is pretty much secondary only to chapter 10 in terms of amount of thought and importance that went into it, so a lot of everything that came before has also been building to this chapter. And, as many of you have commented, Kara is going to still have her backbone in this chapter, and Alex is going to have to work to overcome the ideas that have been building for so long in her head and also to earn Kara's forgiveness. Here we go. Kara has no mercy for them this chapter

    Alex looked warily up at the door in front of her. She and Clark stood side-by-side in one of the more upscale apartment complexes in the city, having both been summoned by a rather cryptic message from Kara earlier that morning telling them to be here at this time. It had been just over a month since last Alex had seen her sister, and neither she nor Clark knew what to expect on the other side of the door. With that in mind, Clark had chosen to wear his supersuit to the meeting. It was likely that Lena already knew, if she and Kara were married, but he figured better safe than sorry.

    As the door cracked open and she began to take in the enormous room, she immediately knew where she was. This was Kara's home. All the oddness she had felt from Kara's apartment had been because the warmth Kara imbued into the place had moved here, along with many of Kara's favorite possessions. It didn't escape Alex's comprehension that Kara had gone out of her way to replace the items with replicas. Another deception to add to the ever-growing list.

    Finally, Alex's eyes settled on her sister, sitting next to Lena on the farther of the two large L-shaped couches filling the living space. Kara was wearing another new suit, this one nearly identical in appearance to the one she had been wearing at the DEO, except for instead of the blue, red, and gold motif, it was all black with silver accents. Alex saw, with no minor amount of alarm, that the silver in this suit was shimmering reminiscently of the way the gold had been when she'd attacked the DEO.

    As for Kara herself, she looked much more herself than when Alex had last seen her. She was a little wan, her hair didn't have quite the same glow to it that it normally did, and she looked like she could do with a large meal, but there was no hint of the vengeful creature that had attacked the DEO a month previously, just her sister looking back at her, if a bit displeased.

    “Welcome to our home,” Kara said, standing somewhat stiffly and gesturing them toward the other end of the couch she and Lena had been sitting on.

    “Can I get you anything to drink?” Lena asked, also standing.

    “I'll take a scotch,” Alex said after Kal had shaken his head.

    “It's barely 10 in the morning, Alex.”

    The chastisement from her sister almost made her feel as if everything was normal between them, as if there wasn't a huge gap where before had been the bond of their sisterhood, but the enormity of the situation was too much for Alex to believe that for a moment.

    “I think I'm going to need it before this is done,” Alex said in answer.

    With a conciliatory nod from Kara, Lena left briefly, coming back and setting the glass on the coffee table before returning to sit next to Kara. She watched as Alex threw back the drink, her eyes flaring in appreciation momentarily before fixing on Kara's bracelet, eyeing the blazing gold metal with no small amount of recognition. Kara, noticing the attention, ran a finger over the bracelet and Lena saw as both Alex and Kal's eyes widened in amazement as the bracelet seemed to ripple and disappear beneath her suit's sleeve.

    The sight seemed to sober the other woman, and she put the empty glass back down on the table and leveled a look at her sister.

    “I was hoping you and I could speak,” she said, “Alone.”

    “Anything you have to say to me you can say to my wife.”

    “Kara-”

    “No Alex, This isn't going away, so you can either live with it, or you can get out.”

    “Seriously, Kara? You're going to kick me out for suggesting-”

    “This is Lena's home. I won't have you disrespecting my wife in her own home.”

    Lena sat back, content to let the conversation volley back and forth between the two sisters for now, Kal seemingly taking a similar stance as he simmered by Alex's side. A moment of quiet had fallen after Kara's declaration, Alex staring at Kara angrily and Kara refusing to back down.

    “Fine,” Alex said after a long moment.

    “Good,” Kara said, “Now apologize.”

    “What?!” Alex asked outragedly.

    “You can apologize to Lena for disrespecting her, or you can leave. Your choice.”

    “When did we lose you to her, Kara?” Alex demanded after a long moment staring at Kara's deadly serious expression.

    “The day you decided I had to choose,” Kara replied without hesitation, “now apologize or get out.”

    Alex looked in disbelief at Kara, but when she didn't budge, she turned to look at Lena.

    “I'm sorry,” she said halfheartedly.

    Kara opened her mouth to say something, no doubt to demand that Alex apologize sincerely, but Lena reached out and laid a hand on Kara's arm, causing her wife to drop it. While the very Luthor part of her reveled in the idea of making Alex grovel for forgiveness, it was neither helpful nor necessary.

    Lena looked back to Alex to find the other woman watching her shrewdly.

    “Apology accepted,” she said looking back to Kara, “Now I suggest we get on with it.”

    “Very well,” Kara said, turning to look back toward Alex and Kal, “I suppose you have a lot of questions, and we are willing to answer them all, but I warn you both to be careful how you speak of Lena.”

    “First and foremost, how did this happen? When did it happen?” Alex asked, gesturing between them.

    And so, Kara and Lena explained, taking turns explaining how their relationship had begun and how they had gotten to this point. They didn't include all the minor details, mostly sticking to the big picture, but already Kara could tell that doubts were seeping into Alex’s and Kal's minds. It seemed despite knowing what Lena meant to her, they still couldn't trust her.

    When Lena had finished the last of the story, Kara looked back and forth between them, feeling resigned as she saw the disbelief in their eyes.

    “This won't work if you refuse to believe a word out of Lena's mouth,” Kara said firmly, reaching over to grab one of Lena's hands as she did so.

    “How can you expect us to?” Alex asked, “After everything her family has done.”

    “I've told you time and again Alex, she is nothing like her family.”

    Kara had no expectation that this argument, the same that they'd had many times over, would sway her sister, and the growing anger on Alex's face was a clear indication that she was right.

    “You act like this is so simple, Kara, like we should just accept your word for it, well how can I when you've been lying to us for years? I'm going to need a lot more than your word Kara, she's a Luth-”

    At that moment, Kara looked at Alex sharply, her eyes glimmering with heat as they snapped onto Alex's, causing her sister to cut off abruptly and bring a hand protectively over her own throat, as if she could still feel the phantom of Kara's hand squeezing.

    “Yes, I lied,” Kara said, her voice low and threatening, “And if you don't trust me anymore, I can live with that, but that was my decision, and I will not let you hold that against her as well. If you need a reason to trust her, then fine. I'll give you one.”

    Kara looked over and met Lena's eyes and gave her a brief nod, watching as Lena left wordlessly towards their bedroom while she, Alex, and Kal waited in a tense, angry silence. Alex was too furious, even if it was well deserved, to be able to see clearly. She had hoped that Lois might've talked some sense into Kal once everything was out in the open, but it seemed that he too would need more convincing.

    As she heard Lena returning to her side, she slipped off the couch so she was kneeling before where Lena was settling back down. This wasn't really something that she had ever intended when she had given it to Lena, but it seemed like there may be no other way to convince them at this point.

    Alex and Kal were looking trepidatiously at the sheathed knife in Lena's hands, their concern growing slightly as Lena took up her position behind Kara. She watched as the worry turned to shock as Lena unsheathed the Kryptonite blade and held it to her throat.

    Kal and Alex surged to their feet as if to intervene, but Kara held up a hand, stopping them both in their tracks.

    “I gave Lena this knife,” Kara began, gesturing to the blade Lena was now pressing firmly into her throat as she spoke, “on the night of our wedding as a demonstration that I trust her completely, with everything that I have and everything that I am. With my life, just as I trust both of you. She could have killed me at any point over the last three years, but she never has, because she never would.”

    Kara looked at them both for a long moment, watching their expressions shift before she kept speaking, “You both have no idea how everything that Lex did affected her, how horribly he treated his own sister, how much day in and day out Lena has struggled with the burden of everything that he had done. Do you have any idea how many times I've had to console her for something that he did?

    “I am sick and tired of you judging her for other's actions, Alex,” Kara said, turning her focus mostly onto her sister, ”She has never done anything wrong, and yet you proclaim her guilty at every available opportunity for things that are beyond her control, that she never had any say in. She has tried so hard to make amends, she has done so much good, not only for you and the DEO, but for the world as a whole, and the last thing she deserves is for you to throw the worst experiences of her life back in her face like that. I'm done with it. I had better never hear either of you use 'she's a Luthor' as a justification for anything, ever again. I mean it.”

    Kara felt Lena pull the knife away and re-sheathed it as she kept glaring at the two of them. Making her way unsteadily back to her feet with Lena's help, she reseated herself on the couch as Kal and Alex seemed to process all they had heard.

    “How can you expect me to accept this after everything her family did to mine, after her brother nearly killed Lois?” Kal said after a few long moments.

    Kara leveled him with a hard look. She should be grateful the conversation had turned towards accepting Lena over not trusting her, but the way with which he still spoke of Lena like she was still part of Lex's ensemble didn't sit well with her.

    “Why should that matter?” She asked sharply, “She's never held anything between you and Lex against me. As a matter of fact, I distinctly remember her apologizing to me several times because of what Lex did to you.”

    “Why should it matter?” Kal asked disbelievingly, his voice raising until he was shouting “He's killed thousands of people, tried to kill me dozens of times, and nearly killed my wife. After everything that he's done, you ask me WHY SHOULD IT MATTER? He deserves to rot in prison for the rest of his life.”

    “Well here's a harsh reality check for you, Kal-El,” Kara said harshly, angling herself between her cousin and wife, “Lex isn't in prison, because you killed him.”

    The words rippled across Kal's features, but Kara pressed on before he could respond, “You lost control, and you killed him, and you stand here and judge Lena for someone else's crimes when she's already forgave you yours. When she covered them up, for you. Do not dare to sit there and condescend to me about what she's done, when you are the reason her brother is no longer in this world, when her own mother tried to kill her for covering it up. When she is the reason that your pristine reputation remains untarnished.

    “Yes, she did it in part for me, but she did it just as much for you, so you can keep helping the world without worrying that people are going to lose their faith in you, that people are going to start looking at you like you are a monster. You should be thanking her, not condemning her.”

    Kal didn't speak immediately, and as the seconds ticked by, Kara was tempted to lay into him again. Just before she opened her mouth to do just that, he finally spoke, not to her, but to Lena.

    “I'm sorry,” he said, sounding sincere and nothing like the angry, ranting man that he had been a moment before, “Thank you.”

    “Thank you,” Lena replied, “And I'm sorry for not doing more to stop Lex, for taking so long to realize what he was, and for the animosity I had for you. For the longest time, I couldn't fathom what he was becoming, and I blamed you for causing my kind, loving brother to change into this hateful, spiteful monster. It took me longer than I'm proud of to realize that part of him had always been there, deep down, and that sooner or later it would've made its way to the surface regardless. If it reassures you at all, Kara accidentally let slip who you were before your final confrontation. I had every chance to tell Lex, but I never did, and I never would have.”

    Kal had the audacity to look offended at her when Lena said she had let slip his secret, but Kara just raised an eyebrow at him, mentioned James' name, and he had quickly conceded the point.

    “It's hard,” Kal said after a moment, “It's so hard to forget what he's done. He nearly took Lois from me.”

    “I know,” Kara replied, “I know. I never told you both where I went when Lex turned the sun red. I figured that since everyone was busy, I'd go see my wife to comfort her through another one of her brother's schemes. When I got to her apartment, I found the door bashed open, broken glass all over the floor. He took her, and he hurt her, and there was nothing I could do about it. If he hadn't been so focused on you, Kal, he probably would have killed her.

    “If you hadn't killed him, I very well might have for what he put Lena through that day, and every day before it. So trust me, I know how you feel. You feel the need to protect Lois, just like I do Lena, I just need you to understand that Lena is one of Lex's victims, just like Lois.”

    “I think I can do that,” he agreed, “That's if I survive Lois's wrath when she finds out about this. I wasn't happy when I found out that she had known all along, but I'm glad you had someone. This can't have been easy for you.”

    Kara smiled at Kal, receiving one in return, but she knew that the battle wasn't won, not yet. Convincing the angry, protective Kryptonian had probably been easier than convincing her sister.

    Kara looked back at her sister, but before she could say anything, Alex beat her to it, “I was just trying to protect you.”

    Kara stared at her sister, momentarily unsure of how to respond to that, before finally settling on, “You arrested my wife...”

    “Maybe I wouldn't have, if you'd ever told me she was your wife,” Alex retorted angrily.

    “Do you seriously expect me to believe you would've been okay with that?”

    “Honestly, Kara, I don't know,” Alex said, rubbing her hand over her forehead, “But what I do know is that you kept this secret from me, for years, and I'm not okay with that.”

    Kara looked intently at her sister. She knew Alex was still mad, and she did have some valid complaints, but she herself was also mad at Alex. What she had done couldn't be changed, but she would do her best to repair what damage she could and accept whatever consequences there were for her actions. She only wished Alex could be a little less stubborn about it.

    “You have every right to be upset with me, Alex,” Kara started, doing her best to keep her tone level, “but you have kept plenty from me, like the fact that you were a DEO agent, or the secret anti-Kryptonian cell in the DEO-”

    “That was in case you ever got infected with red kryptonite again,” Alex interjected.

    “Right,” Kara said, closing her eyes for a moment before continuing, “You had good reasons for your secrets, and I forgive you for them. Can you at least do me the courtesy of letting me tell you why I kept this a secret before you judge me too harshly for it?”

    Alex didn't say anything, but instead gave a terse nod.

    “On Krypton, things were different,” Kara started, “Over the eons, everything became so constricted. We were alive, but many of us didn't truly live. This was true of so many things, but nowhere more so than our emotions. Our traditions were still alive, but they, and we, were pale reflections of what used to be, the true meanings of what it meant to be Kryptonian shunned by stigma and taboo.

    “Ritual had become an almost tedious upholding of tradition, religion was followed, but belief was laughed at, relationships became practically business transactions, dictated by a computer. My parents loved me, but they didn't love me. I don't know if they are even capable of such a thing. Somewhere, deep in my heart, I longed for it, I yearned to be free of those constraints, to truly feel. I dreamt of finding a love, a true love, something my people had tried to bury in their past.

    “When Krypton died, and I ended up on Earth, everything was different, but everything was also the same. I was still constrained, albeit in a different way. I know it was for my and my family's safety, and I could live with that, but that didn't mean it didn't still chafe. Then, one day, I realized what I was feeling for Lena. That secret, hidden thing I had always held so tightly in my heart.

    “All at once, for the first time in my life, I could choose something for me. Not to make my parents proud, not to keep my family happy with me, but just. For. Me. The one thing I had always wanted, more than anything else. If you, or anyone else had known, there was no way you would have allowed it. I couldn't, I wouldn't let you, or anyone else take it from me, not for anything.”

    Alex looked at her for a long, long moment when she finished speaking, her face inscrutable, before she finally replied, “This is a lot Kara, and it might take me a while to understand it all, but you have to know this isn't just about the secret. You attacked the DEO, you disappeared for a month, you nearly killed me and you hurt our friends. Even if you hadn't been lying to all of us for years, that would be a lot.”

    “I understand that, and if you all don't feel the same about me as you used to, I can accept that, but you aren't guilt-free in this scenario either, Alex. You, and to a lesser extent Kal, are the reason I kept this secret. If I had thought for even a moment that you wouldn't overreact, I would have told you. I wanted to tell you. I wanted to be able to share the best thing in my life with my sister, but when it came to Lena, you were overprotective, and paranoid, and you refused to take my word that she was nothing like you painted her.

    “I am sorry for what happened at the DEO. I didn't want that, but I did what I felt I needed to do. Whether it could have been avoided if I had told you sooner and born the consequences, I don't know, but I will live with my actions, because I was doing what I thought best for me and my wife, and I would do so again.”

    “I don't know if that's good enough Kara,” Alex said heatedly, “You very well could have killed someone.”

    “I have lived my entire life on earth in fear that I would lose control of my strength and hurt or kill someone, so trust me when I say no one at the DEO was ever at risk. I was in perfect control the entire time, both physically and emotionally. I could have taken Maggie, like you feared. It would have been fair, all things considered, or I could have torn the building down to the roots until I found Lena, or a hundred other things, but I didn't. I knew exactly where and how hard to hit to incapacitate everyone without any lasting damage. I did what I thought I needed to do to get my point across, and nothing more.”

    Alex, at the very least, appeared slightly ashamed of assuming Kara would take target Maggie to get back at her.

    “I'd like to apologize as well,” Lena said to Alex before either sister could say anything more, “I never intended to get between you and Kara. I let Kara do what she thought best in regards to your family, but the very last thing I ever wanted was to make Kara have to choose.”

    Alex took another few moments to ponder this, but eventually, she seemed to come to a decision.

    “Honestly, I don't know how to feel about this. For the time being, I think it's best we move on. So, on the topic of the DEO, you were much stronger than normal, stronger than I've ever seen you. What the hell did you do, exactly?”

    “My bruised ego and I would like to know as well,” Kal chimed in good-naturedly.

    Kara gestured to Lena, who at once settled into her element and began to explain. She started with the nanotechnology, demonstrating how it could be reshaped, smiling as Alex and Kal watched in amazement as the shell formed and melted into nothing and the ring crawled up and down Lena's arm. She'd also had Kara demonstrate, showing how she had incorporated the nanotech into the very fabric of the suit, allowing the bracelet to easily melt through the black material so it rested underneath the sleeve, or be proudly displayed above.

    Alex had been less than pleased about the revelation that they had specifically designed the bracelets to be hidden, yet another facet of the multilayered lie that Kara had been telling for years that she would have to sort through. Still, she had been remorseful that Kara had been unable to wear hers all this time, knowing how much that would impact her sister.

    “I've seen this metal in the archives,” Kal said, looking fascinatedly as Lena's bracelet glowed softly before gesturing to his own plain gold bracelet “But I could never figure out how to recreate it.”

    “I'd be glad to show you, if you wanted,” Kara offered.

    “I'd love that.”

    Alex watched the interaction between the cousins with a sort of wistful joy, before turning to Lena, “I don't understand, what does this have to do with how you charged Kara up?”

    “When I was studying Kara's cells, I noticed that as they absorbed light, they became more efficient at absorbing light, allowing them to absorb even more, therefore becoming more efficient, and so on until they contained an extraordinary amount of energy.”

    “And you used the nanotech to bombard Kara's cells with an extremely concentrated amount of photons, thereby overcoming the issue of surface area,” Alex said, completing the thought.

    “Would that work for me?” Kal asked.

    “I don't see why not, and I'd gladly make you a suit if you'd like, but I must warn you, the drawbacks are nearly as extreme as the upsides. Kara was in a coma for three weeks after she used it, and she is still suffering side effects. Even with the special recovery suit I designed to modulate her solar intake to ease the burden from the overload, it was an extremely unpleasant experience for her,” Lena paused as Alex and Kal both looked at Kara, who nodded affirmatively, continuing once she again had their attention, “Furthermore, it is a one time only solution. The first overload is bad enough, but the second, at best, would likely blow out your ability to process sunlight at all, and at worst could very well be fatal.”

    “So you are telling me,” Alex said slowly, leveling an incredulous look at her sister, “that this was a one-time thing, and you didn't save it for a time when the planet was in jeopardy?”

    “What do you not understand about this, Alex?” Kara snapped at her sister, all of the rage she still felt for what her sister had done blasting to the surface, “This is not some worthless farce of a marriage like they had on Krypton, or some trifling human thing to be ended on a whim. This, she, means everything to me. This is what I have always craved, since before I even knew what it was. This is who I am, freed from the shackles of my people.

    “Kara,” Alex said, emotions flowing rapidly over her face, one giving way to the next before anything settled concretely into her features, but Kara didn't give her the opportunity to say anything more.

    “No, Alex, don't you understand? There is nothing I wouldn't do for her. I would watch Krypton burn a thousand times, I would let Krypton burn a thousand times, just to keep her safe.”

    Alex stared at her sister, completely shocked by the outburst. She had always known that Kara loved big, but this... this was a whole new level, something she couldn't even begin to comprehend. Kara looked nearly rabid, and Alex had no idea how to help her sister. The realization struck her painfully in her chest, making it hard for her to even breathe.

    Luckily, it seemed, Lena did, for she called out in a language Alex hadn't heard since Kara was newly arrived on Earth, one that had slipped easily from her mind as Kara became more accustomed to English, yet flowed from Lena's tongue as if she had spoken it every day of her entire life.

    From the wonder, and the shame, on Clark's face as they saw the immediate effect it had on Kara, she knew he was thinking the same thing. Neither of them had spent nearly enough effort on this for Kara's sake. But Lena? Lena had.

    Alex watched as Lena pillowed Kara's head on her chest, directly over her heart, the sight triggering a memory of something Kara had told her years ago, when she had been startled by a sudden loud noise and how she had focused on Alex's heartbeat to help center herself. Alex hadn't really thought much of it at the time, but now it occurred to her, and the very realization made her feel sick.

    She knew, with unerring certainty, that Kara listened for Lena's heartbeat, not just when she was stressed or overstimulated and needed the reassurance or distraction, but always, at every single moment throughout the day, and even at night as she slept, constantly alert for any minuscule sign that her wife was in danger.

    And Alex had locked her in a soundproof cell.

    It explained perfectly how Kara had known every time over the past year when Lena had been in danger, quickly and with more certainty than she had any right to otherwise. Kara had been desperate, she had been terrified, and she had been broken, all because she couldn't hear Lena, because of her.

    “Her heartbeat,” Kal murmured next to her, coming to the same revelation as their eyes met, “I always hate it when I can't hear Lois.”

    Alex and Kal talked amongst themselves for the next few minutes, doing their best to allow Kara and Lena as much privacy as they could have while within five feet, but Alex could hear the low, reassuring murmur that Lena was whispering to Kara, and even if she couldn't make out the words, the care and love was unmistakable.

    “Do you know what it sounds like to lose your home, Alex?”

    The question caused Alex to look up, meeting her sister's fiery gaze.

    “It sounds like nothing,” Kara continued, not waiting for Alex's response, “My planet, my people, my family, my home, burned in front of me, and I heard nothing, not a whisper. That silence has haunted every second of every moment of the thirteen years I have been on earth. It echoes endlessly in my head, drowning out even the loudest sounds. There is only one thing on this earth that has ever driven away from me; the sound of my new home, and you took her, and you locked her in a cage, and you left me in silence again.”

    Alex felt her mouth flop open and closed a few times, but nothing came out, especially as she saw Kara's face harden and her eyes blaze with fury, until she was looking into the same face she had when she'd pinned Alex by her throat to the wall in the DEO.

    “You will never touch her again,” Kara commanded, speaking with the same lethal calm and untapped rage as she had when she'd come for Lena, “and if you or the DEO ever dares try, I will raze it to the ground.”

    Alex nodded mutely as Kara finished speaking. Looking into her sister's blazing eyes, feeling her anger and rage press against her skin, Alex knew with certainty that she meant every word of her threat.

    “So, I don't suppose you want me to draw up the normal NDAs,” Alex said after a few moments spent trying to gather herself, whether to deflect from the tension that had built in the room, or distract from the fear that was dripping down her spine, she wasn't quite sure.

    It had been a rather weak attempt at a joke, but thankfully no one seemed inclined to mock her for it. Lena had even been gracious enough to chuckle lightly.

    “No,” Kara replied, “It's her secret as much as mine. If she wants or needs to tell someone, I’ll understand.”

    Alex had figured as much, but despite the obviousness of her ploy, it had succeeded at breaking the tension in the room, and Kara was no longer giving her the death stare, and for that, Alex was immeasurably grateful.

    “Mom is gonna freak when you tell her,” Alex said, attempting to capitalize on her momentum and swing the conversation back into more friendly territory.

    “She already knows,” Kara said, at least having the good grace to look apologetic at Alex's irked expression, “I made her swear she wouldn't tell.”

    At this point, honestly, Alex was over it. She understood why Kara had done what she did, and while reconciling everything that had happened between her and her sister might take a long while, she knew that at some point, they'd get there.

    Sensing that they were done, Alex and Kal stood and turned towards the door, but they both turned back as Kara began speaking again.

    “One last thing before you go. Kryptonian marriages, even the pale imitations of what they became, are immutable. This,” She said, gesturing between herself and Lena, “isn't going anywhere, and if you want a place in my life, you will have to accept it.”

    Nodding, Alex turned towards the door. She might need some time to understand and accept their relationship, and everything that had happened, but she knew she would be able to accept it, eventually. And while she was far from warmed up to Lena, she couldn't deny the love the woman had for Kara, and Alex knew that eventually, she would be able to accept her too.

    Chapter End Notes

    This chapter, maybe more than any other, do I truly love the dialogue of (though I probably could have done better in some of the speech tags). There are so many little exchanges that I thought up that I had to fit and so many topics they had to flow through, and if you ignore some of Alex's less than subtle transition(s), I am very happy with how it turned out (Honestly, I am much more talking about some of the exchanges and some of the speeches, much more so than the transitions).

    For those who may be curious, the Kryptonite knife wasn't particularly included in this fic to come up in this chapter (which is sort of reflected in Kara's internal monologue). The original idea was just that Kara gave it to Lena to show her trust, and idea I had before the conception of this fic, and when I decided that it fit really well and wanted to included it, I didn't realize just how well it would fit into not only the earlier chapters, but this chapter as well. Truthfully, there are so many things in this fic that came together so much better than I could have planned, I almost don't feel like I could take credit. I didn't really do it, it sort of just happened to me.

    So, if anyone wanted to point out that there really isn't any safe way to render anyone unconscious by blunt force, and there will almost always be some sort of negative repercussion, that's fair. I will always attempt to strive for realism where I can, but at the end of the day, the fic does come first (Plus, that scene was too cool not to include). Anyhow, for those who may be like me who don't like things that are unrealistic, I hope also like me you can be rest assured that this did not come by way of lack of knowledge or as a mistake, but was a purposeful choice made for the sake of the plot, and all the other things that are just incorrect or impossible too are choices and not mistakes (as far as I know, if there are mistakes I wouldn't know because I would have fixed them otherwise). There is also something to be said about suspension of disbelief, and certain aspects that just don't make sense or are just outside of inexplicable by any sort of reason or physics, but I don't have enough characters to get into that here.

    Anyhow! Alex is finally getting there, but she still has some stuff to work through (and a few people may still have some choice words for her), but she's officially started down the right road. It's kinda crazy to think that I'm posting the second to last chapter already, and that it's been over three months since I started posting, and most crazy that the final chapter is going to be up in just a week. I wish I could say that I've been spending this whole time working on my next fic, but honestly there's been so much going on in my life that I haven't made nearly as much progress as I would have liked. Well, I still have next weeks chapter notes to talk about what's next, so I'll come back to this chapter for now.

    I hope you liked this one, like I said, this is my second favorite overall, and it's been almost as long coming as chapter 10. It feels like finally, the majority of the threads that I've been stringing this whole time are winding together. There are a few more to come, but everything will be concluded by next chapter. Anywho, as always, feel free to comment thoughts/impressions. Let me know your favorite line, or whether you think Kara was too hard/not hard enough, or anything else, and I'll see you all in a week for the finale.

    Chapter 13

    Chapter Notes

    Well, at long last (No, I actually did not name my fic that just so I could make this pun). the end is here. As many of you can probably imagine, this is an incredibly bittersweet moment for me. This fic has been such a big part of my life and occupied so much of my attention for so long, I almost don't know what to do with myself now that I am posting the last chapter. I don't think that I will ever come back to write a sequel or anything for this story, because I think the way it ends is perfect, but also because I have so many other things I want to write. I still have a lot to say about this fic and everything that I put into it, but for the moment I think I am too emotional for that (I am still writing this before I do my final read, so it will probably only get worse).

    After Alex and Kal had left, Kara had decided that it was time for her to return to her Supergirl duties, even if she hadn’t yet fully recovered. At Lena's insistence, Kara had agreed to be extra careful, not to get into any fights, and not let people shoot her, 'for dramatic effect,' as Lena put it, when she could easily disarm them before they got off a shot.

    Donning the brand-new suit that Lena had presented to her a few days previously, this one made entirely from Lena’s kryptonite-resistant nanoweave and fully integrated with Kryptonian nanotechnology, she made her way out onto the balcony. Feeling resplendent as the suit encapsulated her body, she took off into the skies above National City.

    Later that evening, Alex had texted her letting her know that the president was coming to town. Officially, the story was that she was coming to the city to check on the relief and rebuilding efforts in the wake of the havoc the EMP effect Kara had generated unleashed on the city's electrical infrastructure, but the real reason was that Supergirl had been absent the first time she had visited, the day following the event, but now Kara had made it known she was once again active.

    So, two mornings later, Kara found herself landing on the balcony of the DEO, the first time she had been back since she had rescued Lena. As she walked in, she noticed that all the damage that she had caused had been repaired, the whole room looking like nothing had ever happened. There were more than a few agents scattered throughout, a few of them still even sporting minor bruises or scrapes, and they had all looked at her as she entered. Some with fear, some with shame, or anger, or trepidation, but she kept her head high as she made her way to the conference room. She would not apologize to them, nor would she be made to regret her actions.

    When she entered the conference room, she found not only the president waiting for her, but none other than Lucy Lane at her side. As much as she would love to have been able to catch up with her friend, that wasn't the point of this meeting. If she was lucky, however, they might have time to catch up later.

    “Supergirl, good to see you again,” President Marsdin said, standing from where she had been sitting at the head of the table.

    “Madam President,” Kara said, nodding in greeting.

    “I understand you know my legal attaché, Major Lane,” she said, gesturing to Lucy.

    “I do, Madam President.”

    “Very well, let's get started then,” President Marsdin said, returning to her seat and gesturing Kara to one further down the table, “There is a lot about last month's events that need to be discussed. Your attack on this facility, and the antecedent and subsequent earthquake and electrical phenomenon you caused did a lot of damage to not only government assets, but also to this city and much of the land surrounding it.

    “Luckily, no one was seriously injured, but this cannot be allowed to stand. As of now, my administration has yet to comment on ‘The Day the Sun Fell to Earth’ as the media has coined it, beyond reassuring the city and its people that they have our full support while rebuilding, but the people of this city are scared, and they need to know it won't happen again. For now, all they know is that Supergirl managed to dispel whatever it was that caused the event, and was injured in doing so. No one outside the DEO knows that you were the cause, and I'd like it to stay that way, but if that is to be the case I need certain assurances from you.”

    As Kara listened to the president speak, she reached down for her left wrist, calling her bracelet to the surface of her suit before activating it, watching as the ring slid down and fastened around her finger. She pulled it off, sliding it on her other ring finger and watching idly as the ring slid up her hand and into her suit, making its way back to the correct finger on her left hand. She repeated the process a few times, until the president had finished speaking, and the silence caused her to look up to see the older woman looking at her expectantly.

    “Am I boring you, Supergirl?” She asked.

    “My wife made this for me,” Kara replied, indicating the nanotech in the ring and suit, “I taught her how, but I'll be damned if she didn't take it and soar with it. Isn't it amazing?”

    Kara watched as the president blinked once, the only sign of surprise she would express at Kara's revelation.

    “They didn't include that in the report, did they? Well, allow me to fill in the rest of the gaps for you. The DEO illegally arrested and detained a US citizen, my wife, held her in violation of her rights, did not allow her access to a lawyer, and when I asked to see her, I was denied without just cause and was provided no means to verify that she was alive and well.”

    “You can't mean to imply the government would-”

    “Let me be perfectly clear,” Kara said, cutting off the president's denial, “Lena is under my protection, and there is nothing I will not do to ensure her safety. You want assurances, here they are. If Lena is left alone, I will return to my normal duties and forget about this whole thing. However, if the DEO or any other agency of the government does come after Lena without valid reason, I assure you, I will not hesitate to do whatever is required to keep her from harm.”

    Kara met President Marsdin's eyes steadily, unwilling to back down. The silence stretched on for a long moment before Lucy broke it.

    “Ma'am, Supergirl is correct that the DEO's actions were illegal, and while her actions were also questionable, I believe that should the government attempt to pursue legal or legislative action against her, and the truth of everything came out, the public would side with Supergirl.”

    “Very well,” President Marsdin said at last, “You have my word, Mrs. Luthor will be safe, but there is still the matter of your attack on this facility. There must be repercussions.”

    “Can you give us a moment alone, Lucy,” Kara asked, her eyes never leaving the president's.

    Lucy looked to the president, waiting until she nodded before standing and making her way out of the room.

    “I know you have a job to do, a responsibility to the people and agencies under your command, and to the people of the United States,” Kara said, looking back at the other woman, “I respect that, and I respect you. I voted for you. Twice. But I don't work for you, nor for the DEO. The DEO and I have done good together, and I would like that to continue.

    “I know you are a smart woman. You wouldn't be where you are now if you weren't, so I'm sure you've figured out who I am, and why this all played out the way it did. What happened is unfortunate, but I do not regret my actions, nor will I apologize for them, to you or anyone else. I did what I had to do for my wife, and I would do it again, without hesitation. You do what you must, but I'd ask that you take that, and what I might know, under consideration before making any decisions that will affect my family.”

    Kara didn't wait for the president to respond before she stood and left the room.

    Kara opened the door of her old apartment, revealing Alex and Maggie on the other side.

    “Come in,” Kara said, stepping aside and letting them in, “Food should be arriving soon, and everyone else is on their way.”

    Things were definitely still awkward between her and her sister. They hadn't seen each other in the few days since Alex had come over to hash things out, and Kara wasn't sure exactly where they stood. Part of her hadn't wanted to host game night, not with everything that had happened, at least not until things started to normalize, but Lena had encouraged her, and so she had agreed.

    Alex and Maggie stalled as they made their way in, both laying eyes on Lena at the same moment, neither seeming to know what to do or say to her.

    “Scotch?” Lena asked, graciously breaking the awkward moment up.

    Alex and Maggie both nodded, seeming to lurch back into movement towards the couch as Lena stood to go fetch them drinks. Soon, everyone else had started to pile in. Winn and James arrived together, followed closely by Brainy and J'onn. Mon-El arrived a few minutes later, and much to everyone's delight, Lucy strutted in, having gotten a few days off to spend in the city.

    When he had arrived, J'onn had sought Kara out, pulling her aside in the kitchen.

    “I wanted you to know how sorry I am for the role the DEO played in what happened.” J'onn said, his voice low and somber, “If I had known, I never would have let your wife be taken from you.”

    Kara smiled sadly at him. He spoke with the tone of one who knew what it was to lose those you hold dearest in your heart. But she could tell that he was also upset about the fact that she hadn't told him. She knew he loved her almost as his own daughters, and learning that she had kept such a secret from him had saddened him greatly.

    “Thank you, J'onn. And please know that I didn't want to keep this from you. I made the decision not to tell anyone, because that was what I thought best for me and Lena.”

    “Thank you, Kara,” he said, looking in slightly better spirits, “I understand that, and I appreciate you telling me.”

    Watching J'onn make his way back towards the couches, Kara's eyes caught on Lena, and she felt her smile involuntarily widen. Lena was talking animatedly, Brainy and Winn looking completely invested as she described some new invention she had been working on at L-Corp.

    Her smile faltered uncertainly as she watched Mon-El approach her where she still stood in the kitchen.

    “I wanted to apologize too,” he said, “If I had known you and Lena were bonded, I never would have acted the way I did.”

    “I appreciate that Mon-El,” Kara said truthfully, “But next time you set your sights on someone, learn when to let it go if they aren't interested.”

    He opened his mouth to reply, but he never got the chance before Lena's voice called out across the room, “Kara, darling, could you come here please?”

    Kara did her best to hide her smirk as she made her way to her wife's side. Lena had been less than subtle. If the timing and meaning of her words hadn't been obvious enough, the sheer possessiveness in her voice had surely done the trick.

    Settling down at Lena's side, Kara smiled as Lena pulled her into a less-than-appropriate kiss, lest someone misinterpret the claim she had just staked.

    “You've been wanting to do that since last time, haven't you?” Kara accused teasingly against Lena's lips as she pulled away.

    “Maybe,” Lena answered coyly.

    Most of their friends had been polite, or smart, enough to let the display pass without comment, but Kara had heard James scoff as they kissed.

    “Something the matter, James?” She asked sweetly, taking in his sour expression.

    “You know what, Kara,” he replied, the muscle in his jaw tensing.

    “Say it,” Kara said, the tone of her causing the whole room to fall silent.

    Not a single soul in the room dared move as Kara stared down at James. She could feel her power starting to flare, pulsing into the room, and she was not at all inclined to suppress it. James knew that he was treading into dangerous water, but he didn't seem to have the sense to stop.

    “I don't know how you can love her.”

    Before Kara could snap at him, or do anything of the other things flying through her mind, she felt Lena's hand land on her arm, steadying her, calming her.

    “Mr. Olsen,” Lena said, using the same tone Kara had heard her apply to make grown men fold and cry on conference calls, “You don't have to like me, but I suggest you get used to me. I'm not going anywhere, so you can accept that, or you can get out.”

    James's eyes flared in outrage, looking around the room for someone to support him, but found nothing. Huffing, he stood and made his way to the door, leaving an awkward silence behind him.

    “So,” Winn said after a few moments, “I heard you got some new digs, Kara. Was the suit I made for you not good enough?”

    “Sorry Winn,” Kara replied, glad for the teasing edge in his voice, “Your craftsmanship is shotty at best compared to Lena's.”

    Winn put on a look of mock outrage as she spoke, though if it was due to the comment or pun, Kara wasn‘t sure, but it quickly turned to awe as he watched the nanotech unspool at Kara's command, quickly spreading across her body and replacing her clothes with her new and improved suit. Just as fast as it had came, the suit retreated once again, leaving Kara back to her normal clothes.

    “Damn,” Winn said, anything else beyond him at the moment.

    “If you think that's cool, you should see their penthouse,” Lucy chimed in mischievously.

    “What?! Why aren't we there then,” Winn asked, deeply intrigued at the promising lead Lucy had given him.

    “Because,” Kara said, leveling a sidelong glance at Lucy as she got an innocent shrug in return, “As James kindly demonstrated, this situation probably needs some time to settle first. That is our home, and I won't have anyone disrespect my wife in her own home.”

    Winn nodded understandingly, but Kara couldn't help notice how Alex had averted her attention at her statement. She knew her sister was still struggling to come to terms with everything that had happened and with her relationship with Lena, but she hoped that it wouldn't take her too long.

    “You took Lucy, but you won't take me?? You know how nice I am.” Winn fake pouted at her, drawing her attention back to him.

    “You should've just watched Kara tell off the president, then told her it was badass. That's what I did.”

    Winn gaped at her for a moment, before immediately going back to fake pouting.

    “Fine,” She relented, “Next time I host, we can go if everything else goes okay. That okay with you Lena?”

    When Lena acquiesced, Winn erupted with a cheer, pumping his fist extravagantly in the air.

    The rest of the night passed without any fuss. Alex had been somewhat subdued, but with Maggie's coaxing, she seemed to be getting over it, bit by bit. And while it was still awkward, Kara was immeasurably glad that she no longer had to hide her affection for Lena. If she wanted to touch her, she could. If she wanted to kiss her, she could, and that was worth any amount of awkwardness to Kara.

    Over the next two months, Kara and Lena had begun working with the DEO with a singular goal in mind. Locking Lillian up for good. The only way to do that was to dismantle CADMUS, and so they had gotten to work.

    Kara watched on one day, paying little attention to anything besides her wife typing away at a DEO computer, until Lena had suddenly stopped, looking around until her eyes met Kara's.

    “I did it, I know where she's going, all we need to do is make sure we get there first.”

    Alex approached, hearing Lena's comment, but Kara could tell that a part of her still hesitated to accept Lena's help. She shot her sister a look, and Alex seemed to think for a moment before nodding slightly, sending a flood of relief through Kara's chest.

    Over the next few days, they'd laid their trap. It hadn't been easy. They'd needed to be discrete about it. If Lillian had felt the noose closing around her neck, she would have slipped away before it could tighten. Lena had been instrumental in the process. She'd exploited Lillian's use of her business infrastructure to track CADMUS cells throughout the country, until finally, they'd settled on a plan they could all agree on that would put an end to CADMUS once and for all.

    The following day, Lena sat waiting in the shadows of a large crate in the middle of the warehouse, the same run-down, overlooked sorts of places her mother usually skulked around in. On the other side of the building, she heard the door open, and she couldn't help but smirk to herself as she heard her mother's heels making their way to where she lay in wait.

    Lillian walked around the corner, nearly walking straight past her before she spoke from the shadows.

    “Hello, Mother,” Lena said casually, “I must say. This place isn't up to your usual standards.”

    “Lena,” Lillian said primly, ignoring the dig and belaying no surprise to see her, “Have you finally come to your senses, or is your Kryptonian pet hanging around somewhere... Kara, was it?”

    “It's just you and me,” Lena replied evenly, doing her best to keep her face impassive, even hidden as it was.

    “Really Lena, sullying yourself with a Kryptonian. The Luthors are your family, we love you, and you'd turn your back on us for that beast.”

    “That beast,” Lena said, “Has taught me more about love and family than a cold, heartless person like you could ever understand.”

    As she spoke, Lena moved forward slightly from where she had been lounging, setting her hands on her knees and leaning forward. The action caused her sleeve to pull up slightly on her left arm, revealing the warm, metallic bracelet concealed beneath. Lena watched with satisfaction as Lillian's eyes flared with outrage as they fixed on it, then dropped to the gold band on her finger, adorned with rubies and sapphires, perfectly matching the blue suit with red accents she had donned specifically for the occasion.

    “She is my family,” Lena said as Lillian practically seethed with rage at the fact that she was wearing Kara's house colors, “More than you or Lex ever have been.”

    “So be it,” Lillian said hatefully, pulling out her pistol and leveling it at Lena.

    Faster than Lillian could blink, Kara was there, standing between the two women.

    “Well, this is an interesting way for us to spend our three-year anniversary, Lena. Normal people go out for dinner,” Kara said, completely ignoring Lillian to look back at her wife.

    “Well, you know me,” Lena replied warmly, “I never did like to do things the normal way.”

    “And here I thought I wouldn't get the pleasure of your company,” Lillian sneered, glaring hatefully at Kara, “Or maybe I should let Metallo and Hank have another go with you, they have been so looking forward to their revenge.”

    “I'm afraid Metallo and Henshaw won't be joining us,” Kara said, faking a contrite look, “Should I have mentioned that sooner?”

    Lillian's nostrils flared, but otherwise, her face remained impassive. Without warning, her finger squeezed on the trigger, three shots ringing out in quick succession, the bullets tore out of the gun, glowing sickeningly green as they briefly illuminated the space before they slammed into Kara.

    Three heads looked down as the three kryptonite bullets fell harmlessly to the floor.

    Sticking her head out from where she was standing behind Kara, Lena looked at Lillian, “Really mother, did you think I would let my wife walk around with a fatal weakness? You're slipping. Orchestrating all this really was too easy.”

    Lillian's eyes almost glowed with murderous intent as she tried to aim at Lena's head, but before she could so much as twitch, Kara was there, slamming her into one of the crates in the warehouse, the bones in her arm screaming in pain as Kara pinned the gun away from Lena. Leaning in so they were face to face, Kara let her eyes glow as Lillian struggled fruitlessly to escape her iron grip.

    “You will never hurt her again,” Kara said, the deadly calm in her voice causing Lillian to still as her face paled, “And if you even try it, it will be the last thing you ever do.”

    A few minutes later, as Kara roughly tucked Lillian into a DEO van, she felt Lena come up beside her.

    “Did you really have to let her shoot you,” Lena asked as she tucked her arms around Kara's middle.

    “You said the suit was Kryptonite resistant, and you were right, I barely felt it,” Kara replied.

    “That doesn't mean you should have let her shoot you, what if I was wrong?”

    “You,” Kara said, turning to cup her wife's face, “Are never wrong. And anyway, did you see the look on her face? Totally worth it.”

    “I did,” Lena admitted with a smile, “but next time someone is pointing a gun at you, for my sake, could you please just disarm them before they shoot you?”

    “For you,” Kara said, leaning down and kissing Lena, “Anything.”

    Kara walked into Alex's apartment, smiling in greeting as her sister and Maggie looked up at her.

    “You ready to go,” she asked Alex.

    “Yeah,” her sister replied, “Let me just grab my bag.”

    “Are you sure you can't come, Maggie?” Kara asked while they waited for Alex to return, “I could always come back and fly you out.”

    “I'd love to, little Danvers,” Maggie said, “but I just picked up a case. Can't get away.”

    “Shame. I'll give Eliza your best.”

    At that moment, Alex walked back in, her weekend bag slung over her shoulder, “Bye Maggie, love you. Let's get out of here.”

    With one final kiss for her girlfriend, they left, making their way to the roof so Kara could fly them to Midvale to spend Mother's Day weekend with Eliza. The flight went quickly and relatively silently, given Alex's inability to hear anything Kara said with the wind rushing past her ears.

    “I forget how cold that can be,” Alex said, springing from Kara's arms and rushing for the warm interior of their childhood home.

    Alex walked in the back door, smiling as she saw the fire in the grate, the heat driving off the cold from the flight and the chill that still permeated Midvale, even in May. Her smile widened as she saw Eliza, but she nearly stuttered when she saw the person sipping from a steaming tea cup next to her mother.

    “Lena,” She said, trying to keep her voice as friendly as she could, “I wasn't expecting to see you here.”

    “I flew her in before I came for you,” Kara explained as she stepped into the room.

    Alex's feelings when it came to her sister's wife were still ... complex. Logically, she knew that Lena wasn't like she had perceived her to be, but she was still struggling to accept it, and everything else that came with their relationship. The lies, the arrest, their feelings, and her own all jumbled into a huge mess that Alex still didn't know what to do with.

    In the meantime, she had mostly been avoiding Lena, especially when Kara was present. Until she sorted everything she was feeling out, that seemed the safest bet. Now, however, it seemed she had little choice but to interact with Lena. She would do her best. She hadn't missed the radiant joy that came across her sister's face every time she saw Lena, something now so obvious she didn't know how she had missed it before she knew the truth, and the least she could do was be civil while she sorted out the complicated mess in her head.

    Alex offered Lena a tentative smile and a nod, and she saw relief bloom on the other woman's face as she returned the gesture.

    As the day progressed, Alex mostly stayed passive, much as she had most other times Lena had been around in the months since everything had happened. Truly, she wasn't sure how she fit into any space that Lena also occupied. Part of her still expected the other woman to harbor some resentment towards her for the way she had acted, and the other part just... didn't know what to say.

    Until she could figure that out, Alex stayed on the outskirts of the conversation. Responding when spoken to, but for the most part just watching her sister and Lena. Still, it was a marvel to watch them interact. She had never seen Kara so happy, so content, just from Lena's mere presence.

    She watched with amazement as Lena played idly with Kara's fingers while talking to Eliza. While Kara had never resisted Alex physically, at least not before what had happened at the DEO, she had certainly never allowed Alex to just move her like that. Alex had the oddest feeling that whatever Lena wanted to do, Kara would just let her. She was pretty sure that if Lena pushed her, Kara would just fall over, however ridiculous the thought was.

    But, as Alex thought about it, maybe it wasn't so ridiculous. She remembered what Kara had said; to her, Lena was everything. If Lena woke up one day and decided to plunge the kryptonite knife into Kara's heart, Alex had no doubt that Kara would do absolutely nothing to stop her.

    The thought terrified her, but in a way, it also reassured her, because just as much as Kara loved Lena, Alex could tell Lena loved Kara the same. After everything she had seen, and everything she had heard, she knew that Lena would never, ever, let any harm come to her sister. That, more than anything, started to warm Alex to the idea of Lena in Kara's life.

    Later, after dinner and once night had started to sweep in to replace day and the sun had started to set, Kara and Lena had excused themselves and made their way out onto the beach behind the house. A few minutes later, Alex had followed them, for what reason she didn't quite know, but she hesitated as she stepped out onto the back porch. Down on the beach, about halfway to the water, she could see Lena and her sister.

    They were just ... standing there. Their arms were clasped between them, just above the wrist, and Kara's other hand was on Lena's neck, tilting her head so she could lean down and press their foreheads together, but besides that, they didn't seem to be doing or saying anything. They just stood there with their eyes closed, simply enjoying being together.

    Even without being told, Alex had the feeling that this was something deeply significant to them both, confirmed moments later when her mother stepped up right beside her, her eyes also trained on the couple, “I married them, right on that spot, just over three years ago.”

    Alex watched her sister and Lena for a few minutes longer before she turned to where her mother was patiently waiting.

    “There is so much to unpack here, I don't even know what to think anymore. Kara lied, and she attacked the DEO, and for a moment there, I thought she was actually going to kill me. I know why she did it, but it's still a lot. And Lena, I still don't know how to act around her, or even what I could possibly say to her after everything I said, and everything I did.”

    “You did arrest her wife,” Her mother said, her voice full of the patented parental disappointment before warming to something more akin to teasing, “You know how protective she gets, but she never would have hurt you, Alex. You are her sister, and she loves you. You two will figure this out. As for Lena, an apology might be a good place to start”

    “I don't know if saying sorry even covers it, and at the same time, I don't know if I am sorry. Everything I did, I did with the sole intention of keeping Kara safe. I regret what I did, but not the reason I did it, and I won't apologize for it.”

    “Tell me this honestly Alex. If your sister and Lena had come clean to you six months previously, or a year, or two, or when they got married, when they got together, how do you think you would have reacted?”

    “Kara asked me something similar a few months ago, and I... I still don't know,” Alex replied truthfully.

    “Well I know, and it would not have been pretty. You have always been misguided and biased towards Lena, but that isn't your fault, it's mine and your father's,” Alex tried to interject, but Eliza held up a hand to stop her before continuing, “Ever since Kara came to stay with us, I hounded you to always look out for her, to take responsibility for her, to guide her and keep her safe. At the time, you were just a teenager, and I put the responsibility of an adult on your shoulders. That burden wasn't yours to bear, but I gave it to you anyway.

    “Your actions, while well-intentioned, are a result of you taking the mindset that I forced on you too far. It blinded you when it came to Lena, and you were biased and even hateful towards her without cause. It made you irrational in pursuit of something that was never your responsibility, and I apologize for starting you down that path. I think of Lena like a daughter, and my only hope is that my actions haven't irreparably harmed your relationships with Lena, or with your sister.”

    With that, Eliza went inside, leaving Alex to look out over the now darkened horizon, her sister and Lena still standing unmoving in the same spot. Alex pondered all that her mother had said, but in the end, she found she couldn't agree with all of it. Yes, she had planted the seeds, but Alex was the one who had let them grow into the twisted shapes that they had.

    She was right about one thing though, while well-intentioned, her actions had been just as fueled by her blind hatred of Lena as they had been about protecting Kara. The latter, while part of her decision, had also been a smokescreen in her mind that had prevented her from seeing just how much her actions toward Lena had been motivated by that burning, unjustified loathing.

    She still had a lot to work through and process, but she was on the right path. Of that, she was confident.

    Lena sat at her desk, staring at her laptop without reading anything on it. It had been a few days since they had returned from Eliza's, but her mind was fixated on the visit. More specifically, her wife's sister. She had thought, after the first day they'd spent there that the other woman had actually started to warm up a little to her.

    She didn't want to get ahead of herself though, she knew that Alex still had a lot to work through and think about, and she still had a few issues herself to air out, but hopefully, eventually they could work it out, for Kara's sake.

    As they so often did, her thoughts seemed to summon the Kryptonian to her, but Kara had landed behind her a touch harder than she normally did. Turning towards her wife rather than wait, Lena immediately knew something was wrong, Kara looked worried. Very worried.

    “Zhao, what is it?” Lena asked, standing and quickly moving to meet Kara at the balcony door.

    Before Kara could respond, Jess had opened the door, looking down at her tablet as she made her way towards her desk.

    “Your first meeting isn't for 20 more minutes Miss Lu-” Jess had started, looking up when she got closer and abruptly cutting off as she saw her boss was not at her desk, but in fact over by the balcony with none other than Supergirl.

    “I'm sorry Miss Luthor, I didn't realize you had company, would you like me to push back your meeting?”

    “Yes,” Lena said, then added on second thought, “Actually, please cancel all my meetings for today and reschedule them for some other time this week. When you are done with that, you can go home.”

    Nodding, Jess quickly left to get started. Turning back to Kara, Lena waited for an explanation, which Kara immediately provided.

    “Someone figured out who I am. They kidnapped Alex and demanded I release a prisoner or they'll kill her.”

    “What can I do?”

    After that, Kara had whisked Lena to the DEO, where she had been granted access to a workstation and, with Brainy's help had started to track the kidnappers. Maggie, With J'onn's help, had left to figure out who the prisoner the kidnapper had demanded be released was and how he was connected the kidnapper, which hopefully in turn would reveal both the kidnapper's identity and how they had known Kara's secret.

    Somehow, Alex had managed to rig a beacon to help them locate her, and Lena had almost let Kara leave when she spotted that the signal was only being routed through that location. He was smart, she'd give him that, but she was better.

    It took her another hour, Kara impatiently pacing the whole time, but knowing better than to interrupt Lena’s work, before eventually, she managed to pin down exactly where the signal had originated, and sent Kara off at once. While she was gone, Maggie had return with the information about the kidnapper. If Lena was wrong about the location, then that information might be vital to Alex's safe return.

    However, until Kara reported back, there was little to do but wait. She and Maggie passed the time chatting amongst themselves. No one at the DEO seemed to have much interest in talking to her, especially as they worked towards locating and apprehending the kidnapper, but that didn't bother her, not when Maggie had affectionately referred to her as 'little Luthor', the same as she often would to Kara.

    Alex was pissed. Somehow, she had let her guard down and been captured by some dweeb she had gone to high school with. She had managed to use the camera in her cell to boost the signal of her DEO-issued subdermal transponder, but now, there was nothing to do but wait.
