    She had been in the cell for five or six hours by her best guess, and more than an hour had passed since she rigged the beacon, before Kara had flown suddenly through the ceiling. Her entrance seemed to have set off some sort of trap, because immediately water had started pouring into her cell, but with a few sharp blows, Kara had destroyed the cell she was in and she had been free.

    She was soaked from the waist down, definitely still pissed, but overall no worse for wear. Kara flew her back to the DEO and deposited her in the locker room so she could towel off and put on a dry uniform. Once she had finished changing, she made her way up to the command center. She had barely opened the door before Maggie had barreled into her arms.

    Catching the shorter woman, albeit barely, she was pulled into a fiery kiss that served very well to warm her up before looking around the room at the other's worried faces. She saw Lena off to one side, talking to Kara, and for the first time, the sight didn't jar her.

    After receiving a warm smile and a firm pat on the back from J'onn, Alex made her way over to where Brainy was sitting at his computer.

    “I assume I have you to thank for locating me,” She said, smiling down at the Coluan.

    “Actually,” Brainy replied, swiveling towards her and steepling his fingers together, “It was mostly Lena Luthor that managed to find your location. I, of course, helped, but it is her you should be thanking.”

    Nodding to herself, she made her way over to the secluded corner of the room she had seen Kara and Lena in earlier. She had been meaning to have a conversation with Lena anyway, and it seemed like now was as good a time as any.

    When she found them, they were standing forehead to forehead, much as they had been a few days previously on the beach, a ‘thank you’ falling from Kara's lips. She cleared her throat, garnering both their attentions.

    “Lena,” She said, “I was hoping I could have a word.”

    Kara looked to Lena, who nodded, before walking off, leveling a stern, but also relieved look at Alex as she did so. Lena was looking at her expectantly, so Alex gestured for her to follow before leading off to one of the nearby training rooms where they could have a little more privacy.

    She closed the door behind Lena once she had stepped in after her and turned to see the other woman still giving her that same expectant look.

    “I just wanted to say,” Alex started, wiping her hands on her jeans to rid them of the sweat that had suddenly sprung up, “Thank you for saving my life, and I am sorry for arresting you, and for everything I did and said before that. You didn't deserve any of it.”

    Lena didn't say anything, she just kept steadily looking at her, so Alex continued speaking, “In my head, I had conflated you with your brother, and by extension danger for Kara. I won't apologize for trying to protect her, but I am sorry that I used it as an excuse for treating you poorly and to lie to myself about what I was doing. That was wrong of me, and it was unfair to both you and Kara.”

    Lena looked at her a few moments longer, clearly still thinking, before she spoke, “It hurt, Alex. I won't lie. It hurt that my wife's sister was one of the people that thought the worst of me. I can forgive what you said, because you never said anything different than what I thought about myself before Kara showed me that the fault for Lex's actions didn't lie with me.

    “I didn't deserve how you treated me, but I know you did it out of a desire to protect your sister. That's a better reason than most, and in the grand scheme of things, you treated me no worse than everyone else in my life, everyone but one person: Kara. So let me ask you this, Alex? Do you think it is me that you need to apologize to? I didn't do much to earn your trust, nor did I do anything to deserve your scorn, but Kara? She deserved better from you.

    “She is your sister, and she would be there for you no matter what, and can you honestly say the same? You are the reason she kept this secret, you berated her for saving your life, you shot her out of the sky, with kryptonite. Your own sister. And I'm not saying this to be cruel, or to make you feel bad. I'm saying it because I know Kara never would,” Lena paused for a moment after finishing her tirade, taking a deep breath and looking into Alex’s eyes in such a way that caused the guilt already festering in her chest to squirm even more uncomfortably.

    “Do you want to know where she was when you called her after she caught your plane?” Lena asked after a few of the longest and most uncomfortable moments of Alex’s life, during which she had been completely unable to break eye contact with her sister’s wife, “She was in my apartment in Metropolis, because she was so excited to have finally become a hero that she had to fly across the country just to tell me, and you took that from her. You crushed her. Do you think I was happy that she was risking her life, that she was potentially putting herself in my brother's crosshairs? Of course I wasn't, but I supported her because this was what Kara wanted, and my support was what she needed. She deserved that from you, not to have her dreams dashed.

    “So yes, I can forgive you for what you did to me, because while it hurt, I never expected anything else from you, but I cannot forgive how you treated Kara. If you want me to accept your apology, promise me this. Do better.”

    “You're right,” Alex said after a few deep, steadying breaths, “Kara deserves an apology from me, and I need to be a better sister to her. You have my word.”

    “Then your apology is accepted," Lena said, sticking a hand out for Alex to shake.

    Alex looked down at it like it was a weapon, no doubt remembering Kara's earlier warning.

    “Don't worry,” Lena said, unable and unwilling to suppress the devilish smirk on her face, “She wasn't being literal. She wouldn't actually hurt you, unless you threatened me again.”

    Tentatively, and not looking all that comforted by Lena's reassurances, Alex reached out for Lena's hand.

    Faster than she could react, Lena's hand shot out, grabbing Alex's hand and pulling hard until she was face-to-face with the slightly taller woman, “But let me make one thing perfectly clear. If you ever use Kryptonite on my wife again, I will make what Kara did here look paltry in comparison to what I will do to you.”

    Without waiting for a reply, Lena turned and walked out, leaving a shocked, and more than slightly terrified Alex behind. With the deadly serious tone that Lena had just used, Alex wasn't so sure that Kara was the scary one in that relationship anymore.

    A few days after she had been rescued, Alex had texted Kara, asking if they could have a sister night and offering for Lena to attend too if she wanted. She had been talking a lot with Maggie about everything, and she thought she was ready to take the next step. A few minutes later, Kara texted back agreeing to host at their penthouse, but made no mention if Lena would be attending or not.

    That night, Alex made her way for the second ever time towards Kara's new home, this time with much less anger, about the same amount of trepidation, and a lot more food. Kara was waiting for her at the door, a result of the building's impressive security or her super hearing, Alex wasn't sure.

    She unloaded a few of the items she had brought, all Kara's favorites, not that she was trying to bribe Kara into a good mood or anything, before sitting on one of the couches. She took in a little bit more than she had the last time as she waited for Kara to get settled too, seeing a truly massive, Kryptonian feeding kitchen on the far side of the room. The thought that Lena had likely built that kitchen just for Kara sat warmly in her chest.

    As she was unpacking the food on the table in front of the couch, she saw on the nearby wall that Kara had hung the framed article that she had gifted her for Christmas. It bolstered her that Kara had found a place for it, even despite everything that had happened both before and after Alex had gifted it to her. It took her a moment, but she also noticed right next to it, in a very similar frame, hung another copy of the same article.

    Kara, having finished setting down her load, turned to where Alex was staring at the wall and noticed what had caught her sister's attention.

    “You and Lena got me the same present last Christmas. You two are more alike than you might realize.”

    “I'm starting to get that,” Alex replied, turning back to Kara with a smile, “Speaking of Lena, will she be joining us?”

    “She's out of town,” Kara said, clearly unhappy about it, “Last minute business trip.”

    “Okay,” Alex said, “Why don't we eat, then I think you and I need to have a talk.”

    Kara gladly agreed, and Alex was happy that her sister seemed to be acting more jovial towards her. Alex had no doubt that Lena had told her that she apologized, and that likely had a lot to do with it. Alex quickly ate her fill, glad to sit back and watch Kara demolish the rest, the pleasant feeling of Kara's company warming her, a pleasure she'd rarely had much of over the last year.

    When she had finished eating, Kara looked up at her expectantly, the expression so similar to the one Lena had when she'd taken her aside at the DEO. It was one of many mannerisms that Alex was picking up on that Kara had learned from Lena, and she wondered again how she hadn't noticed any of them earlier. That, however, was a matter for another day.

    “I just wanted to tell you how sorry I am,” Alex started without preamble, “I've talked with Mom, and then the other day with Lena, and they made me realize that I wasn't the sister to you that I should have been. I'm sorry that I arrested your wife, and I'm sorry about how mad I was when I found out the truth about you and Lena. I'm sorry that I shot you out of the sky, and that I was so harsh to you after you saved my life the first time, and most of all, I'm sorry that I wasn't a better sister to you, that I was someone you felt you couldn't tell about your wife.”

    Despite her promises to herself, Alex promptly burst into tears as soon as she was done. She threw herself into her sister, a fresh wave of tears falling from her eyes at just how good it felt when Kara's arms came up and wrapped themselves around her.

    “Thank you,” Kara said, her eyes just as wet as Alex's, “it means a lot that you say that, and I forgive you. You're my sister, of course I forgive you.”

    Alex laughed, then cried again, then laughed while crying, Kara doing the same.

    The sisters continued in this fashion for some time, before Kara eventually pulled back, wiping gently at her eyes, “Can I ask you something?”

    “Of course,” Alex replied.

    “What do you think I saw when I was in the Black Mercy?”

    The question shocked Alex, not only because it was unexpected, but because Kara had always flatly refused to speak of what she had experienced under the parasite's influence.

    “I- I always assumed you saw Krypton,” Alex said.

    “There‘s a time you might have been right, when that's what I would have seen, but that was before I met Lena,” Kara said, her tone almost wistful, “She helped me accept that Krypton was gone, but still with me. For years while I was in the phantom zone, then on earth, I struggled to even comprehend the death of Krypton, the loss that it caused me. There were times, even after I had been on earth for years that I would wake up and forget what had happened, despite the fact that I had watched the whole thing with my own eyes.

    “Some days in that moment just before I opened my eyes, I expect to see my bedroom, to be able to look out over the skyline of Argo and see the red skies beyond it, and every time I woke to see our bedroom in Midvale, or my apartment, every time the window showed me National City's skyline and skies of blue, I broke all over again. And while I love you, I truly do, and Eliza, and everyone else I had on earth, it couldn't plug the whole that the loss of Krypton had made.”

    Kara paused for a moment, looking out of the window with a far-off expression on her face, as if in that moment she was seeing the cityscape Krypton out past the glass, but it lasted for only a moment before she continued speaking, her attention returning to her sister as she did so, “Then I fell in love with Lena. She was the first one to even come close to filling the void within me. She learned Kryptonese, not to help me acclimate easier to earth, or from some sense of idle curiosity about where she had come from, but because she knew how much it meant to me, how much it gladdened me to hear it even while it tore the scars off my wounds. Little by little, piece by piece, even before I realized she was doing it, she filled that void, until one day there wasn't a void anymore, there was only Lena. The pain was still there, but it was no longer debilitating. I no longer woke in the wrong bedroom, or on the wrong planet, I woke up to her.”

    As Kara spoke, Alex felt an overwhelming sadness, but she was also glad. More than anything, she wished that she had been there for Kara like that, that she had done better to make sure that she was okay, but she was more thankful than she would ever likely be able to express that when Kara had needed her, Lena had been there.

    “She has your mother's necklace, doesn't she?” Alex asked, already confident she knew the answer.

    “She does,” Kara answered, “And I made her the same promise that my father made to my mother when he gave it to her, that I would protect her, that I would find my way back to her no matter what. When she had needed me, the black mercy's hold on me was nothing in comparison.

    “And your other necklace?”

    “Lena gave that to me the night of our wedding, the same time I gave her the knife. She knew how much I hated that I wouldn't be able to wear my bracelet or my ring, so she gave me something that I could wear every day.”

    At the mention of her ring, Alex's eyes flicked down at her finger. With everything that had happened, the jewelry hadn't been on the top of her mind, but as soon as she laid her eyes on it, she knew exactly what it was. Somehow, Lena had managed to give Kara a piece of her home back, and for that, Alex appreciated her even more.

    “What I want, more than anything,” Kara said, bringing Alex's attention back to her, “Is for my family to accept my wife. I didn't need the black mercy to tell me that.”

    The rest of the night was filled with much lighter topics, Kara and Alex joking back and forth, ribbing each other, just like they had before everything had gone down. For the first time in a long time, Alex felt like she had her sister back, and it was liberating.

    When she had been getting ready to leave, Kara had gotten up too, but she hadn't headed off to her room, like Alex might've expected, but walked to the balcony door, her suit already materializing to replace her casual wear. She didn't need to be told to know what she was doing. As she closed the door behind her, she imagined she could hear her sister take off, flying to wherever Lena happened to be in the world at that moment.

    Once again, it was time for game night, and as promised, she and Lena were hosting at their penthouse. When everyone arrived, Lena had led them all on a tour. Just like Kara, everyone had been amazed by the first floor, and Lena had taken a deep, almost carnal pleasure in how hard Winn geeked out at the hidden staircase and massive gaming space beneath it.

    Winn had bounced around the room like a child who had eaten too much sugar, commenting on every minute and complementary feature in the room, and Kara had the distinct impression that if they weren't careful, he was going to take up permanent residence on one of the couches.

    In comparison, everyone else had been subdued, but clearly, they all thought highly of the space. Between that, Lena's over-excessive stocks of food, snacks, and drinks on Kara's behalf, and her access to very high-end liquor, Kara knew with certainty that this was now the go-to hang-out spot for their friends, just as Lena had envisioned when she designed it.

    Brainy had been next to geek out, although his manner of expressing excitement was much more reserved than Winn's. When he had laid eyes on Lena's work area, he had immediately gone off to inspect some of the inventions Lena had been tinkering with, attempting to deduce their intentions and suggesting improvements. Winn had followed not far behind, but to be fair, he was rather easy to excite.

    Even J'onn had expressed his admiration for their security features, and that was as close as he would get to admitting he liked it. They all made their way back to the second floor, Alex and Maggie heading off the the kitchenette to raid the liquor cabinet, making everyone a drink.

    They all settled around on the couches, talking and joking, and Kara couldn't be more happy. After a few minutes, their security alerted them that Kal and Lois were on their way up. Kara got up to let them in, loath to disrupt the camaraderie Lena was settling into with the group. Kara greeted both Kal and Lois with a big hug, giving them the same tour that everyone else had just gotten.

    “Kal-El, go make nice,” Lois said, stern but loving, as they returned to the couches where everyone else was lounging.

    Kara did her best to hide her smirk, but Lois did no such thing as she turned to Kara, “Sorry about him. He means well, but he's a bit slow sometimes.”

    “I know how he can be,” Kara said with a laugh, “You should have seen how difficult he was when I had to change his diapers.”

    “I can't believe you've never told me that before. We are so talking about this later,” Across the room, where Kal had just finished giving Lena a somewhat awkward hug, his head snapped toward them, but Lois shot him a look, and he quickly turned back towards the rest of the guests, “Now, correct me if I'm wrong, but that bed looked rather sturdy.”

    Lois waggled her brows as she said it, causing Kara to laugh before she replied, “It is, specially reinforced with a Kryptonian in mind. I think Lena got tired of getting new frames.”

    “You animal,” Lois said with an uproarious laugh, “I might just have to get one myself.”

    “I'm sure Lena could hook you up,” Kara replied with a smile.

    Rao she'd missed Lois. With that, Lois made her way towards Lena, hugging her much more warmly than Kal had. It had taken Lena by surprise, if the wide-eyed look she'd sent Kara had been any indication, but it quickly melted into pure contentment.

    “Sorry about my bonehead husband,” Lois said loudly, looking at Kal as if daring him to object.

    Wisely, he kept his mouth shut as Lois went on, “I'm glad you two found each other when you did. I think you both needed it.”

    “You knew the whole time?” Lena inquired.

    “Of course I did,” Lois replied, but not unkindly, looking up as Kara slid next to Lena on the loveseat, “You don't get to be an award-winning journalist without being able to tell when someone is lying to you. She didn't fool me for a second, although it was definitely a good effort.”

    Lena looked up at her, and she shrugged helplessly. She had told Lena she wasn't very good at lying. It was only with her guidance that she had managed to fool Kal at all.

    Lena looked back at Lois, a sort of 'what are we gonna do about her expression' on her face, and Kara slapped her arm lightly in mock outrage, firmly pouting at her wife.

    “Sorry darling,” Lena said, not sorry at all, “you know it's true.”

    “I'm a great liar,” Kara said, her pout intensifying, “Just ask Alex.”

    As soon as she said it, both she and Lena froze, looking over at Alex. Thankfully, Alex seemed to not take offense, smiling warmly back at them.

    “Sorry, Kara,” Her sister said, looking at Lena then back towards Kara, “I gotta agree with Lena on this one, I think I was just off my game for these last five years.”

    “Wow, betrayal, the both of you.”

    Kara kept the pout on her face, but truly she was happier than she had been in a very long time, Alex and Lena had teased her, together, that was a major step forward, and Kara was ecstatic to see it.

    Kara wrapped her arm around Lena, content to sit back and enjoy the ambiance of the night. She smiled as Lena asked Kal and Lois about their bracelets and Kal his new suit. He had seemed surprised at first when Lena had used his Kryptonian name, something she had picked up from Kara, but he quickly seemed to accept it. Lena's craftsmanship, of course, was immaculate, so there was nothing to complain about.

    Kara looked around, her friends and family surrounding her, laughing and joking, drinking and playing. Lena was tucked into her side, completely at home amongst everyone, her wife was laughing openly at something her sister had just said, and finally, at long last, everything was perfect.

    Chapter End Notes

    So, there it is. I am so glad to finally be able to share all this with you all. I want to thank everyone for reading and all the kind comments. I still have so much to say, so many things I still want to point out and talk about, and there is so much that I learned about my own fic as you all read it, but I don't think I can do that right now. For now, I think I have to go celebrate/mourn/both. This fic really has meant so much to me in a hard time in my life, and it's so hard to be done with it. I expect this fic will always be my favorite, and I will come back to it time and time again. I still want to express everything I have to say about it, and I will probably do that over the coming weeks on my Tumblr, as well as posting the notes that would normally go here. I will also post other thoughts and ideas there, updates about things to come, and more https://www.tumblr.com/settings/blog/admiraldeltafleet. So, goodbye for now, and I hope to see you there or in my next fic.
